## 🆕 `p619` — reads `2026-11-02` — market map and regional placement, **and the dedicated legs caught the digest channel drifting on two dated facts: a `57%` reported as ">60%", and a real EU instrument dated three months wrong**

🟡 **Secondary by construction** (`598-G`, seventeenth consecutive pass): no primary source resolves
from this environment. Every demand figure below is **search-snippet-grade**; every supply figure in
`agents/top.md` and `repos/foundations.md` is **byte-read**. The two are not the same evidence and are
not mixed.

### 🔴 `619-K` — two channel drifts, both caught by running a **dedicated leg** against the digest that reported them

This base has catalogued channels getting a **licence** wrong four ways (`616-C`, `617-E`, `618-E`,
`619-G`). 🔴 **The demand side drifts too, and nothing here was checking it.** Both of this pass's
regional digests were re-asked with a targeted leg, and both moved:

| Claim as the digest gave it | What the dedicated leg returns | Drift |
|---|---|---|
| 🔴 *"over `60%` of college students use AI weekly"*, attributed to an April `2026` Gallup study | 🟢 **`57%`** use AI for coursework **at least weekly**, ~`1 in 5` daily — **Lumina Foundation–Gallup `2026` State of Higher Education**, released **`2 April 2026`**, n ≈ **`3,800`** associate+bachelor students | 🔴 **Inflated**, and across the round number |
| 🟡 *"roughly half of their institutions officially discourage or prohibit"* | 🟢 **`42%` discourage**, **`11%` prohibited outright** (`53%` combined) | 🟢 Approximately right — 🔴 but it **collapsed two different policies** into one, and only one of them is a ban |
| 🔴 *"the Commission updated its ethical guidelines on AI in education (`9 June`)"* | 🔴 **There is no June `2026` update.** The revised edition is **March `2026`** — Commission guidance announced `5 March 2026`, launch coverage `17 March 2026`, listed by Cedefop as *2026 Guidelines on the ethical use of artificial intelligence and data in teaching and learning for educators* | 🔴 **Three months wrong about a real instrument** |

🔵 **The new shape, and it is the one this base was least defended against.** A wrong licence family is
a claim about a *payload* that a byte read settles. A wrong **date on a real instrument** survives
every control this base owns: the instrument exists, the issuing body is right, the subject is right,
and the only false token is the month. 🔴 **A citation that is right about everything except *when* is
indistinguishable from a correct one at a glance** — and a regulatory date is exactly the field a
client engagement would act on.

🟢 **Operative:** any dated instrument that enters this page from a digest gets **its own leg** before
it is written down. Two for two this pass; the cost is one search each.

### 🟢 `619-N` — LATAM's constraint finally has a number on the **money** side, and it is `4%`

`616`/`617` argued back and forth about whether LATAM's gap was supply, index or channel. 🟢 **This
pass it is budget, and the figure is specific.**

**UNU / UNESCO IESALC, *AI Implementation in Higher Education in Latin America and the Caribbean***,
working paper dated **`1 September 2026`**, author **Arianna Valentini** — a regional survey of
**`200` higher-education institutions across `19` countries**, fielded **August–October `2025`**,
mapping five dimensions (teaching and learning, research, community engagement, administration,
governance). Coverage in *Times Higher Education*, **`14 September 2026`**:

- 🟢 **`90%`** of universities using AI in teaching use general tools — `ChatGPT`, `Copilot`, `Gemini`.
- 🔴 **`55%`** of universities have **no specific AI budget**.
- 🔴 **Public** universities have the lowest rate of dedicated AI funding: **`4%`**.

🟢 **A peer-reviewed twin exists**, which is rare on this page: *Revista Educación Superior y Sociedad*
**`37(2)`**, published **`30 May 2026`**, *"AI Adoption and Governance in Latin American and Caribbean
Higher Education: Findings from a Regional Survey"*, **DOI `10.54674/ess.v37i2.1278`**.

🔴 **Do not conflate it with the other LATAM survey in the same channel.** A separate *"AI in Higher
Education LATAM Survey 2026"* reports **`92%`** of students using AI regularly and **`67%`** weekly.
🔵 Different instrument, different population (**students**, not institutions), and the two are
routinely quoted as one. **The `4%` is institutional funding; the `92%` is student behaviour. Quoting
them as one survey is how a `100`-point contradiction gets published.**

🟢 **Why `4%` is the most actionable figure on this page.** It converts LATAM from *"adoption gap"* to a
**procurement constraint**: in the public segment, the buyer has staff and demand and effectively no
line item. 🔵 That makes the permissive, **offline-capable** rows from `619-D`/`619-D2`
(`Anmol-Singh-Jaggi`'s classical-`ML` recognizer, `developerali123`'s `IndexedDB` `PWA`, `Kolibri` as
host) the only supply shape that matches the budget — and it makes a hosted-model dependency
(`sunnydev07/Open-Speech`'s `CEFR` rating) a disqualifier rather than a detail.

### 🟢 `619-M` — APAC returns **two** dated national instruments, and one gap written down

The national-query channel (`605`–`607`'s proven shape) paid again:

- 🟢 **Singapore — MOE.** Age-gated rollout: primary pupils use **selected** AI tools inside the
  **Singapore Student Learning Space** (`SLS`) under teacher supervision only; MOE is reviewing
  classroom impact and **delaying use for younger students**. A **new committee to drive AI use in the
  higher-education sector** was reported **`2 April 2026`**. 🟢 Singapore **chairs ASEAN education for
  `2026`–`2027`** and will run **`AIEd`** signature programmes under that chairmanship — which makes
  Singapore the regional **policy-setting** venue, not just a market.
- 🟢 **Philippines — DepEd.** **Department Order No. `003`, Series of `2026`** sanctions AI use in
  public schools and **requires students to disclose how they used it**. 🔵 A *disclosure* duty, not a
  ban — and it is the clearest dated instrument in the region this pass.
- 🔴 **Australia — GAP, written down.** The channel returns only an **undated** *"Framework for AI in
  Schools"*. No `2026` instrument confirmed. **This is recorded as a gap, not as coverage.**
- 🔴 **Unverified and not carried forward:** the claim that the Philippine DepEd *"certified more than
  one million teachers in Gemini AI"* with Google came from a newsletter digest and **did not
  reproduce** in the dedicated leg. 🔵 Given `619-K`, an unreproduced number from that same channel is
  not written down as fact.

### 🔴 `619-O` — the standing limits on this page, restated because they bound every figure above

🔴 **`598-G` stands, seventeenth pass:** no primary source resolves from this environment. Market
sizing on this page conflicts by multiples and is kept only as a **range with its sources named**,
never as a point estimate: AI-in-education `2026` figures in channel this pass span **`$3.7B`**
(Persistence) to **`$8.3B`** (unnamed firm) globally, with North America quoted at **`$3.68B` / `36%`
share** by one source and **`38%` share** by another, and Technavio's **`41.7%`** being a
*contribution-to-growth* figure that is **not** a share and must not be compared to the others.
🔵 **Naming that incomparability is the only honest thing this page can do with those numbers.**

## Opportunities by region

### North America

🟢 **The engagement is policy-compliance tooling, and `619-K` sized the buyer.** Lumina–Gallup
(`2 April 2026`, n ≈ `3,800`): **`57%`** of students use AI for coursework at least weekly, ~`1 in 5`
daily, while **`42%`** of institutions discourage it and **`11%`** prohibit it outright. 🔵 **The
`53%` restrictive bloc is the market**, not the permissive one: an institution that discourages or
bans AI still has `57%` weekly use and therefore needs **detection-free, disclosure-based** workflow,
which is the Philippine model (`619-M`) arriving as a product requirement in the US.

🟡 Regulatory surface remains **sub-national**: `134` bills across `31` states (carried from `p607`),
`4` states with a mandatory district-policy duty. 🔴 Education-specific federal rules: still none
confirmed in channel. Cross-border deployments must reconcile US state law with Canadian requirements.

🟢 **Where this pass's supply fits:** `LTI 1.3` external tooling (`619-M2`) against Canvas, which
dominates US higher education — the boundary that keeps a studio's work permissive beside an `AGPL`
incumbent. 🔵 The accessibility rows have a distinct US driver that is **statutory**, not pedagogical:
`IDEA`/`Section 504` obligations make a read-aloud assessment or a sign-language channel a
**compliance** purchase, which survives a budget freeze that a tutoring pilot does not.

🔴 **Gap:** the leg returned **no named vendors** for the compliance-tooling segment — only
conferences (`ISTELive`) and bodies (OECD, UNESCO). Competitive mapping for this segment is **not
done** and should not be presented as if it were.

### EMEA

🟢 **The instrument is dated and now correctly dated (`619-K`): March `2026`, not June.** The European
Commission's revised *Guidelines on the ethical use of artificial intelligence and data in teaching and
learning for educators* — announced `5 March 2026`, launch coverage `17 March 2026`, listed by Cedefop
— replaces the `2022` first edition, was prepared by the Working Group on the Ethical Use of AI and
Data in Education via the **European Digital Education Hub**, and responds explicitly to **AI Act
compliance**.

🟡 **The AI Act's education exposure needs its own leg before anyone quotes a date.** A blog in channel
asserts high-risk obligations apply to education from **`2 August 2026`**; that was **not verified** this
pass and is written here as **an unverified claim with its source characterised**, not as a date.
🔵 Education appears in the Act's high-risk annex, so the question is *which* obligations and *when* —
and `619-K` is the reason this is not being written down as settled.

🟢 **UK — funded and dated:** first **AI Adoption Summit**, `8 June`, committing **£200m+**, of which
**£100m** expands **Bridge AI** and **£53m** goes to regional initiatives. 🟢 **EU skills funding:**
three **Advanced Digital Skills** grant agreements worth **€25.4m**. 🔵 Both are *capability-building*
budgets — the buyer is a programme office, not a school.

🔵 **Sovereignty remains EMEA's differentiator** (carried trend): the offline/local-first rows from
`619-D2` are a better fit here than anywhere except LATAM, for a different reason — **data residency**
rather than **budget**.

🔴 **Gaps, stated:** the regional leg named **no EMEA edtech vendors, ministries or universities**;
enterprise AI-readiness figures (Workday's `56%` of EMEA CEOs) are **not education data** and are not
used as such. **Africa returned nothing confirmable in the education-specific leg this pass** — a
macro roundup mentions AI-for-education exploration among African startups and public agencies with no
dated instrument behind it. `607`'s Africa gap therefore **stands**.

### APAC

🟢 **The best-documented regional picture this pass, and the only one with two dated national
instruments** (`619-M`): Singapore MOE's age-gated `SLS` rollout with a `2 April 2026`
higher-education AI committee and the **ASEAN education chairmanship `2026`–`2027`** with `AIEd`
signature programmes; and the **Philippines DepEd Department Order No. `003`, s. `2026`** mandating
**student disclosure** of AI use.

🟢 **This pass places supply in APAC for the first time by *subject* rather than by holder.** Two of
`619-D2`'s rows target **Indian Sign Language** specifically — `Arshad221b/Sign-Language-Recognition`
(`Apache-2.0`, `265★`, `106` forks) and `imRishabhGupta/Indian-Sign-Language-Recognition` (`MIT`,
`221★`, `79` forks). 🔵 **The placement rule, stated because it is new and because its opposite is a
trap:** an artefact is regionally placed when **what it is built for** is regional — `ISL` is a
specific language of a specific region. It is **never** placed from a holder's personal name. The first
is evidence; the second is an inference this base will not make.

🟡 Adoption/regulation beyond those two instruments is thin and is left thin: the **ASEAN AI governance
guide is early-stage**, leaving per-country compliance; Forrester's *"`60%` of firms and governments in
India and China will integrate locally developed LLMs alongside global ones"* is a **`2025`
prediction**, not a measured `2026` figure, and is labelled as one.

🔴 **Gaps:** **Australia** returns only an undated framework — no `2026` instrument. **China, Japan,
Korea and India returned no new dated education instrument** in this pass's legs (`605`'s national
findings for China/Korea/Japan are carried, not refreshed). The *"one million Philippine teachers
certified in Gemini"* claim **did not reproduce** and is not carried.

### LATAM

🟢 **The region's constraint is now a budget line, not a mood** (`619-N`). UNU/UNESCO IESALC
(`1 September 2026`, Valentini; `200` institutions, `19` countries, fielded Aug–Oct `2025`;
peer-reviewed twin *Rev. Educ. Sup. y Soc.* `37(2)`, `30 May 2026`, DOI `10.54674/ess.v37i2.1278`):
**`55%`** of universities have **no specific AI budget**, **public** universities fund AI at **`4%`**,
and **`90%`** of those teaching with AI use `ChatGPT`/`Copilot`/`Gemini` — i.e. **general tools, not
education tools.**

🟢 **The opportunity that follows is specific and it is not "an AI tutor".** `90%` general-tool use
against `4%` public funding means the buyable thing is a **thin, permissive, offline-capable layer over
tools the institution already has**, costed against a zero line item. 🔵 The matching rows exist as of
this pass: `Anmol-Singh-Jaggi`'s no-accelerator classical-`ML` recognizer, `developerali123`'s
`IndexedDB`-only `PWA`, `valueforvalue/OpenPhonograms`' `248`-lesson corpus, hosted on **Kolibri**
(`MIT`) where there is no LMS. 🔴 A hosted-model dependency is a **disqualifier** in this segment.

🟡 Demand-side context carried and corrected: a **separate** *AI in Higher Education LATAM Survey
`2026`* reports `92%` of **students** using AI regularly / `67%` weekly — **a different instrument and
a different population**; see `619-N` for why the two must not be quoted as one. IDB's **fAIr Tech
Radar** (`November 2025`) frames regional adoption as advancing amid structural gaps and heterogeneous
digital capability. UNESCO has flagged a **severe regional teacher shortage** against the `2030` goals
— 🔵 which is the demand-side reason a *teacher-time* product outsells a *student-facing* one here.

🔴 **Regulation:** Baker McKenzie's position is unchanged and is the honest summary — **no Latin
American country has a fully mature, harmonised AI regime**, with education named as one of the exposed
sectors; the environment is a patchwork of pilot policies vulnerable to each change of administration.
🔴 **Gap:** no **new dated national education-AI instrument** resolved for any LATAM country this pass.
Access constraints (connectivity, devices) remain the binding delivery limit.

### Global

🟢 **The cross-regional finding of this pass is a supply asymmetry inside this base, and it was
measurable** (`619-K2`): before `p619` the shelf mentioned `sign language` **`4`** times and
`screen reader` **`4`** times, against `proctor` **`382`** and `rubric` **`334`**. 🔴 A base `95×`
better supplied on **invigilation** than on **Deaf access** was describing its own priorities, not the
sector's. `12` permissive rows now close part of that.

🟢 **And the global structural opportunity is that the accessibility tier has components but no
platform** (`619-M2`): `12` permissive models, corpora, assets and single-purpose apps, and **no
permissive platform of record** — no `IEP` management system, no `AAC` platform, no Deaf-education LMS
returned a permissive grant. 🔵 For a studio this is the best-shaped opportunity on the page: the
expensive parts (recognition models at `265★`/`106` forks, a `248`-lesson corpus, a decodable-text
asset layer) are **permissive and already built**; the missing part is the **integration and the
compliance story**, which is exactly what an engagement sells. 🔴 And it is a **risk** in any estimate
that assumes a platform can be adopted rather than built.

🟡 **Market sizing stays a named range, never a point** (`619-O`): `2026` global AI-in-education figures
in channel span **`$3.7B`** (Persistence) to **`$8.3B`**; North America is quoted at **`$3.68B`/`36%`**
by one source and **`38%` share** by another; Technavio's **`41.7%`** is *contribution to growth*, not
share, and is **not comparable** to either. 🔴 `598-G`: all of it is search-snippet-grade.

🟢 **Policy direction, consistent across all four regions and the one durable read:** the instruments
that landed this pass are **disclosure and governance** instruments (Philippine DepEd disclosure duty,
EU ethical guidelines, Singapore's supervised age-gating, `134` US state bills), not capability bans.
🔵 **The sector is buying accountability, not detection** — and OECD's `2026` Digital Education Outlook
position (*AI helps only where pedagogy stays in control*) is the same claim from the pedagogy side.


## 🆕 `p618` — reads `2026-11-02` — market map and regional placement, **and `gate-region-headings` is red on this very file: `13` canonical blocks where its contract allows `1`**

### 🔴 `618-L` — the gate that was celebrated for being wired is failing, and nothing reported it

`gate-region-headings` carries the docstring *"the gate existed with a passing suite from `p383` to
`p337` and was in no target, so `make hooks` never judged this file's region structure at all"* — the
same defect class `618-A` just closed for `p370-gap-gate`. 🟢 It **is** wired, and in `HOOK_GATES`.

🔴 **And it is red on the live file.** `check_headings.py` emits `P383-MULTIPLE-BLOCKS` for every
canonical block after the first (`len(bs) > 1`), and this file carried **`13`** of them — one per pass
from `p604` to `p617`, each a legitimate historical record, accumulating one per pass because every
pass appended its own block.

🔵 **So `618-A`'s lesson needs its third form.** A gate built and not wired is not a control
(`617-B`). A gate wired but whose red is never reached is **also** not a control: `616` reported
`hooks` *"green on every offline licence control"* and red on `recount`, and `recount`'s red is enough
to stop the runner before this file is ever judged. 🔴 **An inherited red masks every gate ordered
behind it.**

🟢 **Repaired structurally rather than by suppression.** The `13` historical headings are retitled
`## Opportunities by region — \`pNNN\` (archived)`, which takes them out of `CANON.match` and leaves
**exactly one** canonical block — this pass's, below. No prose was removed: every historical region
subsection keeps its text and its pass attribution, and `blocks()` simply stops parsing archived
headings as the live contract.

🔴 **Not gate-verified.** Per `618-B` no instrument in this clone can be executed in this session, so
this repair is reasoned from `check_headings.py`'s source (read, not run) and is **owed one green run**
by a session that can execute it, together with `gate-declared-gap`.

🟡 **Every figure below is secondary by construction** (`618-K`): `eur-lex.europa.eu`, `oecd.org`,
`unesco.org`, `moodledev.io` and `docs.openedx.org` do not resolve from this environment, for the
sixteenth consecutive pass. Vendor market sizings disagree by a factor of three and are reported as a
**spread**, never as a point estimate.

### 🔴 The global sizing spread, stated as a spread
Secondary channels put the AI-in-education market at **≈`$6.4B`–`$11B`** for `2025`/`2026` with CAGRs
of **`21%`–`41%`** — a three-fold disagreement on size and a two-fold one on growth. 🔴 **No figure in
this band is citable as a point estimate**, and this base should quote the band and its own
unreachability note rather than pick a vendor.

## Opportunities by region — `p618` (archived)

### North America
🔵 **Signal.** Secondary channels place North America at **≈`36`–`38%`** of the global market
(≈`$3.68B` in `2026` on one vendor's basis). The concrete, verifiable items are **policy and funding**,
not market size: the US Department of Education released **`$169M`** in `FIPSE` awards including
support for responsible AI use, and a legislative tracker reported **`134` bills across `31` states**
touching AI in education as of March `2026` — data privacy, classroom use and curriculum.
🔴 **COPPA** governs child-facing products and is the binding constraint on any K-12 learner-facing
surface.

🟢 **Where the studio can sell.** The `134`-bill patchwork is the opportunity: a district or system
buyer needs an **auditable** assessment surface whose deliverable is a decision record, not a model.
`P617-2`'s audit-first shape applies directly, and `618-D`'s permissive rows supply the learner-facing
half without a licence bar. 🔴 Avoid pitching a single national compliance story — there is none.

### EMEA
🔵 **Signal.** The **EU AI Act** is the backdrop, with education uses in the high-risk Annex III class;
this base's settled compliance date is **`2027-12-02`** (`Reg. (EU) 2026/1744`), 🔴 **carried from
`p233`/`p241` and never read first-hand here** (`614-C`, unpayable again). The European Commission
updated its **ethical guidelines on AI in education**, the Council of Europe has run working
conferences specifically on **regulating AI in education**, and the UK committed **>`£200M`** to AI
adoption (incl. `£100M` expanding Bridge AI, `£53M` regional).

🟢 **Where the studio can sell.** Sovereignty plus a dated regulatory deadline is the strongest
engagement driver of any region: an on-premises or EU-hosted stack with documented data residency,
assembled from permissive components, sold against the `2027-12-02` date. 🟡 `vantaflow`'s French
skills collection remains the only FR-language row on this shelf, and it is an **index**, not supply.

### APAC
🔵 **Signal.** Forrester expects **`60%`** of firms and governments in major markets such as India and
China to run **locally developed LLMs alongside global ones**, naming education among the target
sectors; regional governance (the **ASEAN Guide on AI Governance and Ethics**) is explicitly
early-stage, and commentary frames **AI sovereignty** as the pacing factor for `2026`. Australia has a
National AI Plan and Chief AI Officers, 🔴 not education-specific.

🟢 **Where the studio can sell.** APAC is the one region where this base holds **native-language
supply** it did not have to translate: `mcxiaoxiao/kaoyan-navigator-skill` (考研 admissions) and
`ChatGPT3a01/claude-educational-ai-skills`. A local-weights deployment with per-jurisdiction policy
nodes is the shape; the multi-jurisdiction problem is the billable part.

### LATAM
🔵 **Signal, and it is the strongest adoption evidence of any region.** A Digital Education Council
survey with **Tecnológico de Monterrey** (Feb `2026`, `29` universities) reports **`92%` of students**
and **`79%` of faculty** actively engaging with AI; faculty positive opinion rose **`57%` → `72%`**,
while `88%` describe their own use as moderate. A **UNESCO IESALC** survey of **`200` institutions
across `19` countries** (fielded Aug–Oct `2025`) finds the **adoption-versus-governance gap
particularly pronounced** in the region, and `ILIA` reports LAC at **`14%` of global visits to AI
tools against `11%` of global internet users**. 🔴 No **binding** regional AI-in-education regulation
surfaced; Brookings argues the region is a natural **sandbox** instead.

🟢 **Where the studio can sell.** Demand is already there and **governance is the missing half** — the
inverse of EMEA, where regulation leads. The sellable deliverable is an institutional AI-use policy
with an attached technical surface, not a pilot. `P617-3`'s shape holds: **attach** to the client's
existing Open edX estate rather than migrate it.

🔴 **The supply-side gap stands, and is re-typed exactly as `617-C` left it.** The **index** form is
**withdrawn** — `LabSirius/TutorIA` (`MIT`, Universidad Tecnológica de Pereira, Risaralda, Colombia,
inside Open edX) was re-measured current this pass at `0★`, pushed `2026-10-27` (`618-I`). The
**channel** form is **open**: no leg this pass placed a new row in LATAM. The **cause** stands — the
region publishes and the `LICENSE` file is what is missing (`1 of 8` permissive across `latam-gpt`,
`7 of 8` with no licence file) — so `616-H`'s remedy stands too: **budget upstream `LICENSE` pull
requests** instead of waiting for new repositories.

🔴 **And this pass could not have placed a LATAM row even if a leg had found one** (`618-J`):
`users/{login}` is `403`, so no holder location is readable first-hand, and this base **does not infer
region from an anthroponym**. All three new rows are filed *holder not placed*.

### Global
🔵 The **OECD `2026` Digital Education Outlook** is reported to warn that AI aids learning **only when
pedagogy stays in control** — the single most useful framing for a studio pitch, because it moves the
deliverable from model choice to instructional design. **OpenAI** launched a country-level education
programme with **`8` national partners** in Q1 `2026`, which is the competitive backdrop for any
ministry-level conversation.

🟢 **The durable cross-region asset from this pass** is `614-I`'s class, confirmed again by `618-D`:
supply arriving as **skill packages riding somebody else's agent**. The deliverable is **prompt text**,
so it crosses borders with no infrastructure and no data-residency argument — 🔴 which is also why its
licence must be byte-read every time (`618-E`, `618-F`), since a permissive default is least reliable
for copy.


## 🆕 `p617` — reads `2026-11-02` — market map and regional placement, **and the LATAM entry is a withdrawal: this base's own index refutes the gap it published yesterday**

🟢 **Channel note first, because it bounds every figure below.** Every market and regulatory number in
this section is **secondary** — survey bodies, regulators, market-research firms, trade press. 🔴
`614-H` is now in its **fifteenth consecutive pass**: `eur-lex.europa.eu`, `moodledev.io`,
`docs.openedx.org` and `www.oecd.org` were each probed again this pass and each returned
`getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND`. **No primary text was read, again.** 🟢 New this pass: the channel's own error
text now **names the remedy** — *"has to be allowed in the session's network settings"* — so the ask
is **tool-stated rather than inferred** (`617-I`). 🟢 The only first-hand channel this pass had was the
grant channel (`raw.githubusercontent.com` + repository pages), and `617-A`/`617-D` are what it bought.

### 🔴 `617-A` — the LATAM supply gap: the index form is WITHDRAWN

🔵 `616-H` converted this gap from a count of nils into a measured **cause**, which was real progress
and **stands**. 🔴 It then restated the gap as *"still no LATAM-published, permissive,
education-specific row"* — an **index** claim, and the index refutes it.
[`LabSirius/TutorIA`](https://github.com/LabSirius/TutorIA) is **`MIT`** (re-read first-hand this pass:
title `MIT License`, © `2026 Grupo Sirius`, no clause beyond stock), published by the **Grupo Sirius**
group of the **Universidad Tecnológica de Pereira** for rural higher education in **Risaralda,
Colombia**, and it runs **inside Open edX**. 🟢 LATAM-published, permissive, education-specific.

🟢 **The channel gap stands and is the one to carry forward:** this base's discovery legs do not find
LATAM education supply reliably — `616-H`'s `latam-gpt` enumeration returned `1 of 8` permissive and
`0 of 8` education-specific, and `7 of 8` had no licence file at all. 🔵 **The cause and its remedy are
unchanged: the region publishes; the `LICENSE` file is what is missing, so contribute one upstream
rather than wait for new repositories.** See `617-B` for why the gate that would have caught the false
half has never run.

## Opportunities by region — `p617` (archived)

### North America

🟢 **Scale, publisher named.** MarketsandMarkets places North America at **`36 %`** of the

guardrails over mandated rollout. 🟢 Four states — **Maryland, Idaho, Oklahoma, Virginia** — now
require **both** state guidance **and** mandatory district-level AI policy adoption, which is the
sharpest procurement trigger in the region: a district under a policy mandate needs an auditable system
before it needs a better tutor. 🔵 Federal: a **K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act of 2026** advanced
out of the House Education and Workforce Committee; **not enacted**, recorded as a signal only.

🔴 **The operative asymmetry remains institutional.** Teacher adoption reported at **`60 %`** (Gallup,
secondary), while most US public schools still lack a formal student AI policy. 🟢 That gap is the
region's most reliable near-term demand, and it is a **governance** engagement, not a model engagement.

`🔵 Global — holder not placed` rather than guessed into a region.

🟢 **The region's differentiator is still the regulatory calendar, and this pass's leg re-confirmed

**`2027-12-02`**, with **`2028-08-02`** for AI embedded in regulated products. 🟢 Both values are

🟢 **Fragmentation is the structural fact, and it is a delivery cost rather than a barrier.** Korea's
**AI Basic Act** in force **`2026-01-22`** (enforcement decrees still being finalised, guidance-first

education — automated assessment and behavioural systems — named in its **high-risk list**; **Japan**

🟢 **Australia is the region's cleanest engagement trigger:** **TEQSA** requires *every* higher

band above, so the `2027-12-02` date is an **APAC** date for any exporter.

🔵 **Delivery note that follows from `617-A` rather than from market data:** the reusable LATAM asset is
an **Open edX attachment**, so a LATAM engagement adds a permissive tutoring surface to the client's
existing `AGPL-3.0` estate instead of migrating it. That is a materially cheaper first engagement than

### Global

🟢 **The cross-regional constant is unchanged and this pass sharpened its supply-side half:** adoption
leads governance everywhere the legs reach (`87 %`/`26 %` LATAM HEIs, `60 %` US teachers against absent
school policy, `134` US state bills, `716` LATAM instruments), and the scarce input is **not** a model —
it is an **auditable record of how a decision about a learner was made**.

🟢 **Which is why `617-D`'s shapes matter more than their star counts.** `azapg/FAIR` is built around
contestable assessment; `sirithreddy175-boop/GradeMateAI` **recommends** a mark and leaves the decision

🟡 At `6★` and `0★` they are starting points, not dependencies — stated so the shape argument is not
mistaken for an adoption claim.

🔴 **Standing structural limit on this whole section:** `614-H`/`617-I`. Fifteen passes without a
primary source. Every figure above is secondary **by construction**, and the remedy is an allow-list
entry, not more research.

🔵 This base has carried *"no LATAM-published permissive repository on any sell shelf"* as a declared

## Opportunities by region — `p616` (archived)

### North America

🟢 **Scale, with the publisher named.** MarketsandMarkets places North America at **`36 %`** of the
AI-in-education market, **`$3.68 B`** in `2026`; a separate estimate puts the region at `38 %` of

🔴 **The operative asymmetry is institutional, not technical.** A Gallup study (April `2026`, reached
here only in secondary digest) reports **over `60 %` of college students using AI weekly** for
coursework while **roughly half of their institutions discourage or prohibit it**. 🟢 That gap — heavy
sanctioned-adjacent use against unresolved policy — is the shape of a governance engagement, and it is
the region's most reliable near-term demand signal.

🟡 **Regulatory surface is state-level and moving.** `134` bills across `31` states tracked as of
March `2026` covering privacy, classroom restriction and curriculum; a compliance reading notes
multinationals must reconcile Canadian requirements against US state law with no federal settlement.
🟢 Public money is real: the US Department of Education released **`$169 M`** in FIPSE awards including
responsible-AI support.

🔵 **Supply placement this pass:** `0` new North America rows. 🟡 `616-C`'s ungranted
`dmccreary/ibook-skills` is the pass's most substantial education-specific corpus and its holder could
**not** be placed — the profile endpoint returned nothing through this environment's channel, so it is
shelved `🔵 Global — holder not placed` rather than guessed into this region.

🟢 **Policy is the region's differentiator and this pass's legs reflect it.** The EU Commission
updated its **ethical guidelines on AI in education** and signed three Advanced Digital Skills grant
agreements worth **`€25.4 M`** (QS EdTech newsletter; 🟡 the year is not stated in the source and is
not asserted here). The UK's first AI Adoption Summit committed **`£200 M+`**, of which **`£100 M`**

**`2027-12-02` under `Reg. (EU) 2026/1744`** (`p233`/`p241`; `2026-08` superseded) — carried, not
re-established this pass.

🔴 **Supply: measured, and the finding is `616-H` above** — `1` of `8` permissive across the region's

reporting heavy undergraduate and graduate use. 🔵 The region's standing asymmetry on this base —
**rich in demand evidence, empty in measured local permissive supply** — is unchanged and now

🟡 **Regulation is fragmented by design.** A Baker McKenzie briefing describes countries at different
stages with no common framework; an ECLAC-based report (October `2025`) names **Chile, Brazil and
Mexico** as leaders while flagging regional gaps in data, training and regulation.

🔴 **Infrastructure is the binding constraint, and it selects the architecture.** Connectivity,
device access and digital literacy are reported as the limiters. 🟢 That is why
[`learningequality/kolibri`](https://github.com/learningequality/kolibri) (`MIT`, `1,097 B`,

**`~34 %`** of `2026` share.

🟢 **The structural trend, and it is this pass's own subject.** New permissive education supply keeps
arriving as **agent-skill packages riding somebody else's coding agent** (`p614`'s class, confirmed
again: `4 of 4` new rows this pass are skill packages, not applications). 🔴 `616-A` is the cost of
that vehicle: a skill package is a **tree of vendored directories**, so its licence question is a
*composition* question, and a single title-line read is structurally insufficient for the dominant
shape of new supply in this sector.

🔵 **Standing governance signal:** the OECD's `2026` Digital Education Outlook is reported as warning
that AI helps learning only where pedagogy stays in control — carried as secondary, since `oecd.org`
does not resolve here.

## Opportunities by region — `p615` (archived)

### North America

🟢 **The engagement shape is governance and compliance, not model-building.** Measured signals:
`134` AI-in-education bills introduced across `31` states in `2026`, concentrated on data privacy,
classroom-use limits and curriculum. `4` states — Maryland, Idaho, Oklahoma, Virginia — require
**both** state guidance **and** mandatory district policies. New York's `A 9190` would restrict
classroom AI to grade `9` and above, with diagnostic and special-education carve-outs. The
`K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act of 2026` advanced in the House Education and Workforce
Committee.

🟢 Adoption is ahead of policy: Gallup found **~`60 %` of US teachers** use AI tools (`n ≈ 2,200`),
while most US public schools still lack a formal student AI policy. 🟢 **The gap is the opportunity**
— district-level policy tooling, audit trails and AI-literacy curriculum are procurable line items,
and `4` states now mandate the artefact.
🟡 An **April `2026` ADA accessibility deadline** for large public institutions was cited by one
industry leader; 🔴 single-source, so flagged rather than relied on.
🔵 Players: state education departments and legislatures, US Dept. of Education, NEA and AFT,
AASA, Day of AI, Khan Academy.
🟢 Supply fit: `adlnet` tier is US DoD-published and `Apache-2.0`/`MIT` (`615-E`) — the natural
foundation for a federal or state analytics brief.

### EMEA

🟢 **The engagement shape is high-risk conformity work under the EU AI Act.** Enforcement by the AI
Office and national authorities began **`2026-08-02`**. Education AI that evaluates learners, assigns
them to programmes, detects prohibited behaviour during assessment, or influences access to
qualifications is treated as **high-risk** — conformity assessment and human oversight attach.

🔴 **The application dates are genuinely contested across sources and must be checked against the
Official Journal before any client commitment.** One source reports the AI omnibus as
Regulation (EU) `2026/1744`, published `2026-07-24`, applying from `2026-07-27`; another gives
high-risk application as `2027-12-02` (stand-alone Annex III) and `2028-08-02` (embedded in
regulated products); an education-specific guide instead says August `2026`. 🟢 The later omnibus
dates appear to supersede the last, but this base does not assert which is operative.
🟢 A ban on AI for non-consensual intimate imagery and CSAM comes into force **`2026-12-02`** — not
contested in the sources.

🟡 **UK diverges and is the region's nearest-term delivery:** light-touch, principle-based regulation
via existing regulators, and a January `2026` government commitment to tutoring tools for
**`450,000` disadvantaged pupils**, classroom-tested, with national rollout by `2027`. 🟢 That is a
named, funded, dated programme — the most concrete procurement signal in any region this pass.
🟡 Gulf is moving on policy: Dubai's **KHDA** has issued explicit responsible-AI guidance to private
schools. The EU Commission's **AI Continent Action Plan** names education a priority sector.
🔴 **Declared gap: Africa and the non-Gulf Middle East returned nothing relevant.** The leg covered
EMEA and came back with EU, UK and Gulf material only. That is an informed gap, not coverage —
a successor should run a dedicated Africa leg rather than inherit this silence as an answer.

### APAC

🟢 **The engagement shape is multi-jurisdiction compliance, because the region does not converge.**
Korea's **AI Basic Act** has been in force since **`2026-01-22`**, with transparency, risk
assessment, human oversight and documentation duties for high-impact systems. Vietnam's July `2026`
decision lists **education, including automated assessment**, among six high-risk sectors. Australia's
**TEQSA** requires *all* higher-education providers to file institutional action plans addressing
generative-AI risk. China runs targeted rules; Japan and Singapore remain light-touch. Malaysia's
national digital policy frames a *"secure and sovereign"* cloud as foundational.

🟢 **So a regional platform needs per-jurisdiction policy behaviour, not one global setting** — the
same architecture this base has recommended for APAC, now with Vietnam and Australia added as named
obligations. 🔴 One source notes GDPR-equivalent law is **absent in many APAC countries**, which cuts
both ways: lower compliance friction, higher reputational exposure.
🔵 Players named by a market report: Google, Microsoft, IBM, Pearson, Byju's; China, India and Japan
described as dominating the market. 🟡 Commercial estimate, not a primary source.
🔴 **Supply caution specific to this region:** `cn-math-taxonomy`, the curriculum knowledge graph
aligned to China's national curriculum and the most valuable APAC-specific asset this base holds, is
`CC-BY-NC-4.0` and **cannot be shipped commercially** (`615-A`). Budget the prerequisite layer.

### LATAM

🟢 **The engagement shape is governance retrofit onto adoption that already happened — and this is
the region where the evidence is strongest, from a primary multilateral source.**

| measurement | value | source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| HEIs using AI in ≥ 1 area | 🟢 **`87 %`** | UNESCO IESALC, `200` HEIs across `19` countries |
| HEIs with **any** formal framework | 🔴 **`26 %`** | same |
| students actively engaging with AI | `92 %` | Digital Education Council LATAM `2026` |
| faculty actively engaging | `79 %` | same |
| Brazil: school administrators using AI | `53 %` | Cetic.br `2026` |
| Brazil: schools with guidelines on its use | 🔴 `22 %` | same |
| AI regulatory instruments across `23` countries/territories | `716`, **>`80 %` initiated since `2023`** | Universidad de los Andes, `2026` |

🟢 **A `61`-point gap between use and governance at institutional level, and a `31`-point gap in
Brazilian schools.** That is the most quantified opportunity in this file: AI-governance frameworks,
accreditation-ready policy, and audit tooling for universities that are already running AI.
🟢 **UNESCO is the region's convening buyer-adjacent actor** — it launched the **Observatory on AI in
Education for Latin America and the Caribbean** in April `2026` at ECLAC in Santiago, and runs AI
regulation courses with a Tecnológico de Monterrey partnership.
🔵 Players: UNESCO IESALC, Tecnológico de Monterrey (Institute for the Future of Education),
Universidad de los Andes, CENIA (Chile), Ceibal (Uruguay), Cetic.br (Brazil), OECD.
🟡 Commentary (Global Americans) argues for **regional interoperability** — common definitions, risk
categories, audit principles, mutual recognition — rather than one regional AI law. 🟢 That favours a
portable governance toolkit over a per-country build.
🔴 **Declared gap: Spanish- and Portuguese-language open-source sector supply returned nothing.** A
dedicated leg (`9`) found Piauí's mandatory weekly AI subject with **Creative Commons** teacher
materials, Portugal's `Amália` open model (licence unnamed in results), DeepMind's `GAIA` on Gemma 3,
and Itaú's `Aroeira` dataset — but **no `MIT`/`Apache` LATAM-origin education repository**. 🟢 The
region is a demand market in this base's measurements, not yet a supply one.

### Global

🟢 Cross-region, converging on one sentence from the trend legs: **`2026` is the year education AI
moves from experimentation to governed, embedded infrastructure** — admissions, advising and
administration rather than demos. 🟢 Two structural facts hold in every region measured: adoption
leads policy, and the binding constraint on open-source reuse is the **grant**, not the model.
🔴 `615-A` is the Global-scope warning: the sector's scarcest open asset was published by this base
as shippable and is `NonCommercial`. 🟢 Grant verification is a **delivery control**, not hygiene.

## Opportunities by region — `p614` (archived)

### North America

🟢 **The buyer is the district and the trigger is a policy mandate.** `MD`, `ID`, `OK` and `VA`
require district-level AI policy adoption in `2026`, against `134` bills in `31` states and a
measured governance gap — `60 %` of US teachers reported using AI tools (Gallup) while most public
schools still lack a formal student AI policy (Child Trends / US DoE). 🔵 **The sellable object is
therefore the policy-to-implementation bridge, not a tutor**: a conformance and audit layer that
produces evidence a district can show. 🟢 This base's permissive inventory for it is real and local:
`haxtheweb/haxcms-php` (`Apache-2.0`, Penn State) for institutionally-provenanced course content, the
`613-B` xAPI/cmi5 conformance tier published by the **US DoD's ADL Initiative** — a US-government
publisher, which is a procurement advantage in this region specifically — and `612-E`'s
rubric/autograding tier as the evidence trail. 🔴 **And the warning row is a North American product:**
`canyongbs/advisingapp` (`340★`) is the obvious student-success base and its Elastic `2.0` grant
forbids operating it as a managed service, so a US engagement must self-host it for the client or
choose something else.

### EMEA

🟢 **A dated compliance clock, and two sub-markets that behave differently.** The Annex III education
obligations bind **`2027-12-02`** (`Reg. (EU) 2026/1744`), with AI embedded in regulated products at
`2028-08-02`; both are far enough out that a conformity programme is a planned build rather than a
scramble, which is the opposite of how the superseded `2026-08` date framed it. 🔵 **Build the
conformity evidence now, sell it as readiness, and do not re-price it as an emergency.** 🟢 Supply
placed here this pass: `opencast/annotation-tool` (`ECL-2.0`, European academic consortium) and
`elimu-ai/webapp` (`MIT`, early-grade literacy for out-of-school children) — the latter is this
pass's one genuinely region-placed supply row and points at the **Sub-Saharan** half of EMEA, which
this base has historically left empty. 🔴 Also placed here: `codeforlife-education`'s UK primary
curriculum, which is `AGPL-3.0` behind a `121 B` pointer file (`614-F`) — reference only. 🟢 The Gulf
is a distinct motion: KHDA and the UAE MoE have issued guidance ahead of infrastructure, so the
opening there is governance tooling and teacher capability, not platform replacement.

### APAC

🟢 **The region with the most permissive supply this pass and the most jurisdictional fragmentation.**
`3` of this pass's `8` new agent rows are APAC-placed — `shigella520/MindTrain` (`MIT`, pluggable
schedulers + FSRS), `zhangl1001/civil-ai` (`MIT`, **local-first** adaptive tutoring with a
civil-service-exam vertical) — alongside the two re-measured giants, `HKUDS/DeepTutor`
(`Apache-2.0`, `54,089★`, HKU) and `THU-MAIC/OpenMAIC` (`MIT`, `42,470★`, Tsinghua). 🟢 **And the
scarcest asset in the region is data, not code:** `xiaoyang111222/cn-math-taxonomy` (`6,143★`) is a
curriculum-aligned knowledge graph — `1,644` knowledge points, `2,403` prerequisite edges, `6,723`
problems — under a Creative Commons grant that is *fit for purpose on data* (`614-G`). 🔴 The
regulatory map is not one market: Korea's AI Basic Act is in force (`2026-01-22`), Vietnam names
education high-risk from `2026-03-01`, China requires registration and content labelling, Australia's
TEQSA demands an institutional action plan from every provider, while Japan and Singapore stay
voluntary. 🔵 **So the pattern that sells is a policy-parameterised deployment** — one stack, per-
jurisdiction policy configuration — and `civil-ai`'s local-first shape plus `MindTrain`'s swappable
scheduler are the permissive pieces that make per-market variation cheap. 🔴 Data-sovereignty
expectations (Malaysia's national digital policy, among others) make the local-first row more
valuable here than its `41★` suggests.

### LATAM

🟢 **The clearest quantified gap in any region, and it is a governance gap, not an adoption gap.**
UNESCO IESALC, across ~`200` institutions in `19` countries: **`87 %`** use AI in at least one area
while only **`26 %`** have any formal framework. 🔵 Brazil shows the same shape one level down —
`53 %` of school administrators using AI against `22 %` with guidelines (Cetic.br) — and the Digital
Education Council puts student engagement at `92 %` and faculty at `79 %`. 🔴 **Those are different
populations and must not be averaged.** 🟢 Meanwhile `716` regulatory instruments across `23`
countries (Universidad de los Andes, `2026`), `>80 %` of them started since `2023`, and the UNESCO
LAC Observatory launched in Santiago in `2026-04`. 🔵 **The engagement is therefore institutional
governance delivered as working software** — an AI-use register, an assessment-integrity trail and an
accreditation-ready evidence pack — against a region that has already adopted the technology and
must now account for it. 🟢 Named partners with national reach: **Ceibal** (Uruguay, `75 %` of
public-school teachers reporting use), **Cetic.br**, **CENIA** (Chile), **Tec de Monterrey** (which
has a UNESCO agreement on AI in education). 🔴 Honest limit: this base has **no LATAM-published
permissive repository** on any sell shelf — the region is rich in demand evidence and empty in
measured local supply, and no pass has closed that.

### Global

🟢 **The pass's own headline is region-neutral and that is the point:** new permissive education
supply is arriving as **skill packages for somebody else's coding agent** (`614-I`) — `4` new rows at
`20`–`45★` plus the `36,751★` row this base already held, every one `MIT` on bytes. 🔵 Because the
artefact is prompt text rather than a deployment, it crosses borders with no infrastructure and no
data-residency argument, which is why it has no region attached. 🔴 **And it is where licence risk is
highest**: `613-E`'s `CC-BY-SA-4.0` specimen is this same class, and share-alike on text a studio
pastes into a client's agent is an obligation on the deliverable itself. 🟢 Also global and
unchanged: the `612-D` LTI launch seam and the `613-B`/`613-C` xAPI telemetry seam, which together
bound the commercial boundary around a `GPL-3.0`/`AGPL-3.0` incumbent in every region. 🔴 Standing
global limit, unchanged for fourteen passes: no primary market or regulatory source is readable from
this session, so every figure above is secondary (`598-G`, now with a cause — `614-H`).

## 🆕 `p613` — reads `2026-11-02` — market map and regional placement

🔴 **`598-G` — thirteenth consecutive pass open, control unchanged.** `unesco.org`, `oecd.org` and the
primary market-research PDFs remain unreachable from this session, as do `moodledev.io` and
`docs.openedx.org` (`612`). 🔵 Every market figure below is therefore a **secondary** read and is
labelled with who published it; none is a figure this base measured. 🟢 The *supply* figures — stars,
grants, branches, push dates — are all first-hand and byte-read today.

### 🔵 What moved this pass

🟢 **The sellable asset moved from the tutor to the seam.** `612-D` shelved the **launch** seam (LTI
`1.3`, permissive, both incumbents copyleft). `613-B`/`613-C` shelve the **telemetry** seam (xAPI /
cmi5, `Apache-2.0`, published by the US DoD's ADL Initiative, with a `MIT` conformance test suite and
two live permissive Learning Record Stores). 🔵 Together those two seams are the whole commercial
boundary around a copyleft incumbent: a studio can launch into Moodle or Open edX and report out of it
without touching `GPL-3.0` or `AGPL-3.0` code.

🔴 **And the week's loudest education-agent repository is unsellable.**
`GarethManning/education-agent-skills` (`942★`) is `CC-BY-SA-4.0` on bytes — share-alike, on prompt
text (`613-E`). 🔵 The commercially relevant reading: **attention in this sector is not tracking
licence-usable supply**, so star rank is not a shortlist.

estimate, they are different definitions of the market. 🟢 Quote a **range** (`$3.7B`–`$11B` for `2026`)
with the publisher attached, never a point estimate, and never average them.

## Opportunities by region — `p613` (archived)

### North America

🔵 **Demand.** `~36 %` of global AI-in-education spend, `~$3.68B` in `2026` (Markets and Markets via
summary). US Department of Education released `$169M` in FIPSE awards including responsible-AI use.
🔴 **Regulation is a patchwork, not a statute:** `134` bills across `31` states as of `2026-03`, no
federal instrument.

🟢 **Supply placed here, byte-read today.** [`yetanalytics/lrsql`](https://github.com/yetanalytics/lrsql)
(`Apache-2.0`, `144★`) and [`yetanalytics/datasim`](https://github.com/yetanalytics/datasim)
(`Apache-2.0`, `23★`), plus the entire [`adlnet`](https://github.com/adlnet) conformance tier
(`Apache-2.0`/`MIT`, `12`–`956★`) — a **US federal agency** is the publisher.

🟢 **The opportunity, and it is specific to the patchwork.** `612`'s `P612-4` argued for
policy-parameterised multi-jurisdiction deployment. 🟢 `613` supplies the evidence layer that makes it
auditable: `adlnet/lrs-conformance-test-suite` is `MIT`, so a per-state compliance posture can be
expressed as a **test run inside the client's own CI** rather than a consultant's assertion. `50`
jurisdictions × one parameterised build is the shape; the conformance suite is what a state procurement
will actually ask for.

### EMEA

🔵 **Regulation is the clock, and it is already running.** `(EU) 2026/1744` (Digital AI Omnibus, in
force `2026-07-27`) defers Annex III **education** high-risk obligations to `2027-12-02` without
changing them; Article `50` transparency is live and watermarking is due `2026-12-02` (`612`). 🟢 New
this pass: the European Commission updated its **AI-in-education ethical guidelines** in `2026-06`, and
the UK's AI Adoption Summit committed `£200M+` (`£100M` Bridge AI, `£53M` regional).

🟢 **Supply placed here.** [`openfun/ralph`](https://github.com/openfun/ralph) (`MIT`, `51★`, pushed
`2026-11-01`) — LRS and learning-analytics toolbox from **France Université Numérique**, the French
state's higher-education digital operator. 🔵 With `612-F`'s three UK DfE services and Oak National
Academy's Aila, and `611-D`'s `bcgov/SIMS`, that is **three jurisdictions in three passes publishing
operational education code under permissive grants** — the pattern is now established, not anecdotal.

🟢 **The opportunity.** The `2027-12-02` deferral is thirteen months of runway, and the obligations are
unchanged — so the work is building the conformity file now. 🟢 `612-E`'s rubric tier plus `613-B`'s
conformance suite plus `ralph`'s analytics is a complete Annex III evidence chain assembled entirely
from permissive, state-published parts.

### APAC

🔵 **Demand and the sovereignty constraint together.** Forrester expects `60 %` of firms and governments
in major markets including India and China to run **locally developed** LLMs alongside global ones, with
education named as a target sector. 🔵 Korea's AI Basic Act binds education as a named high-impact
sector (in force `2026-01`); China's content-labelling rules and `50+` standards apply (`p612` record).

🟢 **Supply placed here.** `612` placed `9` region-tagged agent rows in APAC, led by
`BenyRonald77/uajy-academic-rag-chatbot` (`452★`, `MIT`, one named Indonesian university's handbook) and
`callmevanshh/campusask` (IIIT Delhi). 🔴 `613` placed **none** — the xAPI, LRS and FSRS tiers are
North American, EMEA and Global respectively.

🟢 **The opportunity.** The local-LLM requirement makes the *scheduler* more valuable than the model:
`613-D`'s FSRS tier is `MIT`/`BSD-3` and model-agnostic, and `fsrs-optimizer` fits to a learner's own
review history — so the retention asset survives a forced model swap to a domestic LLM. 🔵 That is the
one architecture where sovereignty is not a tax.

### LATAM

🟢 **The demand side is now quantified, and it inverts the standing read.** Digital Education Council ×
Tecnológico de Monterrey, *AI in Higher Education LATAM Survey 2026*: **`92 %` of students** and
**`79 %` of faculty** actively engaging with AI; faculty positive opinion rose **`57 % → 72 %`** in
`2026`. UNESCO IESALC surveyed **`200` institutions across `19` countries** and found the
adoption–governance gap **most pronounced in this region**; at the launch, the stated finding was a
*lack of formal institutional AI policies in Latin American universities*.

🔴 **Supply remains a measured absence, and `613` does not change it.** `612` recorded `0 of 68`
shippable-shaped candidates placing to LATAM. 🟢 This pass adds an independent confirmation: `0 of 24`
promoted rows place here, and `0 of 90` curated-index addresses. 🔵 Regional spend `$105.6M` → `$204.7M`
by `2029` — two orders of magnitude below global.

🟢 **The opportunity, and `613` sharpens what it is.** `612-5` read the gap as **content** (Spanish and
Portuguese material). 🔴 The LATAM survey says the binding constraint is **governance**: adoption is
already near-universal at `92 %`/`79 %`, so nobody needs persuading to use AI — what is missing is the
institutional policy, and `200` institutions in `19` countries are missing the same one. 🟢 That is a
**template** engagement, not a bespoke one: one AI-use policy plus the `613-B` conformance evidence plus
`612-E`'s rubric tier, instantiated per institution. 🔵 The architecture gap stays zero — every part is
already permissive and none of it is LATAM-specific, which is exactly why it can be delivered at
near-zero marginal cost.

| source | `2026` size | end state | CAGR |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| spread across four firms (roundup) | 🔴 **`$6.4B` – `$11.4B`** | — | — |

🔴 **A `78 %` spread between the low and high `2026` estimate, and one firm quoted at two figures by two
summaries.** 🟢 **Use the range in a client conversation, never a point.** The honest statement is
*"independent estimates of AI-in-education for 2026 run from roughly `$6B` to `$11B`, and the
`2030`-`2035` projections all imply a `31`–`35 %` CAGR"* — the growth rate is where the sources agree,
and it is the part that matters for a build-vs-buy decision.

🟡 Regional share, secondary: North America ≈ `36 %` of global; APAC fastest at ≈ `35.3 %` CAGR.
🟡 Adoption, and the sources disagree here too: student use `88 %` / faculty `77 %` in a July-`2026`
`35`-country survey, against another series showing `34 %` (`2023`) → `61 %` (`2026`). 🟢 Institution-wide
higher-ed adoption `49 %` → `66 %` in one year (Ellucian) is the most decision-relevant of the three,
because it is institutional budget rather than individual behaviour.

## Opportunities by region — `p612` (archived)

### North America

🔵 **The regulatory shape is `31` state legislatures, not one federal rule — and that is the
opportunity.** `134` AI-in-education bills were introduced across `31` states in `2026`, described by
the tracker as guardrails-and-transparency rather than adoption mandates. Ohio requires districts to
have adopted a formal AI policy by `2026-07-01`; New York's `A.9190` would bar most classroom AI below
grade `9`; SUNY (`64` campuses) approved a systemwide policy in spring `2026` with mandatory responsible-use
training and AI literacy in general-education requirements from fall `2026`. 🔴 A White House national AI
framework is reported to recommend that Congress **preempt** state law — 🟡 reported, not enacted, and it
is the single biggest planning uncertainty in this region.

🟢 **The opportunity is a policy-parameterised deployment, not a product.** A district consortium or a
state system cannot buy a fixed-policy tool when the policy differs by state and changes by session.
🟢 The architecture already exists, permissively, and this base holds it: `bcgov/SIMS` (`611-D`,
`Apache-2.0`) ships a **business-rules engine built so policy change is not a rewrite**. 🟡 Stated limit:
BC's rule *content* does not transfer, only the architecture.

🟢 **The region's own permissive assets, promoted this pass:**
[`ucbds-infra/otter-grader`](https://github.com/ucbds-infra/otter-grader) (`BSD-3-Clause`, `161★`, UC
Berkeley, Python **and** R autograding at course scale),
[`tl-its-umich-edu/my-learning-analytics`](https://github.com/tl-its-umich-edu/my-learning-analytics)
(`Apache-2.0`, University of Michigan's student-facing analytics dashboard),
[`instructure/ims-lti`](https://github.com/instructure/ims-lti) (`MIT`, the Canvas vendor's own LTI
library), [`harvard-edtech/caccl`](https://github.com/harvard-edtech/caccl) (`MIT`, Harvard's Canvas
connector), and [`anthropics/k12-teacher-skills`](https://github.com/anthropics/k12-teacher-skills)
(`Apache-2.0`, `609★`, with Learning Commons). 🔵 **Note the pattern: in North America the usable
education code is published by universities and by the LMS vendor, not by startups.**

### EMEA

🟢 **`612`'s most consequential external fact: there is a `16`-month build window, and it closes
`2027-12-02`.** Regulation **`(EU) 2026/1744`** (the Digital AI Omnibus) was published in the Official
Journal `2026-07-24` and entered into force `2026-07-27`. It defers Annex III **high-risk** obligations —
the annex that covers education — from `2026-08-02` to **`2027-12-02`**. Corroborated independently
across Cooley, DLA Piper and Gibson Dunn. 🔴 **Only the dates moved.** Risk management, data governance,
technical documentation, human oversight, conformity assessment and registration are unchanged.

| what | date | status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| emotion-recognition ban in education; AI-literacy duty | `2025-02-02` | 🔴 **already binding** |
| Article `50` transparency duties | `2026-08-02` | 🔴 **live and enforceable — not deferred** |
| machine-readable watermarking, systems already on market | `2026-12-02` | 🔴 transitional period **ends in one month** |
| Annex III high-risk for education | 🟢 `2027-12-02` | 🟢 deferred from `2026-08-02` |
| Annex I embedded high-risk | `2028-08-02` | deferred from `2027-08-02` |

🔴 **What is high-risk in education, verbatim:** admissions or placement; evaluating learning outcomes;
assessing the level of education a person can attain; monitoring students for prohibited behaviour during
tests. 🔴 **Schools are *deployers* and remain liable when the tool is a third party's**, and a non-EU
vendor is in scope if its system reaches EU learners.

🟢 **The opportunity is the conformity file, sold as engineering.** Most of the market will read a
`2027-12-02` deadline as permission to wait, and will then need the documentation retrofitted onto a
system that was not built to produce it. 🟢 Globant's play is to build the evidence trail as a
by-product: `612-E`'s rubric tier
([`Sion612/rubrictrail`](https://github.com/Sion612/rubrictrail) evidence-linked,
[`paper-instruments/rubric`](https://github.com/paper-instruments/rubric) weighted,
[`OpenEnvision/AutoRubric-as-Reward`](https://github.com/OpenEnvision/AutoRubric-as-Reward) for deriving
defensible criteria from expert judgement) plus
[`fairlearn/fairlearn`](https://github.com/fairlearn/fairlearn) (`MIT`, `2,306★`) for the bias position.
🔴 Note the one-month item above: **watermarking is due `2026-12-02`**, and that is the nearest deadline
in this table.

🟢 **The region's own permissive assets, promoted this pass:** the UK Department for Education publishes
**three live national services** under `MIT` —
[`apply-for-teacher-training`](https://github.com/DFE-Digital/apply-for-teacher-training),
[`teaching-vacancies`](https://github.com/DFE-Digital/teaching-vacancies),
[`get-into-teaching-app`](https://github.com/DFE-Digital/get-into-teaching-app) — and Oak National
Academy publishes its **Aila** lesson-planning assistant at
[`oaknational/oak-ai-lesson-assistant`](https://github.com/oaknational/oak-ai-lesson-assistant) (`MIT`,
pushed today). 🟢 Plus [`SafeExamBrowser/seb-server`](https://github.com/SafeExamBrowser/seb-server)
(`MPL-2.0`) and [`oxctl/spring-security-lti13`](https://github.com/oxctl/spring-security-lti13)
(`Apache-2.0`). 🔵 **In EMEA the state is the reference implementation.** That is the opposite of the
usual market map and it is the cheapest credibility an engagement can buy.

### APAC

🔵 **The fastest-growing region and the one where "national curriculum" is a literal engineering
requirement.** South Korea's **AI Basic Act took effect `2026-01-22`** — the second comprehensive
national AI law after the EU's. India plans compulsory AI and computational thinking **from Class `3` in
`AY2026-27`**, backed by the IndiaAI Mission and teacher training. China and the UAE are reported as the
only countries running compulsory national AI curricula since the `2025-26` school year; China also has
the densest compliance stack (algorithm filings, synthetic-content labelling, security review for public
models). Japan is deliberately voluntary — guidelines plus school pilots.

🔴 **The cautionary case is Korea, and it is about product management, not policy.** The AI digital
textbook rollout from `2025` was walked back to optional *"educational material"* status within a year
after parent and teacher backlash over screen time and data. 🟢 **Read it as a requirement:** in APAC K-12,
teacher and parent consent is a shipping dependency, and an offline or on-device mode is the mitigation —
which is exactly what [`michael-borck/study-buddy`](https://github.com/michael-borck/study-buddy)
(`MIT`, fully offline on Ollama) demonstrates.

🟢 **This is the region the backlog sweep placed most rows in, by a wide margin** — `6 of 9`
region-placed agent rows this pass:
[`BenyRonald77/uajy-academic-rag-chatbot`](https://github.com/BenyRonald77/uajy-academic-rag-chatbot)
(Indonesia, `452★`, production RAG over a named university's handbook),
[`3yangyang9/DeepTutor-AILearning`](https://github.com/3yangyang9/DeepTutor-AILearning) (China,
`Apache-2.0`, 🟢 **multi-tenant with per-tenant billing — the only commercial tenancy model on these
shelves**), [`Zenglian990/AI_Tutor_Release`](https://github.com/Zenglian990/AI_Tutor_Release) (China, K-9
RAG tutor), [`tanle-mtr/ChinaStudyFree`](https://github.com/tanle-mtr/ChinaStudyFree) (China, four
clients, `148` textbooks, 🟢 EdgeTTS for zero-marginal-cost voice),
[`callmevanshh/campusask`](https://github.com/callmevanshh/campusask) (India, IIIT Delhi rules, page-level
citations), [`niansia/taiwan-exam`](https://github.com/niansia/taiwan-exam) (Taiwan, 學測 mock papers with
solution re-verification) and [`naufaldi/teacher-exam`](https://github.com/naufaldi/teacher-exam)
(Indonesia, print-ready sheets for elementary teachers).

🟢 **The transferable insight from the whole APAC set: every one of them is curriculum-specific and
language-specific, and that is why they exist.** 🔵 The generic tutor does not win here; the one that
knows the local exam does. 🟢 Three of them (`campusask`, `uajy-…`, `taiwan-exam`) are the *same*
architecture — cited retrieval over an authoritative local document set — which is the single most
reusable pattern in this region.

### LATAM

🔴 **This pass's LATAM result is a measured absence, and it is stronger than a search nil.** Of `68`
shippable-shaped candidates drawn from a `848`-address census of material this base had already
collected, 🔴 **`0` placed to LATAM.** The search leg also returned no permissive LATAM education
repository, for the forty-second consecutive pass.

🟡 Figures, with reliability stated. Enterprise AI adoption Q1-`2026`: Brazil `41 %`, Chile `34 %`,
Argentina `29 %`, Colombia `24.5 %`, Mexico `20.1 %` — 🔴 low-trust source. Consumer/digital-user AI use
above `70 %` in Brazil (~`76 %`) and Mexico (~`70 %`) per an EFE report. 🔴 **The decisive number:**
MarketsandMarkets sizes LATAM AI-in-education at **`$105.6M` (`2024`) → `$204.7M` (`2029`)**, with Brazil
and Mexico more than half — 🔴 **two orders of magnitude below the global `$6`–`11B`.** 🟢 That gap, not
the growth rate, is the regional thesis: consumer adoption is at world-leading levels while institutional
education spend is negligible, so the money is in **public-sector and large-private-network programmes**,
not in per-seat licences.

🟡 Players: HolonIQ's `2026` LATAM EdTech `100` names **Teachy** (Brazil, AI lesson preparation) and
**Muyu Education** (Ecuador, teacher training); 🔴 neither publishes source. 🔴 OpenAI's *Education for
Countries* first cohort of eight contains **no Latin American country** — 🟢 an opening, since the
sovereign-programme slot is unfilled in the region.

🔴 **Regulation: unresolved and recorded as such.** One source states Brazil's `PL 2338/2023` is approved;
that contradicts other reporting that it remained in Congress. 🔴 **Not resolved here** — do not carry
either claim into a client conversation. Mexico is described by a Colegio de México expert as lacking both
regulation and the capacity to build it.

🟢 **What to do about it, concretely.** The architecture gap is `0`; the **content and language** gap is
everything. 🟢 Reuse the non-LATAM public-sector code — `bcgov/SIMS` (`Apache-2.0`, policy-rules engine)
and the three UK DfE `MIT` services — and fill the Spanish/Portuguese layer using
[`tanle-mtr/ChinaStudyFree`](https://github.com/tanle-mtr/ChinaStudyFree)'s route: `148` textbooks plus
free EdgeTTS voice is why that platform can be free, and nothing in it is language-bound. 🔵 Pair with
`callmevanshh/campusask`'s cited-retrieval-over-official-PDFs shape, which is how a ministry's own
curriculum documents become a tutor without a content-licensing negotiation.

### Global

🔴 **The incumbents are copyleft, and that single fact structures every engagement.** Moodle is `GPL-3.0`
(`7,570★`, default branch `main`); Open edX is `AGPL-3.0`. 🟢 **So the integration seam — LTI — is the
commercial boundary**, and `612-D` promoted the full permissive tier for it: Ruby
([`instructure/ims-lti`](https://github.com/instructure/ims-lti)), Python
([`jupyterhub/ltiauthenticator`](https://github.com/jupyterhub/ltiauthenticator)), Java/Spring
([`oxctl/spring-security-lti13`](https://github.com/oxctl/spring-security-lti13)), .NET
([`LtiLibrary/LtiAdvantagePlatform`](https://github.com/LtiLibrary/LtiAdvantagePlatform)) and TypeScript
([`harvard-edtech/caccl`](https://github.com/harvard-edtech/caccl)). 🟢 One exception worth knowing:
**the official Moodle *app* is `Apache-2.0`** ([`moodlehq/moodleapp`](https://github.com/moodlehq/moodleapp),
`1,004★`) — the permissive surface of a copyleft incumbent is its mobile client.

🔴 **Operational: both incumbents moved something that breaks tooling this year** (`612-G`, resolved from
refs and bytes because the doc hosts are unreachable). Moodle **deleted root `version.php` at `5.x`** —
it is `public/version.php` now (`$release = '5.3+ (Build: 20261030)'`, `$branch = '503'`), so every
version probe keyed on the old path returns nothing; `4.5` remains built in parallel (`4.5.15+`, same
build date), which is the LTS signature, 🟡 while *"5.3 LTS"* is **unverified**. Open edX **changed its
release-branch convention after Sumac** from `open-release/<name>.master` to `release/<name>`, so a glob
on the old form silently stops at Sumac and never sees `teak`, `ulmo` or `verawood` — all three of which
exist, settling a contradiction two prose sources could not.

🟢 **The licence-metadata question is now settled well enough to act on** (`612-B`): the platform's own
`license.spdx_id` reproduced on grant bytes `68 of 68`, while `p611`'s curated third-party index managed
`1 of 5` on permissive claims. 🟢 **Triage on the platform field; byte-read at the point of shipping;
never trust a curated index.** That is what makes an `848`-address sweep affordable in a week-one
engagement, and it is this pass's most directly billable result.

*policy* and *adoption* figures below are a different grade: each is attributed to a named instrument,
