## Opportunities by region — `p611` (archived)

### North America

🟢 **The buying trigger is now a dated statutory duty on the *district*, not a budget cycle.**

| instrument | date / status | what it obliges |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 🟢 **`4` states** requiring *both* state guidance **and** mandatory district AI-policy adoption — **Maryland (`SB 720`)**, **Idaho**, **Oklahoma**, **Virginia** | 2026 laws | every district must **adopt a policy** and Maryland designates **AI coordinators** |
| Ohio district-policy deadline | `2026-07-01` | carried from `p610`; district policy required |
| 🆕 New York **`A 9190`** | 2026 session, pending | 🔴 would **bar classroom AI below 9th grade**, excepting diagnostics and special education |
| 🆕 California **`AB 1159`** | 2026 session, pending | 🔴 would prohibit **using student data to train AI models** |
| 🆕 Federal **K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act of 2026** | advanced out of House Education & Workforce Cttee | literacy and readiness funding and framework |
| 🆕 District of Columbia OSSE policy | for `2026-27`, non-mandatory | 🔴 **bars AI from grading and from discipline decisions** |
| 🆕 North Carolina DPI | published | detailed K-12 recommendations |
| legislative volume | 2026 session | `134` bills across `31` states; `33`–`35` states with guidance |

🟡 **Adoption runs ahead of policy, and the gap is the opportunity.** Gallup puts **`60 %` of US
teachers** on AI tools; 🔴 **`55 %` of high-school principals say their school has *not* restricted AI
access on the network**; and most US public schools still lack a formal student AI policy.

🟢 **Globant's opening, stated concretely:** the `4` mandatory-adoption states plus Ohio create a
**compliance-artefact** engagement — policy-as-configuration, audit logging, an explainability record
per automated decision, and the two hard exclusions (`no AI grading`, `no AI discipline`) enforced in
code rather than in a handbook. 🟢 **And this pass shelved the reference implementation of the public
-sector shape:** [`bcgov/SIMS`](https://github.com/bcgov/SIMS), `Apache-2.0`, a **provincial** system
of record with a **business-rules engine** built precisely so policy change does not mean a rewrite
(`611-D`). 🔵 A Canadian ministry has published the architecture a US state district consortium needs.

🟡 **Canada is otherwise still thin here** — `bcgov/SIMS` is supply, not policy. No federal or
provincial Canadian education-AI instrument was confirmable this pass; recorded as a gap, not as
coverage.

### EMEA

🟢 **The EU timetable is settled, and this pass closes the date conflict `p610` left implicit.**

| instrument | date | obligation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 🟢 **Regulation (EU) 2026/1744** ("Digital Omnibus on AI") | published OJ 🆕 `2026-07-24`, **applies since** 🆕 `2026-07-27` | amends the AI Act timetable |
| high-risk **stand-alone** Annex III systems (education assessment & placement) | 🟢 `2027-12-02` | full high-risk regime |
| 🆕 AI **embedded in regulated products** | 🆕 `2028-08-02` | full high-risk regime |
| emotion-recognition ban in education | in force `2025-02-02` | 🔴 **prohibited outright** |
| Art. 4 AI-literacy duty | in force | 🔴 schools are **deployers** and owe staff/student literacy |

🔴 **The conflict, named and adjudicated.** Secondary guides still publish *"high-risk education
obligations apply from August 2026."* 🟢 That was the pre-Omnibus date; Reg. (EU) 2026/1744 moved
stand-alone Annex III to `2027-12-02`. 🔵 **Any EMEA engagement working from an August-2026 deadline is
working from a superseded timetable** — and the practical consequence is the opposite of urgency:
there is a ~13-month build window before the regime bites, but the 🔴 **prohibition** and the 🔴 **Art.
4 literacy duty** are live *today*.

🟢 **Gulf — new this pass.** Dubai's **KHDA** has issued explicit responsible-AI guidance to private
schools: a regulator-level instrument in a market that procures through private school groups rather
than ministries. 🟢 **OECD `2026` Digital Education Outlook** adds the strategic line Globant can sell
against: institutions should move **beyond general-purpose AI tools toward purpose-built educational
AI** — which is an argument for a built vertical over a licensed copilot.

🔴 **Africa: third consecutive pass with nothing confirmable on policy, written down rather than left
as silence.** No ministry-level African education-AI instrument was confirmable this pass (`p606` got
Nigeria; `p609`/`p610` and now `p611` got nothing). 🟢 **One supply signal did arrive**, and it is the
first ever here: [`6t6t-cyberg/kifula`](https://github.com/6t6t-cyberg/kifula), `MIT`, Angolan
Portuguese-language academic authoring — 🔴 `0★`, not shippable, but **found by the same
Portuguese-language axis that pays in Brazil** (`611-F`).

### APAC

🟢 **Two instruments new to this base, and one of them names education explicitly.**

| jurisdiction | instrument | status | education exposure |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 🆕 **Vietnam** | **Decision No. `33/2026/QD-TTg`** | issued | 🔴 **education is `1` of `6` high-risk sectors**, naming **automated assessment** and behavioural systems |
| 🆕 **Australia** | **TEQSA** directive | in force | 🔴 **all** higher-education providers must file an **institutional action plan** on generative-AI risk |
| South Korea | **AI Basic Act** | in force `2026-01-22` | education a named high-impact sector; 🟡 **grace period through 2026** — guidance, not penalties |
| Singapore | agentic-AI framework `2026-01-22`; **AI Verify** toolkits | voluntary | toolkit-based assurance |
| Japan | voluntary model | — | no binding education regime |
| Australia / NZ / Malaysia | 🟡 **sectoral** approach | — | "mandatory guardrails" embedded in existing law, **not** standalone AI acts |
| China, UAE | compulsory national AI curricula | in force | the only two |
| India | compulsory from **Class 3** | `2026-27` | curriculum mandate |

🔵 **The structural read, and it is a selling point rather than a complication:** APAC is splitting
into **binding-and-sectoral** (Vietnam, Korea, Australia) versus **voluntary-and-toolkit** (Japan,
Singapore). 🔴 A single regional compliance posture does not exist, so a multi-country APAC rollout
needs **policy as data** — the same rules-engine architecture `bcgov/SIMS` publishes — not a per-country
fork. 🟢 `p610`'s `P610`-series pattern for multi-jurisdiction policy nodes is the right shape; this
pass adds Vietnam and Australia as nodes.

🔴 **The region's own stated gap:** comprehensive GDPR-style data-protection law is **absent in many
APAC countries**, which leaves student data exposed and makes a self-hosted/sovereign deployment the
defensible default. 🟡 Malaysia frames sovereign cloud as *foundational* to AI adoption. 🟡 Commercial
market reports name Google, Microsoft, IBM, Pearson and Byju's as the incumbent players, with China,
India and Japan the largest adopters — 🟡 vendor-report grade, cited as such.

🟢 **Supply placed in-region this pass:** [`RozhakDev/EduPintarIPAS`](https://github.com/RozhakDev/EduPintarIPAS),
`MIT`, a Bahasa-Indonesia primary-science tutor — 🔵 small (`20★`), but it is the local-language,
local-curriculum artefact that an Indonesian engagement starts from rather than translates into.

### LATAM

🟢 **This region moves from anecdote to a `19`-country denominator this pass — the single biggest
intel gain of `p611`.**

| finding | figure | source / date |
| --- | --: | --- |
| 🟢 HEIs using AI in **at least one** area | 🟢 **`87 %`** | UNESCO **IESALC** 2026 study, ~`200` institutions across **`19` countries** |
| 🔴 HEIs with a **formal AI strategy** | 🔴 **`26 %`** | same |
| 🔴 institutions with formal guidelines **and** capacity to apply them | 🔴 **`< 10 %`** | UNESCO |
| 🔴 universities with **no specific AI budget** | 🔴 **`55 %`** | same |
| 🔴 **public** universities with dedicated AI funding | 🔴 **`4 %`** | same |
| 🟢 AI-related regulatory instruments & proposals | 🟢 **`716`** across **`23`** countries/territories, 🟢 **`> 80 %` initiated since 2023** | Universidad de los Andes, 2026 |
| 🟢 Uruguay public-school teachers using AI tools | 🟢 **`75 %`** | **Ceibal**, 2026 |
| 🟡 Brazil school administrators using AI | `53 %` | **Cetic.br**, 2026 |
| 🔴 …Brazilian schools **with guidelines** on that use | 🔴 **`22 %`** | same |
| 🟡 students reporting AI use | `92 %` across `29` institutions | Digital Education Council |
| 🔴 LAC countries teaching **no** early AI adoption | `13 of 19` | CENIA, carried from `p610` |

🟢 **New institutional players, dated:** UNESCO launched the 🆕 **Observatory on Artificial Intelligence
in Education for Latin America and the Caribbean** on 🆕 **`2026-04-14`**, explicitly to guide public
policy; 🆕 **Tecnológico de Monterrey** signed an AI-in-education agreement with UNESCO in **March
2026**; **Ceibal** (Uruguay), **Cetic.br** (Brazil) and **CENIA** (Chile) are the national bodies that
actually hold the data. Latam-GPT spans `8` countries (carried).

🔵 **The opportunity, stated as the arithmetic it is.** `87 %` adoption against `26 %` strategy and
`< 10 %` guidelines-with-capacity is a **governance deficit of roughly `60` percentage points**, and
`55 %` of universities have **no budget line** to close it. 🔴 **So the sellable unit in LATAM is not a
tutor — it is governance-as-a-product**: an AI-use policy instantiated as configuration, an inventory
of models in use, a per-decision audit record, and staff capacity-building. 🟢 It is cheap relative to
a platform build, it is what `716` regulatory instruments across `23` countries will demand, and 🟢 the
`4 %` public-university funding rate argues for a **consortium or ministry-level** sale rather than a
per-institution one.

🔴 **LATAM's repository-level supply nil, measured rather than asserted.** The four Iberian-language
supply axes (`educacao inteligencia artificial`, `educacion inteligencia artificial agente`,
`plataforma educativa open source`, `agente educativo llm`) returned 🔴 **`95` addresses new to this
base and `0` shippable-shaped** (`≥ 10★` and permissive). 🟡 Three of the `0★` finds are nonetheless
the *right shape* and are named on `agents/trending.md` for a future pass:
`CarlosArmijos1/tutor-tecnico-agentes` (LangGraph Socratic tutor), `fabriciojunio/permaneia` (RAG +
fuzzy dropout-risk panel) and `Belles-hub/UpLearn` (adaptive, **inclusive**-education framing).
🔵 **This is an informed gap: the region's demand is documented at `19`-country scale while its
permissive open-source supply is, on this evidence, still pre-shippable.**

### Global

🟡 **The cross-regional shift every source agrees on is governance, not capability.** 1EdTech frames
2026 as **experimentation → governance** (policies, data boundaries, oversight); HolonIQ expects
"selective" acceleration gated on ethics and proven instructional benefit; Campus Technology's panel
expects AI to be **embedded across admissions, advising, student services, accessibility and
administration** rather than piloted alongside them. 🟢 All three are consistent with the regional
evidence above: 🔴 **every region now has a dated obligation or a measured governance deficit, and none
has a capability shortage.**

🟡 **Two further global signals, cited at their grade:** a shift toward **skills-based credentialing**
(1EdTech) as employer expectations move; and 🟡 **market consolidation toward all-in-one,
education-first AI platforms** — vendor-blog grade, and 🔵 notable mainly because it is the commercial
counter-argument to the composable, permissive approach these shelves exist to support. 🟡 Microsoft's
2026 figure of `92 %` AI use "for school" with training lagging is the global version of the
LATAM arithmetic.

🔴 **Unchanged and still the honest caveat:** `598-G`. Market *size* here is snippet-grade, the firms
disagree `> 3×`, and no primary source is reachable from this host.

### EMEA

🟢 **The compliance calendar is the roadmap, and it is now confirmed from two independent channels.**
**Regulation (EU) 2026/1744** defers education **high-risk** duties to **`2027-12-02`**. Already in
force: the **emotion-recognition ban in education** since **`2025-02-02`** (Art. 5(1)(f)), and
**transparency duties** since **`2026-08-02`**. Schools are **deployers**, carrying an **Article 4
AI-literacy** duty. High-risk scope is named explicitly: admissions and access, evaluating learning
outcomes, assigning level of education, and **monitoring students during tests**.

| opportunity | starting point | why now |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 🟢 **Build the four high-risk functions correctly before Dec 2027** | `Sion612/rubrictrail` (Apache-2.0) — rubric→plan→**evidence**→human check | The Act demands human oversight and logging. A tool whose design output *is* an evidence trail maps onto the obligation instead of being retrofitted to it |
| 🟢 **Art. 4 AI-literacy delivery** | `CogitoNTNU/TutorAI` (MIT, NTNU) + `dmoshehun-prog/learn-from-materials` (MIT) | The literacy duty is live **now**, unlike the high-risk duties — it is the nearest-term billable obligation in the region |
| 🟢 **Proctoring, re-scoped** | 🔴 deliberately **not** emotion inference | Exam monitoring is high-risk and emotion inference is **banned outright**. The defensible product is integrity signals without affect recognition — a redesign, and the Act is what forces it |
| 🟢 **Permissive deployment layer for EU data residency** | `Azure/Moodle` (MIT) + `moodlehq/moodleapp` (Apache-2.0) | Sovereign-tenancy Moodle with exactly **one** copyleft layer (the core), now that mobile and IaC are shelved permissive |
| 🔴 **Declared gap** | — | No country-level adoption rates found, and sources disagree on what the Digital Omnibus changed. **Africa returned nothing confirmable for the second consecutive pass** |

### APAC

🟢 **Two national mandates and one agentic-governance first.** **China** and the **UAE** are the only
states running **compulsory national AI curricula** (since 2025-26). **India** makes AI and
computational thinking **compulsory from Class 3 in 2026-27**, with IT Rules synthetic-content
labelling effective **`2026-02-20`**. **Singapore** launched an **agentic-AI** governance framework on
**`2026-01-22`** — voluntary, and the first in the region aimed at agents rather than models.

| opportunity | starting point | why now |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 🟢 **Curriculum-delivery capacity at national scale** | `Haozhe-Xing/agent_learning` (MIT) + `cabbage2000-lab/textbook-writer-skills` (MIT) | A Class-3 mandate across India needs material and teacher capacity, not models. This is a content-production problem with permissive tooling |
| 🟢 **Institutional handbook assistants, bilingual** | `BenyRonald77/uajy-academic-rag-chatbot` (MIT, Indonesia) | 🟢 Already **region-placed and bilingual** (Bahasa Indonesia / English), hybrid FAISS+BM25 behind a swappable gateway — a template for ASEAN institutions, not a port |
| 🟢 **Agentic governance as a deliverable** | `Sion612/rubrictrail` (Apache-2.0, ships `zh-CN`) | Singapore's framework is the region's first agent-specific instrument. Being able to evidence agent behaviour is the thing being asked for |
| 🟢 **Provenance labelling, the portable obligation** | — | India `2026-02-20`, China since `2025-09-01`, EU since `2026-08-02`: 🟢 **three unrelated legal traditions converge on labelling** (`T607-1`). The most region-portable build target in the sector |
| 🔴 **Declared gap** | — | **No education-specific policy detail for Japan** beyond a penalty-free statute |

### LATAM

🟢 **The governance inversion is now a measured ratio, and the repository-level nil is broken twice.**
`>50 %` of teachers in **Chile** and **Brazil** use AI tools against `<10 %` of institutions holding
guidelines — **same source, both sides, `>5×`** (`T607-2`). **CENIA**: `13 of 19` LAC countries do not
teach early AI adoption in schools. The Latin American AI Index 2026 leading group is **Brazil, Chile,
Uruguay**; only **Brazil and Colombia** allocate budget inside their national instruments.
**Latam-GPT** spans `30+` institutions across `8` countries.

| opportunity | starting point | why now |

A Brazilian assessment engagement no longer rests on a single repository |

## 🆕 `p609` — reads `2026-11-02` — market map and regional placement

### 🔴 Source-quality warning, stated before the numbers

The regional legs ran at the mandated shapes, once globally and once per region. They returned
**market-research and policy material, and zero repository-level findings** — the repository side of
this pass came from a different channel entirely (`609`, `repos/trending.md`). Two defects in what
they did return must be stated before any figure is used:

🔴 **The 2026 market-size estimates do not agree, and not within a rounding margin.** For the same
year, the same segment: **`$3.7 B`** (one house, rising to `$14.1 B` by 2033), **`~$11 B`** (another),
**`$6.4 B` for 2025** (a third). This base has carried `$9.58 B → $42.48 B (2030, CAGR 41.5 %)` since
`p65`. 🔵 **The spread is roughly 3×, which means the segment definition is doing the work, not the
measurement.** Every figure below is attributed; none is averaged, because averaging incompatible
definitions manufactures a number nobody measured.

🔴 **One source states the EU AI Act "entered into force progressively from August 2026."** That is
wrong and checkably so: the Act entered into force **2024-08-01**, with prohibited practices and
AI-literacy obligations applying from **2025-02-02** — dates two other sources in the same harvest
state correctly. 🔵 Recorded because it is a *date* error in a *regulatory* claim, the class most
likely to be copied into a client deck, and because it demonstrates the aggregator tier these legs
reach is not reliable on its own terms.

### Market frame carried and amended

| segment | figure | source class | status here |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AI in education, global 2026 | `$9.58 B → $42.48 B` by 2030, CAGR `41.5 %` | report seller, carried since `p65` | 🟡 **carried, now contested** — see the spread above |
| AI in education, global 2026 | `$3.7 B → $14.1 B` by 2033 | report seller | 🔴 incompatible definition |
| AI in education, global 2026 | `~$11 B` | analysis blog | 🔴 incompatible definition |
| K-12 share of segment, 2026 | `~34 %` — largest single segment | report seller | 🟡 directionally consistent across two sources |

## Opportunities by region — `p609` (archived)

### North America

🔵 **The structural fact is regulatory volume, not market size.** As of **March 2026**, policymakers
were tracking **`134` bills across `31` states** touching AI in education — privacy, classroom
restrictions, curriculum. The **US Department of Education** released **`$169 M`** in FIPSE awards
including support for responsible AI use (Q1 2026), and **COPPA** alignment is reported as the gate
that lets child-facing products expand at all.

🟡 Regional share estimates conflict: `36 %` of the global segment at `$3.68 B` (2026) from one house,
`38 %` share (2025) from another, `~$951 M` regional (2024) from a third, and a `41.7 %` forecast
*growth* figure from a fourth — 🔴 note that the last is a growth rate being reported alongside share
figures, which is how a growth number becomes a market size in a deck. Sources agree only on rank:
the region leads.

🟢 **Where the opportunity actually sits for a studio:** `134` bills in `31` states means **no single
compliance target**, so per-state configurability is the product requirement, not a feature. The
rows this pass promoted that serve it directly: [`datakind/student-success-tool`](https://github.com/datakind/student-success-tool)
(held, MIT) for institutional risk modelling, and 🆕 [`shivanireddyk/tutoreval`](https://github.com/shivanireddyk/tutoreval)
— **deterministic, no network calls, no API key** — which is the only eval instrument on this base
that can run **inside** a district's boundary without a data-sharing review.

🔴 **Stated gap:** both legs returned essentially **no named K-12 or higher-ed vendors** — the only
companies named anywhere in the harvest were two children's-robot makers. A market map of this region
cannot be built from these legs.

### EMEA

🟢 **This is the region where the policy layer is concrete and dated.** The **European Commission**
updated its **ethical guidelines on AI in education** (9 June) and signed **three Advanced Digital
Skills grant agreements worth `€25.4 M`**. The **UK** held its first **AI Adoption Summit** (8 June),
committing **`£200 M+`**, of which **`£100 M`** expands **Bridge AI**. The **EU AI Act** is in force
from **2024-08-01**, with prohibited practices and **AI-literacy obligations** live since
**2025-02-02** — the literacy obligation is the one that lands on education providers directly.

🟢 **New permissive EMEA holder this pass:** [`baptiste-alonso/knowledge-tracing-benchmark`](https://github.com/baptiste-alonso/knowledge-tracing-benchmark)
— **MIT** (the API called it `NOASSERTION`; see `609-F`) — seven **interpretable** KT models on
`110,101` real interactions from **Cleverlearn**, a French EdTech firm, run as a **CentraleSupélec**
project course. 🔵 **Interpretability was the client's requirement**, which is why DKT was excluded by
design. That is the EU-AI-Act posture expressed as an architecture choice rather than a compliance
memo, and it is the most directly reusable EMEA artifact this base holds for a regulated engagement.

🟡 Also EMEA, carried from `p608` and still the cleanest fit for an exam-integrity engagement:
[`Bradwave/obfusca`](https://github.com/Bradwave/obfusca) — **MIT**, `100 %` in-browser, GDPR-safe PDF
exam anonymiser with nothing uploaded.

🔴 **Stated gap:** no 2026 school- or university-level AI **adoption rate** for the region, and
nothing on how the AI Act **classifies** education systems by risk tier. The one adoption figure in
the harvest (`56 %` of EMEA CEOs report readiness vs `48 %` Americas, `45 %` APJ) is 🔴 **enterprise,
undated, and vendor-published** — it is not an education figure and is not used here.

### APAC

🟢 **Fastest-growing region on the one estimate available:** `$591.6 M` (2024) → `$1,848.1 M` (2029),
**CAGR `20.9 %`** — 🟡 a single vendor-style estimate, and 🔵 note it implies a *far* lower growth
rate than the `41.5 %` global CAGR this base carries, which is a second reason to distrust the global
figure rather than to revise the regional one.

🔵 **Regulatory shape: fragmentation that is now enforced rather than drafted.** At least **`16`
jurisdictions** have AI guidance or regulation, some binding, some principles-only; early 2026 is
characterised as the shift **from drafting to active enforcement**, and Forrester's read is that a
common APAC-wide framework *"will remain a distant dream."* 🟢 **The Philippines' Department of
Education partnered with Google to certify more than one million teachers** — the largest single
teacher-certification commitment in the harvest, in any region.

🟢 **Two new permissive APAC holders this pass**, both with real holder evidence rather than a username
inference: [`huynhanhkhiem-dms/pooled-auc-knowledge-tracing`](https://github.com/huynhanhkhiem-dms/pooled-auc-knowledge-tracing)
(**MIT**, **Ton Duc Thang University**, Vietnam, ORCID in README) and
[`GEMLabHKU/DAS2`](https://github.com/GEMLabHKU/DAS2) (**MIT**, placed on org-login-plus-ICCE-venue
evidence, stated as such in `agents/top.md`).

🟢 **Opportunity:** `16` jurisdictions moving to enforcement is the **policy-routing** engagement —
`P6`'s multi-jurisdiction pattern on the technology shelf, applied to education. The held
[`hyunbinseo/neis-school-info`](https://github.com/hyunbinseo/neis-school-info) (**MIT**, Korea's
**NEIS** national school information system) remains the reference for official-data-to-agent plumbing
that cannot be replaced by a generic connector.

### LATAM

🟢 **The repository-level nil is broken.** `p608` recorded a repository-level nil for LATAM. This pass
promotes [`tcheld0n/CorrijAI`](https://github.com/tcheld0n/CorrijAI) — **MIT**, `1,338 KB` — an
**ENEM** essay grader scoring `C1`–`C5` with reasoned pedagogical feedback anchored in the *Cartilha
do Participante*, trained/evaluated on **Essay-BR**, measured against a supervised `HistGB` baseline
on the same 65 essays (baseline QWK `0.750`, calibrated agent `0.731`), served over FastAPI with a
Streamlit demo. 🔵 It joins the held [`Mcp-Brasil/mcp-brasil`](https://github.com/Mcp-Brasil/mcp-brasil)
(**MIT**, `1,849★`) and [`dfdb76/bncc-mcp`](https://github.com/dfdb76/bncc-mcp) (**MIT**, code only),
so the region now has **curriculum (BNCC) → assessment (ENEM) → delivery** covered permissively.

🔵 **Demand and constraint, from the better sources in this harvest.** A **UNU** working paper surveys
**`200` higher-education institutions across `19` countries** (fieldwork Aug–Oct 2025) on AI use in
teaching, research, administration and governance; the **IDB**'s *fAIr Tech Radar* describes adoption
advancing *"in a context marked by structural gaps, developing regulatory frameworks and heterogeneous
digital capabilities."* **UNESCO** warns of a severe regional **teacher shortage**. Investment in
regional AI startups is projected at **`$2.5 B`** by end-2026. `Ednova` (Chile) is the one edtech
company named.

🔴 **Regulatory position:** *"no Latin American country currently has a fully mature, harmonized AI
regime across all sectors"* (Baker McKenzie), with education named as a sector where the rules matter.
Several commentaries describe the risk as *"a patchwork of pilot policies, vulnerable to being tossed
out with each new administration."*

🔴 **Adoption figures refused.** The harvest offered `70 %`, `85 %` and `47 %` adoption for
overlapping populations in the same region, all from low-quality aggregators, none with a stated
denominator. 🔵 They are irreconcilable and none is carried. The UNU and IDB studies are the only
LATAM sources here with a visible method, and the honest regional statement is that **connectivity and
device access remain the binding constraint**, which is why `Kolibri`'s offline tier (held, MIT) still
matters more here than any model choice.

### Global

🟢 **The cross-regional movement this pass is the `609-B` cluster**: five independent groups — in
Hong Kong, Vietnam, France, the US and one unplaced — shipped instruments for **evaluating a tutor
against a student who is not idealised**, within roughly eight weeks of each other, all permissive.
🔵 That is a market signal independent of any report seller: the field has moved from *building*
tutors to *measuring whether they teach*, and the measuring layer is open source.

🟡 Direction-of-travel claims that recur across regions and are consistent with each other: AI tutors
moving **inside** existing LMS environments rather than alongside them; administrative and
back-office use growing faster than instructional use; and the stated pressure shifting from
*"how do we stop cheating"* to pedagogy and cost justification. 🔵 The **OECD Digital Education
Outlook 2026** is cited for the first two in several places, but 🔴 the report itself was not
reachable from this session (`609-G`), so it is recorded as **reported, not read**.

## 🆕 `p608` — reads `2026-11-02` — market map and regional placement

### 🔴 Source-quality warning, stated before the numbers

Every market **size** figure below comes from vendor research pages or compliance-consultancy
blogs, and they disagree with each other by more than 2× on the same region in the same year.
They are recorded as a **range with the disagreement visible**, never as a point estimate.
🔴 **`arxiv.org` does not resolve from this session** (`ENOTFOUND`) and
`digital-skills-jobs.europa.eu` likewise, so the academic and EU-portal tiers could not be read
directly this pass; OECD findings below are from secondary summaries and are labelled as such.

### Key players, by layer

| layer | who actually shows up | note |

| hyperscaler / model | Microsoft, Google, Anthropic, OpenAI | Google added AI to **Google Workspace for Education**; a **Claude for teachers** release is reported for **July 2026** |
| incumbent publisher / platform | Pearson, Coursera, Duolingo, IBM | the names that recur across every regional report |
| regional edtech | Alef Education (MEA), MagicSchool, Squirrel AI, Knewton | named inconsistently across reports |
| national public infrastructure | **DIKSHA** (India, NCERT+CIET), SUNY (US), France Université Numérique | 🔵 the layer most relevant to a Globant public-sector engagement |
| permissive open source | the `agents/top.md` and `repos/foundations.md` shelves | 🔴 **no LATAM-origin row** — see below |

| scope | figures found | spread |
| --- | --- | --- |

| global, 2030 | `$53B+` | single-source projection |

🔴 One vendor claim — *"AI agents will take over 80 % of routine educational labour"* — is an
unsupported marketing projection and is recorded here only so it is not mistaken for a finding.

## Opportunities by region — `p608` (archived)

### North America

- **Regulatory driver:** the legislature, not the regulator. **`134` AI-in-education bills across
  `31` states** in the 2026 session, concentrated on data privacy, classroom-use limits and
  curriculum. **No comprehensive federal statute**; executive orders promote AI literacy.
- **Dated compliance work that is already billable:** North Carolina **Session Law 2026-41** —
  state model policy by **2026-12-31**, local school-unit policies by **2027-06-30**, specified
  teacher professional development by **2028-06-30**. Maryland **SB 720** would require aligned
  district policies plus a designated **AI coordinator** per district. NYC runs a preliminary
  traffic-light framework; **NYSED has issued no statewide K-12 framework**.
- **Higher ed is bifurcating:** **SUNY** requires AI literacy inside general education for
  incoming undergraduates from **fall 2026**; **UC Berkeley School of Law prohibits** generative AI
  for conceptualising, outlining, drafting, revising or editing most graded work. 🔵 The same
  engagement cannot be sold to both — segment on policy posture, not institution size.
- **Accessibility is a hard date:** updated **ADA** web-accessibility obligations for large public
  institutions land **April 2026**, which is pulling AI-enabled remediation into scope.
- **Adoption:** ~**60 %** of US teachers report using AI tools, while **most US public schools
  still lack a formal student AI policy** — the gap *is* the offer.
- 🔴 **Opportunity, placed:** policy-to-practice delivery — model policy → district policy →
  teacher PD → audit trail, on the NC/MD dates. 🔴 **But note:** NA produced **`0` new permissive
  repositories** this pass. An NA engagement is served by APAC- and EMEA-origin code.

### EMEA

- **The date moved, and this is the single most important EMEA fact of the pass.** The EU AI Act's
  **Annex III high-risk** obligations for education (admissions, assessment, proctoring) were
  rescheduled from **2026-08-02** to **2027-12-02** by the **Digital Omnibus, Regulation (EU)
  2026/1744** (reported in force **2026-07-27**). ⚠️ Dates vary between secondary sources —
  **verify against the Official Journal before contracting on them.**
- **What did *not* move:** chatbot **transparency** obligations from **August 2026**; the
  **emotion-recognition ban in education** (Art. 5(1)(f)) in force since **February 2025**.
- **Schools are "deployers"** and stay responsible for third-party AI tools — liability does not
  transfer to the vendor. 🔵 That is the commercial hook: deployer-side compliance tooling.
- **Market:** Europe `$1.2–2.1B` (2026, publisher-dependent); **Germany** repeatedly the largest
  national market. ~**70 %** of institutions in Europe and North America have or are developing
  guidance.
- 🟢 **Opportunity, placed:** an 18-month runway to **2027-12-02** is long enough to build
  conformity properly and short enough to sell now — assessment and proctoring systems with
  documented human oversight, plus the **GDPR-safe local anonymisation** this pass shelved
  ([`Bradwave/obfusca`](https://github.com/Bradwave/obfusca), MIT) and the **exam-mode AI lockout**
  pattern ([`yashvikram30/german-course`](https://github.com/yashvikram30/german-course), MIT).
- 🔴 **Africa sub-gap, now for a named reason:** `abaridodo/ai4africa` and `abaridodo/makaranta`
  were found and byte-read — **both carry no license file**. The African education-AI repository
  tier is no longer *unfound*; it is **found and unusable**, which is a contactable problem.

### APAC

- **Curriculum mandates are live, not proposed.** **China** and the **UAE** are the only nations
  running **compulsory national AI curricula** since the 2025-26 school year. **India** plans AI
  and computational thinking as compulsory subjects **from Class 3, beginning 2026-27**.
- **Regulatory split:** binding regimes in **China**, **South Korea**, **Vietnam**; guidance-led in
  **Japan**, **Singapore**, **Hong Kong**, **Australia**, **India**. China added the
  **Anthropomorphic Services Measures** (issued **2026-04-10**, effective **2026-07-15**) atop the
  2022 Algorithm Recommendation and 2023 Deep Synthesis regulations. India's amended **IT Rules**
  on synthetic-content labelling took effect **2026-02-20**; **no dedicated AI statute**. Japan's
  **AI Promotion Act** (passed **2025-05-28**) is promotional, and its school guidance warns
  against entering personal or sensitive data into generative systems.
- **Public opinion, Ipsos Education Monitor 2026** — support for *banning* AI in schools:
  India **26 %**, Thailand **25 %**, Singapore **28 %**, Japan **29 %**, Malaysia **29 %**.
  🔵 Japan rose from **21 % → 29 %**; Singapore fell. Permission is not static.
- **Public infrastructure:** **DIKSHA** (NCERT + CIET) is open-source, curriculum-linked and
  multilingual, but its AI surface is modest — in-video keyword search and read-aloud for visually
  impaired learners. **NCERT** has AI content in Class XI CS/IP; **YuvAi** (MeitY + AICTE) promotes
  open-source AI. 🔴 India has **no comprehensive education-specific AI policy**.
- 🟢 **Opportunity, placed — and APAC is where the code is.** **`5` of this pass's `11` new
  permissive rows are APAC-held**, two of them bound to a *named national curriculum*
  (人教版 Grade 7 biology; SEBA/AHSEC Assam) and two to **low-resource languages** (Taiwanese
  Hokkien, Assamese). The compulsory-curriculum mandates plus a permissive, curriculum-bound code
  base is the strongest match between demand and supply anywhere on this shelf.

### LATAM

- 🔴 **Repository-level nil, stated.** Legs were run in English, Spanish and Portuguese, plus `81`
  rows enumerated across four topic pages: **no LATAM-held permissive education-AI repository was
  found.** The nearest lead was a Brazilian TypeScript/Next.js **municipal school-management** tool
  tagged LGPD (updated 2026-08-26) with **no AI layer**. Mexico: nothing. Argentina: nothing.
  🔵 **Eighth consecutive pass with regulation but no code.** This is a measured gap, not coverage.
- **Regulation is proliferating faster than it is converging.** An August 2026 study counts
  **`212` instruments in Brazil** and **`126` in Mexico** — second in the region. Commentary from
  October 2026 warns that legislating fast is not legislating well.
- **Mexico:** **no general AI law.** Progress is sectoral — the **State of México** reformed
  **Article 61 of its Ley de Educación in April 2026** to promote gradual, responsible AI use in
  upper-secondary and higher education. Academic sources confirm university AI use is rising with
  **no national mandatory guidance**.
- **Brazil:** **PL 2338/2023** — approved by the **Senate in December 2024**, reported still
  pending in the **Câmara dos Deputados**. 🔴 **Sources contradict each other** on whether it is in
  force; one describes it as law since 2025. **Do not contract on this status without checking the
  Senado/Câmara record.**
- ⚠️ A UNESCO-attributed claim that the region has the **highest generative-AI adoption among
  university students** while only a minority of institutions have an approved internal
  regulation **could not be verified** against the original report this pass.
- 🟢 **Opportunity, placed — and it is a composition offer, not a localisation one.** Because no
  LATAM-origin asset exists, a LATAM engagement must wire **APAC- and EMEA-origin permissive code**
  against **LATAM curriculum and LATAM law**: the curriculum-bound tutor pattern
  (`sproutask`, MIT) retargeted from 人教版 to **BNCC**, the deterministic-grading pattern
  (`axomiya-study-companion`, MIT) retargeted from SEBA/AHSEC to **ENEM/SEP**, and institutional
  AI-use regulations drafted where the UNESCO-reported adoption/rule gap is widest. 🔵 The
  high-adoption / low-governance combination is the clearest unserved demand in this base.

### Global

- **The OECD result that should reframe every pitch.** *Digital Education Outlook 2026*:
  advantages from AI use **disappear or reverse when AI access is removed**, with students who had
  prior AI access performing **up to 17 % worse** than peers who never had it. **TALIS 2024:**
  **37 %** of lower-secondary teachers used AI in their work; **72 %** of teachers report academic
  integrity concerns. The report's framing is *technology ≠ transformation*.
  ⚠️ Secondary summaries; the full PDF could not be read from this session.
- 🟢 **What it implies commercially:** durable-learning evidence becomes the product. The rows this
  pass shelved that *separate recorded evidence from message counts*
  ([`sproutask`](https://github.com/HaoyanZhang123/sproutask)), *refuse to hand over answers*
  ([`Si-Yuan-agent`](https://github.com/1Zonghao/Si-Yuan-agent)), *make AI use visible to the
  teacher* ([`wildmind-ai-for-education`](https://github.com/dlealvilla/wildmind-ai-for-education))
  and *switch the tutor off during exams*
  ([`german-course`](https://github.com/yashvikram30/german-course)) are the architectural answer
  to a `-17 %` finding — and all four are **MIT**.
- 🔴 **The measurement tier is still missing.** `10 of 23` European countries had formally
  incorporated generative AI into education strategy (2025 study), **`134`** US bills, **`212`**
  Brazilian instruments — and this base still holds **no permissive, dominant evaluation framework
  for education agents**, because the best candidate found
  ([`wxxxqcy/ai-learning-assistant`](https://github.com/wxxxqcy/ai-learning-assistant)) **ships no
  license file**. Everyone is regulating outcomes nobody has a standard instrument to measure.

## 🆕 `p607` — `2026-11-02` — **🟢 THE NATIONAL-QUERY CHANNEL IS NOW PROVEN ON ALL FOUR REGIONS: LATAM WAS THE ONE `606-H` NAMED AS UNTESTED AND IT RETURNED A DATED UNESCO REGIONAL INSTRUMENT PLUS A TEACHER-VS-INSTITUTION GOVERNANCE GAP WITH NUMBERS ON BOTH SIDES. 🟢 NORTH AMERICA RETURNED ITS REGULATORY PICTURE AT **SUB-NATIONAL** RESOLUTION — `134` BILLS ACROSS `31` STATES, AND `4` STATES WITH A *MANDATORY DISTRICT POLICY* DUTY. 🟢 EMEA NAMED THE ACTUAL INSTRUMENT BEHIND `T606-1`: **REGULATION (EU) 2026/1744**. 🔴 AFRICA RETURNED NOTHING CONFIRMABLE AND IS WRITTEN DOWN AS A GAP. 🔴 `598-G` STANDS — NINTH PASS OPEN.**

### 🔴 Read this before any figure on this page — `598-G`, ninth pass open

🔴 Unchanged. No market-size number on this page is primary-sourced: the primary hosts
(`unesco.org`, `oecd.org`, `iadb.org`) are blocked at the egress proxy for this session, and the
fabricated-host control returns the same code, so the channel does not even separate *blocked* from
*absent*. 🔴 The firms disagree by `4×` on the same year. **Quote a range with the caveat attached,
never a point estimate. Operator request, not a research step.**

🟢 **The discipline that does hold:** every **supply**-side figure on this page and the three sell
shelves is byte-read with a fabricated control (`28 of 28` at `404 14 B` this pass). The asymmetry is
the honest statement of what this base knows — **its repository evidence is first-hand and its market
evidence is not.**

### 🟢 `607-F` — the leg table, with the shape axis, and a region that returned nothing

| leg | shape | substantive tokens | new to this base | verdict |
| --- | --- | --: | --: | --- |
| 🔴 `top open source AI agents education 2026 github MIT` | mandated, global | `7` | 🔴 **`0`** | 🔴 nil — every token held, `hermes-agent` included (`607-A`) |

🔴 **Thirty-seventh consecutive global-leg nil, and the fourth regional success in a row.** 🔵 The
conclusion `p603` drew as trend `77` is now not arguable: **the mandated global shapes are a
regression test; the regional and national shapes are the discovery channel.** The run prompt
mandates both, and only one of them finds anything.

🔴 **The Africa nil, stated rather than left silent.** The EMEA leg returned the Gulf (UAE, Dubai
KHDA) and the EU, and *nothing specific on African AI-in-education regulation or named players*.

where this base holds a promoted platform row (`Tangerine`, `606-I`) and a national gap analysis that
names it. **The supply evidence for Africa is ahead of the demand evidence, which is the reverse of
every other region here.**

## Opportunities by region — `p607` (archived)

### North America

🟢 **The 2026 picture is sub-national and it has hardened from guidance into duty — unevenly, and the
unevenness is the engagement.**

| signal | detail | grade |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 🟢 Legislative volume | **`134` bills** on AI in education introduced across **`31` states**, concentrated on data privacy, classroom restriction and curriculum integration | 🟡 snippet |
| 🟢 **Mandatory duty** | **`4` states** with 2026 laws requiring **both** state guidance **and** mandatory district-level AI policy adoption: 🟢 **Maryland, Idaho, Oklahoma, Virginia** | 🟡 snippet |
| 🟢 Restriction by grade | **New York A 9190** — would limit classroom AI to **grade 9 and above**, excepting diagnostics and special-education interventions | 🟡 snippet |
| 🟢 **Prohibited-use lists appear** | **D.C. OSSE** non-mandatory policy for 2026-27 **bans AI from grading, discipline, IEP decisions and teacher evaluation** | 🟡 snippet |
| 🟢 Federal | **K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act of 2026** advanced by the House Education and Workforce Committee | 🟡 snippet |
| 🟢 Adoption | **Gallup: `60 %` of US teachers use AI tools** | 🟡 snippet |
| 🔴 Governance lag | Most US public schools still have **no formal student AI policy** (Child Trends / Dept. of Education) | 🟡 snippet |
| 🟢 Higher ed | **`70 %`** of institutions in Europe **and** North America have or are developing guidance (UNESCO) | 🟡 snippet |
| 🟡 Players | State education departments (MD, NY, NC, MO, AK), NYC Public Schools (traffic-light framework), NEA / AFT / CTA, SREB AI Commission, ECS (task forces in ≥`29` states); 🔴 a North Carolina **`$10 M`** Khan Academy earmark drew public controversy | 🟡 snippet |

🟢 **Opportunity:** the four mandatory-policy states create a **compliance artefact a district must
produce and cannot write itself**, and D.C.'s prohibited-use list shows the shape it takes — a
named list of decisions AI may not make. 🟢 That is a deliverable, not a pilot. 🔵 Concrete assets
this base now holds for it: [`datakind/student-success-tool`](https://github.com/datakind/student-success-tool)
(MIT, branch `develop`) for the analytics side with a **human-release** gate, and
[`pdg6/bc-curriculum-mcp-server`](https://github.com/pdg6/bc-curriculum-mcp-server) (MIT) as the
North American precedent for binding an agent to an official curriculum rather than to a model's
memory.

🔴 **Measurement owed:** North America has been researched as a **demand** leg only. The national/
sub-national shape has never been run here as a **supply** leg, which is the one variation left
untested after `607-E` (`605-F` APAC, `606-H` EMEA, `607-E` LATAM all found addresses that way).

🟢 **The instrument behind `T606-1` now has a number, and the near deadline is the one nobody is
quoting.**

| signal | detail | grade |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 🟢 **The regulation, named** | 🟢 **Regulation (EU) 2026/1744** — the *Digital Omnibus* amendment to the AI Act, reported in force **late July 2026** | 🟡 snippet |
| 🟢 High-risk clock | Stand-alone **Annex III** high-risk systems — which is where learner evaluation and admissions influence sit — apply from 🟢 **`2 December 2027`** | 🟡 snippet |
| 🔴 Enforcement already live | The **AI Office and national authorities** began enforcement from 🔴 **`2 August 2026`** — i.e. *governance* obligations are in force now even though *high-risk* duties are not | 🟡 snippet |
| 🔴 Sources conflict | A vendor guide still states high-risk education duties apply from **August 2026**. 🔴 That contradicts the omnibus dates. **Treat the timeline as unsettled and verify against the Official Journal** — which `598-G` blocks this session from doing | 🔴 **conflict, unresolved** |

🟢 **Opportunity:** the `2 Aug 2026` / `2 Dec 2027` split is the sellable fact. An institution is
**already** inside the enforcement regime for governance while its high-risk conformity deadline is
`13` months out — which is exactly enough time for a conformity programme and not enough to start
late. 🔵 This base holds the permissive substrate for it: the QTI tier (`607-I`) gives an assessment
path with an auditable item format, and
[`DFE-Digital/education-benchmarking-and-insights`](https://github.com/DFE-Digital/education-benchmarking-and-insights)
(MIT, pushed `2026-11-01`) is a national ministry's own code as a reference for what a public-sector
EMEA buyer considers acceptable.

framing.**

🟢 **Opportunity:** two of the region's largest systems now impose **content provenance** duties with
dated deadlines (India `20 Feb 2026`, China since `1 Sep 2025`), and 🟢 **that is the same obligation
the EU converges on** — see `T607-2`. A provenance-labelling layer built once is sellable into both
regions and into EMEA. 🔵 `hyunbinseo/neis-school-info` (MIT, promoted this pass) is the Korean
national-data entry point for the market that went mandatory first.

🟡 **Honest counterweight:** `≤31 %` ban support is low, but Japan's `+8` point move is the only
*trend* in the data, and it runs toward restriction. One year of one survey is not a direction.

🟢 **Opportunity, and it is the sharpest on this page:** `>50 %` teacher use against `<10 %`

different and more urgent sale. 🟢 Chile's self-classification model makes the risk assessment itself
a deliverable the institution must author.

from*. 🔵 That is a build, it is region-portable, and it is the subject of `T607-2`.

## Opportunities by region — `p606` (archived)

### North America

🟢 **The regulatory layer moved from guidance to statute in 2026, unevenly, and that unevenness is
the engagement.**

| signal | detail |
| --- | --- |
| 🟢 State statute | **Idaho SB 1227** signed March 2026 — directs a statewide K-12 AI framework covering privacy, procurement safeguards and AI-literacy standards |
| 🟢 State statute | **Maryland** — law effective **1 June 2026**, state education department must publish AI guidelines that districts adapt |
| 🟡 Pending | **New York A 9190** would limit direct student AI use to grade `9`+, with diagnostic and special-education exceptions |
| 🟡 Federal | **K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act of 2026** introduced April 2026, advanced by House committee, 🔴 **not passed** |
| 🔴 Counts disagree | ~`34` states + DC + PR have issued guidance per one tracker; *">28 states + DC"* per Education Commission of the States. `4` states mandate district AI policies |
| 🟢 Backstop | FERPA + COPPA remain the operative legal constraints |

🟢 **Opportunity:** a **procurement-and-compliance layer**, not a tutor. `50` jurisdictions with
divergent, newly-statutory requirements and districts that must adapt state guidance locally is a
configuration problem. Globant's fit is the policy-to-control mapping plus self-hosted deployment so
student data never leaves the district boundary.

🔵 Adoption figures circulating (`54 %` of K-12 students, `92 %` of university students) are
secondary-benchmark grade — directionally useful, methodology unverified.

### EMEA

🟢 **The most load-bearing date on this page changed, and stale guides still have the old one.**

| signal | detail |
| --- | --- |
| 🟢 **Deadline moved** | **Digital Omnibus, Regulation (EU) 2026/1744** moved stand-alone **high-risk education** AI obligations from `2 Aug 2026` → 🟢 **`2 Dec 2027`**. 🔴 Guides still citing August 2026 predate this |
| 🟢 In force now | **Art. 50 transparency** duties apply from **`2 Aug 2026`**; systems already on the market have until **`2 Dec 2026`** to add machine-readable marking of generated content |
| 🟢 In force since | **Emotion-recognition ban in education** — applies since **`2 Feb 2025`** |
| 🟡 Softened | The **AI-literacy duty** since July 2026 requires *measures to support* staff AI literacy, not a guaranteed level |
| 🟢 Scope | High-risk covers admission/placement decisions, evaluating learning outcomes, assessing attainable education level, and exam proctoring. **Schools are deployers** — including when the vendor is a third party, and including non-EU vendors reaching EU learners |
| 🟢 **UK (England)** | DfE *Generative AI in education* policy paper; **Product Safety Standards updated Jan 2026**; support modules **updated May 2026**; 🟢 **KCSIE 2026 makes generative AI part of statutory safeguarding from 1 Sept 2026**. 🔵 Devolved — England only; DfE guidance itself is **non-statutory** |
| 🟢 **Germany** | **KMK `KI-Kompetenzrahmen`, 16 Oct 2026** — four areas (*bewusst umgehen · kreativ anwenden · gezielt einsetzen · aktiv mitgestalten*), cross-curricular, NRW-led; `22` reflection cards + competence navigator in the shared **AIS.chat**. 🔴 Criticised for no concrete lesson/exam requirements |
| 🟢 **Nigeria** | **National EdTech Strategy 2025–2030**, six pillars; **Naija Teacher AI** (TRCN + GMind AI) targeting **1.5 M teachers, full adoption Q3 2026**; WAEC/NECO to computer-based testing by 2026. 🔴 **`5.67 %` of public primary schools have computer facilities vs `54.55 %` private** |
| 🔴 **Kenya — declared gap** | Draft **National AI Strategy 2025–2030** went to consultation; CBC emphasises digital literacy; `M-Shule` and `Eneza Education` named. 🔴 **No confirmed 2026 Kenyan open-source education policy found.** Recorded as a gap |

🟢 **Opportunity, two distinct ones:**
1. 🟢 **The `2 Dec 2027` runway is the EU sales argument.** A client building an assessment or
   admissions AI now has `13` months before high-risk obligations bite — long enough to build with
   logging, human oversight and documentation designed in, far cheaper than retrofitting. The
   `2 Dec 2026` marking deadline for **already-deployed** systems is the nearer and more urgent one.
2. 🟢 **Africa is an offline-first platform market, not an LLM market.** `5.67 %` facility coverage
   makes tablet-based, sync-later assessment the viable shape — which is why **Tangerine** was
   promoted to `verticals/solutions.md` this pass (`606-I`), the platform Nigeria's own gap analysis
   names.

### APAC

🟡 **No single regulatory model, and school-level attitudes diverge by country — so a regional
strategy is the wrong unit.**

| country | regulation | education |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 🟢 **South Korea** | **AI Framework Act in force January 2026** — the region's only general AI law | AI curriculum being introduced |
| 🟡 **China** | No general AI law; specific rules on recommendation algorithms, deepfakes, generative services, and 🟢 **AI-content labelling since Sept 2025** | 🟢 Formal AI-in-education policy; AI curriculum |
| 🟡 **Japan** | **AI Promotion Act** — promotion-focused, voluntary guidelines, 🔵 **no penalties** | AI curriculum being introduced |
| 🟡 **India** | No dedicated AI law; relies on guidance + data-protection law; 🟢 amended IT Rules require **AI-content labels from Feb 2026** | — |
| 🟢 **Singapore** | — | 🟢 Formal AI-in-education policy; AI curriculum |

🔵 **Attitudes (Ipsos):** opposition to banning AI in classrooms runs `23 %` (Indonesia) to `31 %`
(South Korea), India `26 %`, Japan `29 %` — and **Japan's support for a ban rose `21 % → 29 %`**, so
the direction of travel differs by country, not just the level.

🟢 **Opportunity:** **jurisdiction-aware policy enforcement as a product feature.** One deployment
serving Korea (general AI law), China (labelling mandate), Japan (voluntary) and India (IT Rules
labelling) needs the policy as configuration, not as code. 🔵 `605-F` established that APAC must be
researched as **national** legs; this page should be read the same way.

### LATAM

🟢 **The capital and the cohort are real; the systemic adoption is not yet.**

| signal | detail |
| --- | --- |
| 🟢 Cohort | **HolonIQ 2026 Latin America EdTech 100** — 🟢 **Brazil is nearly half the cohort** and the majority of deal and award activity |
| 🟢 Players | **Teachy** (Brazil — AI lesson preparation, has expanded into Asia), **Vetto** (Brazil), **Mattilda** (Mexico — billing/collections for private schools), **Ginia** (AI employability matching), **Elevify** (Brazil — seed **US$1.35 M**, led by Newtopia) |

🟢 **Opportunity:** the gap between a funded startup cohort and *"very limited"* system-level

ministry that cannot deploy it. 🔵 Concrete asset this base already holds: Brazil's **BNCC national
curriculum** as npm/PyPI/MCP packages with `1,721` verified learning objectives (`p603`), which is
the localisation substrate a Brazilian engagement starts from.

🔴 **Next measurement owed:** LATAM **national** legs (Brazil / Mexico / Colombia / Chile). Both
regions tested so far returned national detail where the acronym leg returned none (`605-F` APAC,
`606-H` EMEA); LATAM is the untested third.

## Opportunities by region — `p604` (archived)

🔵 One `###` per region, every region present. 🔴 Where a region's evidence is weak or its
supply is absent, that is written down rather than filled in. 🔴 **`598-G` stands: every demand
figure on this page is a search snippet. Every supply figure is byte-read at the default branch
with a fabricated-path control.** A client conversation leads with the second.

### North America

🟡 **Demand.** The largest regional share — `36 %` to `41.7 %` depending on the source. 🟢
**Teacher-side adoption is real and surveyed:** Gallup data cited this pass puts **`60 %` of US
teachers** using AI tools, while a Child Trends / US Department of Education analysis finds **most
US public schools still have no formal AI policy for students** — 🔵 the gap between practice and
governance *is* the consulting opportunity.

🔴 **Regulation is state-level, fragmented, and now mandating policy rather than guidance.**
`134` AI-in-education bills across `31` states in the `2026` session. 🟢 **Four states —
Maryland, Idaho, Oklahoma and Virginia — passed `2026` laws requiring *both* state guidance *and*
mandatory district-level AI policy adoption**, which converts advisory work into procurement.
Maryland's SB 720 / HB 1057 additionally designate AI coordinators. New York's A 9190 would
restrict classroom AI to grade `9` and above with diagnostic and special-education exceptions.
🔵 DC's OSSE issued a non-mandatory `2026-27` policy that **bars AI from grading, discipline, IEP
decisions and teacher evaluation** — a usable template for the four classes of decision a client
should not automate. Federally, the House Education and Workforce Committee advanced the **K-12
AI Literacy and Readiness Act of 2026**; no comprehensive statute. COPPA remains binding on
anything touching minors. 🟡 Berkeley Law banned AI on all exams and credited coursework from
summer `2026` — the counter-current, in writing.

🟢 **Supply, byte-read.** North America remains where the permissive institution-grade education
software is. This pass promoted `5` North-American rows:
[`marimo-team/learn`](https://github.com/marimo-team/learn) (MIT, `362★`),
[`EduNLP/edu-convokit`](https://github.com/EduNLP/edu-convokit) (MIT, `117★`),
[`yetanalytics/pedestal-oidc`](https://github.com/yetanalytics/pedestal-oidc) (Apache-2.0),
[`pie-framework/pie-qti`](https://github.com/pie-framework/pie-qti) and
[`sonyccd/qti-playground`](https://github.com/sonyccd/qti-playground) (both MIT), plus
[`ljchang/marimo-grader`](https://github.com/ljchang/marimo-grader) and
[`DecoyLex/ltix`](https://github.com/DecoyLex/ltix).

🟢 **The opportunity.** 🟢 **Sell the four-decision boundary, then the build.** DC has already
written down which decisions AI may not make; the four states above have made *having a policy*
compulsory. A studio that arrives with DC's boundary as a default architecture — human-in-the-loop
on grading, discipline, IEP and evaluation — satisfies the mandate and the procurement in one
artefact. 🔵 Carry `604-A`'s licence census alongside it: a US university OSS-review gate reading
`spdx_id` still blocks `10 of 11` ECL-2.0 repositories as unlicensed, including Sakai's analytics
tier.

### EMEA

🟡 **Demand.** 🟢 **Regulation is the forcing function and it has dates — but the dates moved
this pass, and the sources conflict.** The EU AI Act treats most education systems as Annex III
high-risk; enforcement by the AI Office and national authorities began **`2 August 2026`**. 🔴
**However:** the Digital Omnibus amendments — published as **Regulation (EU) 2026/1744** in the
Official Journal on `24 July 2026` and applying from `27 July 2026` — **postpone the high-risk
obligations to `December 2027` for standalone Annex III systems and `August 2028` for embedded
ones.** 🔴 A second source still states `August 2026`. 🟢 **Recorded as a conflict, not resolved:
the Omnibus-dated source is the more recent and the more specific, and the primary text is outside
this session's network policy (`598-G`).** 🔵 Either way the substantive obligations are stable:
conformity assessment, and human oversight that can **override or shut the system down**.

🟢 **Adoption is already past the pilot stage:** QS research cited this pass puts **`67 %` of
academics and `62 %` of students** using generative AI weekly, and **guidance outnumbers outright
bans roughly `2 : 1`**. 🟢 The Gulf is policy-led — Dubai's KHDA has issued explicit responsible-AI
guidance to private schools. The European Commission's **AI Continent Action Plan** names education
a priority sector. 🟡 Employer co-funding is material: Microsoft, AWS and Caterpillar commitments
total **~`$3.6 B`** (HolonIQ).

🟢 **Supply, byte-read — and a genuinely new axis.** `3` EMEA rows this pass:
[`AI-for-Education/fabdata-llm`](https://github.com/AI-for-Education/fabdata-llm) (MIT, `+01:00`),
[`otacke/h5p-theme-picker`](https://github.com/otacke/h5p-theme-picker) (MIT, `+01:00`), and
🟢 [`AI-for-Education/voice-ai-evaluation-framework`](https://github.com/AI-for-Education/voice-ai-evaluation-framework)
(MIT, `+00:00`) — **ASR evaluation for early-grade reading assessment on Kiswahili child
speech.** 🔵 That is the first sub-Saharan African child-speech literacy artefact on this base.

🟢 **The opportunity.** 🟢 **Sell the override, and sell it as an architecture.** The Act's human
oversight requirement is the one clause that cannot be satisfied by a model choice — it is a
system-design obligation, and the deferral to `2027`/`2028` is exactly the window in which to build
it rather than retrofit it. 🔵 Second, distinct play: **African-language literacy assessment.** The
Kiswahili row shows the method exists under MIT; the supply gap is everything around it.

### APAC

🟡 **Demand — and this is the subsection `604-H` unblocked.** 🔴 The acronym leg returned
compliance commentary for a second pass; the four national legs returned ministries.

🟢 **China.** Five central departments led by the Ministry of Education issued an **AI + Education
Action Plan** in spring `2026`: AI literacy across *all* school levels plus lifelong learning,
integration into local curricula, interdisciplinary teaching, after-school provision,
AI-education demonstration schools, and national platforms to deliver AI courses to **rural and
remote schools**. 🟢 Target date `2030`. 🔵 Beijing already reports **≥ `8` class-hours of AI per
student per academic year**. 🟡 Sources disagree on the announcement date (`2`, `10` or
mid-April `2026`).

🔴 **Korea — the most useful failure in the sector, and it is now documented.** The AI digital
textbook (AIDT) programme was **reclassified from textbook to optional supplementary material**.
Adoption fell **`37 % → 19 %`** between semesters (another source: `4,095 → 1,686` schools), with
regional spread from `98 %` in Daegu to `8 %` in Sejong. 🔴 Around **`10` publishers sued the
government in June `2026` for roughly `₩200 bn`.** An audit reportedly found **skipped pilots, late
standards and weak screening**. 🟢 A separate set of AI-related textbooks is scheduled for pilot
use in the **first semester of `2027`**. 🟢 Korea's **AI Basic Act entered into force `22 January
2026`**, with a guidance-first grace period through `2026`.

🟢 **Singapore — the live reference deployment.** MOE began rolling out an **Authoring Copilot**
(lesson and assessment materials) and a **Learning Assistant** platform in **January `2026`**, and
is expanding its Google partnership for educator training. All students across the institutes of
higher learning get in-discipline **AI competency development from academic year `2027`**
(announced `21 May 2026`). 🟡 A compulsory teacher AI-literacy module by end-`2026` is reported by
a lower-authority source only.

🟡 **Japan** is deliberately observational — MEXT holds a neutral stance and monitors school
pilots; the government AI plan of `14 July 2026` references school deployment and teacher
training without published school-level targets. 🟢 **Vietnam's** Law 134/2025 took effect
`1 March 2026`, and decision 33/2026/QD-TTg names **education among six high-risk sectors,
including automated assessment**. 🟢 **Australia's** TEQSA requires **all** higher-education
providers to submit institutional action plans on generative-AI risk.

🟢 **Supply, byte-read — `6` APAC rows this pass**, the largest regional batch:
[`psychquant/che-ntucool-mcp`](https://github.com/psychquant/che-ntucool-mcp) (MIT, `+08:00`,
National Taiwan University's Canvas), [`OmarNiazi/classroom-mcp`](https://github.com/OmarNiazi/classroom-mcp)
(MIT, `+05:00`), [`metyatech/qti-html-renderer`](https://github.com/metyatech/qti-html-renderer)
(MIT, `+09:00`), [`iamlucasb/lti-core`](https://github.com/iamlucasb/lti-core) (MIT, `+08:00`),
[`pingakshgaur/TatvaX-AI-PROTOTYPE`](https://github.com/pingakshgaur/TatvaX-AI-PROTOTYPE) (MIT,
`+05:30`, `8` Indian languages) and [`20251123/ai-education-project`](https://github.com/20251123/ai-education-project)
(MIT, `+08:00`, Traditional Chinese AI literacy).

🟢 **The opportunity.** 🟢 **Sell Korea's post-mortem into China's mandate.** China has committed
every school to AI literacy by `2030`; Korea has just demonstrated, with numbers and a lawsuit,
exactly how a national rollout fails — skipped pilots, late standards, weak screening, and a `19 %`
floor on voluntary adoption. 🔵 A studio that can name those four failure modes and show the
pilot-gate, standards-first sequencing that avoids them is selling risk reduction against a
documented precedent, not a capability deck. 🟢 Second play: **Singapore's pattern is the
replicable one** — a teacher-facing authoring copilot first, a learner-facing assistant second, and
AI competency embedded per discipline rather than taught as a separate subject.

### LATAM

🟡 **Demand — and this is the strongest primary-grade demand evidence on the whole page.** The
UNESCO IESALC / UNU-IAS study of **`200` higher-education institutions across `19` countries**
finds 🟢 **`87 %` using AI in at least one area of activity** and 🔴 **only `26 %` with a formal AI
strategy.** 🔵 Adoption is **bottom-up** — driven by individual academics, researchers and students
rather than institutional programmes. 🟢 **That `87 / 26` split is the single most actionable
number in this document:** the demand is already inside the institution and ungoverned.

🔴 **Regulation is fragmenting rather than consolidating.** A `2026` Universidad de los Andes study
counts **`716` AI-related regulatory instruments, rules and legislative proposals across `23`
countries and territories, more than `80 %` of them initiated since `2023`.** 🔴 No binding
education-specific AI law was found in this pass's legs. 🟢 The coordinating body now exists:
UNESCO's **Observatory on AI in Education for Latin America and the Caribbean**, launched
**`14 April 2026` in Santiago**, led by UNESCO with CAF, Chile's CENIA, Cetic.br, ECLAC, Fundación
Ceibal, Fundación Santillana, Tecnológico de Monterrey, ProFuturo, Universidad del Desarrollo and
IRCAI.

🟢 **Supply, byte-read — `3` LATAM rows, and two of them are evidence rather than code:**
🟢 [`lplnufpi/essay-br`](https://github.com/lplnufpi/essay-br) (MIT, `-03:00`) — Brazilian
high-school essays **graded by human professionals against the ENEM criteria**;
🟢 [`bncc-dev/bncc-benchmark`](https://github.com/bncc-dev/bncc-benchmark) (MIT code + CC BY 4.0
items, `-03:00`) — a hallucination benchmark over the **BNCC**, Brazil's national curriculum; and
[`glacy/evolutIA`](https://github.com/glacy/evolutIA) (Apache-2.0, `-06:00`) — Spanish-language
exam-item generation. 🔵 These join [`portabilis/i-educar`](https://github.com/portabilis/i-educar)
and [`portabilis/i-diario`](https://github.com/portabilis/i-diario) as the regional platform of
record established at `p603`.

🟢 **The opportunity.** 🟢 **Sell governance to the `87 %`, and prove competence with the
`26 %`'s own curriculum.** The institution does not need to be convinced to adopt — it has
adopted, unofficially, without a strategy. The sellable first engagement is an AI-use inventory
plus a strategy artefact, which `74 %` of surveyed institutions lack. 🔵 And the differentiator is
local: **a benchmark over the BNCC and a human-scored Portuguese essay corpus let a studio answer
"does this model know *our* curriculum, and can it mark *our* exam?" with a measurement instead of
a demo.** 🔴 Weigh the `716`-instrument fragmentation as a real cost: a multi-country LATAM
programme needs per-jurisdiction policy mapping, not one regional policy.

### Global

🟡 **Market size, and the sources do not agree.** A ResearchAndMarkets-derived release puts AI in
education at **`$7.52 B` (`2025`) → `$10.6 B` (`2026`) → `$42.48 B` (`2030`)**; HolonIQ cites
**`$12.3 B` by `2026`**. 🔴 Both are search-snippet-grade (`598-G`). 🟢 The direction is not in
dispute and the level is; quote a range, never a point.

🟢 **What the trend sources agree on across regions.** 1EdTech frames `2026` as the year AI in
education **moves from experimentation to governance** — explicit policies and data boundaries —
and expects digital credentials and skills-based pathways to grow as employer expectations shift.
Tutorflow describes the same move as experimentation → **operational use inside repeatable
workflows** (lesson prep, formative feedback). Educativa highlights **autonomous agents that plan
lessons and draft assessments**. 🟢 TeachBetter argues **purpose-built education platforms are
displacing generic AI tools**, with budget-constrained schools pulling toward all-in-one products.

🟡 **The counter-current, recorded as such.** HolonIQ expects *selective* acceleration — more
weight on demonstrated instructional value and ethics — and notes countries piloting AI companions
**with strict boundaries** (UK, Greece). 🔵 Read alongside Korea's `37 % → 19 %` and Berkeley Law's
exam ban, this is the realistic frame: the growth is in governed, narrow deployments, not in
general-purpose assistants.

🔴 **The standing gap in this entire section:** every demand figure above is a search snippet
citing a vendor, law-firm or consultancy page. 🟢 Every supply figure is byte-read. 🔴 `598-G`
remains an **operator request, not a research step** — `unesco.org`, `oecd.org`, `iadb.org` and
`unu.edu` are outside this session's network policy, re-measured `000` today with a
non-discriminating control.

### 🟢 What this means for how these legs are budgeted, stated as a recommendation rather than a complaint

🔴 **Thirty-one consecutive passes of `0` new demand-side tokens is itself a finding about the channel,
not about the market.** The commercial literature on AI in education is produced by a small set of
market-research vendors and recycled by content farms; this base has held the substantive figures for
many passes and the queries are stable, so 🟢 **the legs now function as a *currency check* — they
confirm nothing has moved — and the discovery budget belongs on the supply side,** where this pass's
`30`-axis topic sweep returned `614` unheld addresses and `28` hand-verified permissive components.
🟡 **The legs should still be run every pass, exactly as mandated** — a currency check that stops being
run stops being a check, and the first time a figure *does* move, the nil streak is what makes it
visible.

### 🔴 One demand-side caution this pass will not let pass unmarked

The `2026` market-size figures this page carries remain **mutually inconsistent at source**: `$11.4B`
(`2026`) → `$57.2B` (`2033`) from one vendor, `$3.7B` (`2026`) → `$14.1B` (`2033`) from another, and a
third at *"about `$11B` globally in `2026`"*. 🔴 **A `3×` spread in the base year is not a forecasting
disagreement, it is a definitional one** — the three are not counting the same thing. 🟢 **Use the
figures directionally and never quote a single one as the market size**; the regional *shares* and the
dated institutional surveys under `## Opportunities by region` are the better-grounded numbers and are
what a client deck should rest on.

### 🔴 The correction this page needs most, and it is methodological

This page has repeatedly reported the education-AI supply market as effectively mapped, on the
strength of a star-ranked discovery nil. 🔴 **That nil was a sampling artefact.** `p599` drew `39`
addresses from `6` topic axes and generalised; this pass drew `334` from the **same six at the same
ordering** and `138` were new (`41.3 %`), then `1,646` from `28` axes never walked, of which `1,322`
were new (`80.3 %`) — `678` unheld addresses in total (`600-B`, `600-C`).

🔵 **The market-intelligence consequence is direct:** 🔴 **a coverage claim built on one page of one
ordering is not a coverage claim.** 🟢 **And the honest limit in the same breath** — of `678` unheld
addresses only `66` were permissive *and* `≥ 5★` *and* active in `2026`, and `310` carry
`license: null` or `NOASSERTION`. 🔵 **The supply is findable and mostly not shippable**, which is a
far more useful sentence for a proposal than either "the market is covered" or "the market is wide
open", and it is the first time this page can state it with denominators on both sides.

### 🟢 Gate state recorded with the pass, as `598`-era practice requires

🔴 **This repository arrived at `p600` with `23 of 69` standing gates red** (`46` green) — pre-existing,
and recorded so a later pass does not attribute them to this one. 🟢 **`gate-region-headings` was
red on arrival and is green on exit:** the `## Opportunities by region` block carried an out-of-vocab
`###` heading (*"Declared regional gaps"*), which made a cross-regional gap register look like a
sixth region to the compiler. 🔵 It is now a `##` sibling of the block, the five `###` subsections are
exactly the closed vocabulary, and the declared gaps for each region live **inside** that region's
subsection where a region filter will actually reach them.

## 🆕 `p599` — `2026-11-01` — the market-size disagreement is in the GROWTH RATE, not in the 2026 base, and that reverses the deck rule this base has been carrying

### 🟢 Five firms measured this pass, and the `2026` bases agree

| firm | `2026` base | endpoint | CAGR | grade |
| --- | --: | --- | --: | --- |
| Trend X Insights | **`$7.30 B`** | `$61.26 B` by `2034` | `31.5 %` | secondary |
| Precedence Research | **`$9.58 B`** | `$136.79 B` by `2035` | `34.52 %` | secondary |
| Resourcera (citing) | **`$10.4 B`** | `$32.27 B` by `2030` | `31.2 %` | tertiary |
| Research and Markets | **`$10.6 B`** | `$42.48 B` by `2030` | `41.5 %` | secondary, report dated `2026-02` |
| Grand View Research | **`$11.4 B`** | `$57.2 B` by `2033` | `25.9 %` | secondary, already carried |

🟢 **Every one of the five falls inside the `$7 B–$11.4 B` band this page already publishes.** The
band is `5` passes old and it survived a fresh five-firm sweep without moving — which is a stronger
result than a revision would have been.

🔴 **The endpoints do not agree, and the spread is `4.3×`**: `$32.27 B` (Resourcera, `2030`) against
`$136.79 B` (Precedence, `2035`). 🔵 **So the disagreement between firms is a disagreement about the
growth rate, not about today's market.** Precedence states the mechanism itself — its figures
*"depend on market boundaries and the treatment of embedded AI revenues"*.

🔵 **This refines, and partly reverses, the rule this base adopted at `p93`.** That rule
(*"never put a market size in an education deck without naming the scope"*) was derived from the
MarketsandMarkets regional series summing to `≈ $2.07 B` against a `$7–11.4 B` band — a **scope**
failure. Measured here, scope is not the whole story: 🟢 **the `2026` base is robust across firms and
safe to quote; it is the forecast endpoint that is firm-specific and must never be quoted alone.**
New rule, additive rather than replacing: **quote the `2026` base (five firms agree inside `±22 %` of
the midpoint); if an endpoint is required, name the firm, the year and the CAGR in the same
sentence.** Registered as **`599-E`**.

### 🔴 A `$12.5 B` figure was served this pass and is NOT banked

A trends-leg source reports the market *"surged past the `$12.5 billion` milestone in `2026`"*, and a
second reports `$12.3 B`. 🔴 **Neither is corroborated by any of the five research firms above, and
both exceed the highest of them (`$11.4 B`).** Both are vendor/aggregator blogs. 🟢 **Rejected, not
averaged in** — and recorded here so a later pass does not re-find them and read two mutually
citing blogs as two sources. 🔵 **The band's ceiling does not move on blog evidence.**

### 🔴 The LATAM provenance trap this pass caught before filing

The LATAM leg served: *"la PUC firmó con Microsoft el programa **ConectIA**, que busca una
integración completa de la IA a nivel institucional para el año **2026**"* — a clean,
quotable, region-placed engagement reference. 🔴 **`ConectIA` was `0`-hits in this entire base**, so
it would have been filed as new regional intelligence.

🟢 **Checked against the primary institution first.** `uc.cl`'s own newsroom dates the UC
Chile–Microsoft ConectIA launch to **`2024`** (*"the first step … was taken at the end of last
year"*, under rector Ignacio Sánchez, who left office in March `2025`), and `2026` UC AI coverage —
the Chile–California AI mission — **does not mention ConectIA at all**. 🔴 **So the aggregator
re-dated a `2024` initiative as a `2026` target.** `ConectIA` stays unshelved as `2026` supply and is
filed here as a caution. 🔵 **The general shape, worth more than the instance: a secondary LATAM
aggregator will carry a real programme with a *forward* date attached, and the forward date is where
the fabrication lives — not in the programme name.** Registered as **`599-F`**.

### 🔴 Three primary legal hosts unreachable, so the Annex III date is STILL secondary-only

| host | result | consequence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu` | 🔴 `ENOTFOUND` (DNS) | Commission's own AI Act timeline unread |
| `eur-lex.europa.eu` | 🔴 `ENOTFOUND` (DNS) | Regulation (EU) `2026/1744` primary text unread |
| `artificialintelligenceact.eu` | 🔴 `ENOTFOUND` (DNS) | the standard third-party tracker unread |

🟢 **Re-corroborated at secondary grade, and the dates this base carries did not move**: the Digital
Omnibus — **Regulation (EU) `2026/1744` of `2026-07-08`, in force `2026-07-27`** — moved stand-alone
Annex III high-risk obligations to **`2027-12-02`** and AI embedded in regulated products to
**`2028-08-02`**, while **Article 4 AI literacy has applied since `2025-02-02`** and is unaffected.
Source grade: a guide updated `2026-09-01`. Enforcement by the AI Office and national authorities
**began `2026-08-02`** — 🔵 **which is precisely the date that keeps being misread as the Annex III
deadline.**

🔴 **And the wrong reading was served for the SIXTH consecutive pass**, in the same sweep: *"several
guides say high-risk education obligations start in August 2026"*. 🔵 **Six instances in six passes
is no longer an error to correct; it is the market's majority reading** — so an EMEA education client
has probably been given `2026-08-02` by their installed advisor, and the first deliverable of an
EMEA engagement is the corrected clock, with the `2026-08-02` enforcement date explained as the
thing their advisor actually saw.

### 🔴 The regional legs: `4 of 4` paid, `0 of 4` served a token this base lacks

Eighth consecutive pass at `4 of 4` paid. Tokens served and already held: North America — `134`
bills / `31` states, the four states mandating both state guidance and district policy
(Maryland, Idaho, Oklahoma, Virginia), the `K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act of 2026`, the
April `2026` ADA Title II accessibility deadline, the `~70 %` Europe-and-North-America guidance
composite; EMEA — Reg. `2026/1744`, Article 4, the `Apply AI Alliance`, deployer classification;
APAC — Korea's `AI Basic Act` (in force `2026-01-22`, high-risk grace to `2027`), India's
compulsory AI from `Class 3` in `2026-27`, China's mandatory content labelling, Japan's promotion-only
`AI Promotion Act`; LATAM — UNESCO IESALC/UNU `200` institutions / `19` countries, Brazil's
`PL 2338/2023`, Chile's `2021` national AI policy, Mexico's absent federal AI law.
🔴 **Every one already carried.** The one *apparently* new token was `ConectIA`, refuted above.

🔵 **Saturation on the demand side is now the stable result, not the news** — and `599-B` explains
why it coexists with a live supply channel: this base's demand legs and its supply legs are
*differently* exhausted, and only the supply side responds to a change of ordering.
