## 🆕 p591 — 2026-11-01 — all four regional legs run and the demand-side saturation is now measurable at `15 of 16` tokens already held; the genuinely new regional intelligence this pass produced is SUPPLY-side, and it is a `5.5×` asymmetry between APAC and EMEA in adoptable in-language artefacts

### 🔴 The demand-side legs, measured against this base rather than described

🔵 **`p588` declared the regional intel legs saturated at `20 of 24`. This pass puts a
sharper number on it.** All four mandated regional legs ran in full. Every distinctive
token they returned was then grepped against this base's `11` curated files
(`archive/` excluded):

| Token served by a regional leg | Files already holding it | New? |
|---|--:|---|
| `134` bills / `31` states (North America) | `23` | 🔴 no — **retired at `p455`, fifth consecutive serve** |
| K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act of 2026 | — (held, see NA below) | 🔴 no |
| Gallup ~`60 %` of US teachers using AI | `9` | 🔴 no |
| North America = `36 %` of the global market | `61` | 🔴 no |
| EU **Digital Omnibus** high-risk deferral | `64` | 🔴 no |
| Korea **AI Basic Act** | `71` | 🔴 no |
| Vietnam law **134/2025/QH15** | `23` | 🔴 no |
| Australia **TEQSA** genAI action plans | `4` | 🟡 thinly held |
| Dubai **KHDA** guidance to private schools | `4` | 🟡 thinly held |
| EU **AI Continent Action Plan** | `2` | 🟡 **thinnest held of the `16`** |
| Canada **Working for Workers Four Act, 2024** | `1` | 🟡 thinnest, and 🔴 **not education law** — it is employment law, served by an education query |
| UNESCO **IESALC** `87 %` of LAC institutions | `133` | 🔴 no |
| **Digital Education Council** LATAM `92 %` / `79 %` | `38` | 🔴 no |
| Tecnológico de Monterrey partnership | `8` | 🔴 no |
| Market `$10.6 B` 2026 → `$42.48 B` 2030 | `52` / `52` | 🔴 no |

🔴 **`15 of 16` already held, and the one near-miss is employment law mislabelled as
education law.** 🔵 **The scheduling conclusion, stated plainly:** the mandated regional
*demand* legs are a reconfirmation instrument on this industry and should be budgeted as
one. 🟢 They are not worthless — they confirmed nothing has reversed — but a pass that
expects new demand facts from them is mispriced.

### 🟡 The one demand-side reconciliation worth recording

🔴 **The EU high-risk date for education AI is still reported three different ways, and this
pass's sources disagree with each other in the same search.** One guide this pass retrieved
states high-risk obligations *"apply from August 2026"*; a second states the **Digital
Omnibus** amended that to **2 December 2027** for stand-alone Annex III systems and
**2 August 2028** for AI embedded in regulated products; the European Commission's own page
confirms the AI Office and national authorities **began enforcing from 2 August 2026** and
that the amendments entered into force **27 July 2026**. 🟢 **Current position to quote:
the Omnibus dates (Dec 2027 / Aug 2028), with the enforcement machinery already live since
August 2026.** 🔴 **Why this cannot be closed here:** the primary source is the Official
Journal, and `588-B` stands — no non-GitHub primary host is reachable from this harness.
🔵 So this is recorded as *the best secondary reconciliation available*, not as a verified
date, and an engagement must have counsel confirm it.

### 🟢 What this pass DID add: supply-side regional intelligence

🔵 **This base's regional pages describe demand — adoption rates, regulation, budgets —
and have never placed the *supply* a studio would actually deploy.** `p591` measured it:
`37` discovery axes, `45` addresses this base did not hold, `65` grants byte-read
(method and tables on [`../repos/trending.md`](../repos/trending.md) and
[`../agents/top.md`](../agents/top.md)).

| Region | 🟢 adoptable in-language / in-region artefacts added this pass | 🔴 blocked (no grant) | What the number means for an engagement |
|---|--:|--:|---|
| **APAC** | 🟢 **`11`** | `4` | Deep in-language supply across Chinese, Korean and Japanese: study workbenches, national-textbook ingestion, high-stakes exam prep. 🟢 **An APAC engagement can compose rather than build.** |
| **LATAM** | 🟢 `6` | 🔴 **`6`** | 🔴 **The only region where blocked equals adopted.** Courseware licenses, tutors do not — `591-C`. |
| **EMEA** | 🔴 **`2`** | `1` | 🔴 **The thinnest in-language supply of any region this pass** — one German STEM tutor, one Persian subtitling tool. For a continent of `24` official EU languages this is close to nothing. |
| **North America** | `2` | `1` | English-language supply is abundant but is filed `Global`, so the regional count understates it. 🔵 Read NA as served by the `Global` tier. |

🔴 **`591-F` — the EMEA in-language gap is now the largest measured supply gap in this
base, and it is larger than LATAM's.** This base has spent `5` passes on LATAM's
in-language asymmetry (`p587`'s `33×`) and has never measured EMEA's. Measured this pass:
**`2` in-language EMEA artefacts against APAC's `11`, a `5.5×` asymmetry in the same
instrument** — and unlike LATAM, where the supply exists but is unlicensed, in EMEA **the
supply is simply absent**. 🟢 **Those are different commercial situations and need different
answers:** a LATAM engagement negotiates or rebuilds six known tutors; an EMEA engagement
has nothing in-language to negotiate for and must localise from the `Global` tier. 🔵 This
is the measurement to re-run next pass with EMEA-language free-text axes (German, French,
Spanish-of-Spain, Italian, Polish, Dutch, Arabic, Turkish), which **no pass has run** —
`p576`'s native-language legs were per-*jurisdiction regulation*, not per-language supply.

### 🔵 Regional demand snapshots — restated, with provenance, so nothing above reads as new

#### 🟢 North America

Posture unchanged: **state-led, no federal statute**, restrictive rather than enabling.
This pass reconfirmed the `134`-bill/`31`-state volume figure (🔴 retired — do not price
against it), the **K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act of 2026** at committee stage,
**Maryland**'s guidance-plus-local-policy package, **New York**'s proposal to limit
classroom AI to grade 9 and above, Gallup's ~`60 %` teacher adoption, and North America at
`36 %` of global market share. 🔴 **New facts: nil.** 🟡 One token arrived that this base
holds once — Canada's **Working for Workers Four Act, 2024** — and it is **employment law,
not education law**; recorded so it is not re-filed as a Canadian education instrument.

#### 🟢 EMEA

**EU:** enforcement machinery live since **2 August 2026** (AI Office + national
authorities); high-risk obligations for education deferred by the **Digital Omnibus** to
**2 December 2027** (stand-alone Annex III) and **2 August 2028** (embedded in regulated
products); amendments in force **27 July 2026**. 🔴 Sources disagree — see the
reconciliation above. **UK:** light-touch, principle-based through existing regulators,
consultation ongoing. **Gulf:** Dubai's **KHDA** has issued explicit responsible-AI
guidance to private schools (🟡 thinly held, secondary, commercial-blog source). **EU
uptake policy:** the **AI Continent Action Plan** names education among the sectors to
accelerate (🟡 held in only `2` files — the thinnest token this pass served).
🔴 **Reliable EMEA *adoption* statistics were not returned by this leg** — a declared nil,
and the second consecutive pass to declare it. 🔴 **And see `591-F`: EMEA is also this
base's thinnest region for adoptable in-language supply.**

#### 🟢 APAC

**Korea:** AI Basic Act in force, high-impact duties (transparency, risk assessment, human
oversight, documentation) from **22 January 2026**, with a guidance-first grace period
through 2026. **Vietnam:** law **134/2025/QH15** effective **1 March 2026**, risk-based,
and **education is a named high-risk sector with automated assessment called out
explicitly** — the most directly product-relevant APAC obligation in this base.
**Australia:** **TEQSA** requires *all* higher-education providers to file institutional
genAI action plans (🟡 thinly held). **Singapore:** consulting on AI Risk Management
Guidelines. **Indonesia:** rules expected as a Presidential Regulation. **China:**
mandatory registration and content-labelling. **Japan:** light-touch/voluntary.
🟢 **APAC is also the region with the deepest adoptable supply this pass (`11`)**, so it is
the one region where regulation is tightening *and* composition is cheap.

#### 🟢 LATAM

**Adoption is near-universal and governance is not.** UNESCO **IESALC** 2026: `87 %` of
institutions in Latin America and the Caribbean use AI in at least one area, with adoption
driven by faculty, researchers and students **rather than institutional policy**. **Digital
Education Council** 2026 LATAM survey (30,000+ responses, 29 institutions, with
Tecnológico de Monterrey): `92 %` of students and `79 %` of faculty actively engaging with
AI, and students asking for clearer governance, citing data privacy and fairness in
AI-based assessment. An October 2026 index of 20 economies places **Brazil, Chile and
Uruguay** in the leading group, notes more than half have AI in school curricula, and finds
**only Brazil and Colombia allocate budget** inside their national instruments. Against
`~70 %` of institutions in Europe and North America having or developing guidance, LAC sits
at `45 %`. 🔴 **New facts: nil — all five figures were already held.** 🟢 **What is new is
`591-C`:** the region's in-language *supply* exists, and `6 of 6` of its conversational AI
tutors carry no licence.

## p589 — 2026-11-01 — all eight mandated legs run, `19` intel tokens recovered and every one of them secondary: the `3×` publisher spread this page already publishes is reconfirmed at `3.1×`, LATAM returns its first IGO instrument in three passes and it is unreadable, and `5 of 5` primary hosts are unreachable with a control pair proving the failure cannot be diagnosed

🔵 **Source class is marked on every row below, per `584-F`.** `SECONDARY` = press,
vendor blog or aggregator. `LEAD` = a real, dated, named instrument whose primary host is
**unreachable from this harness**, so it is quotable only as *"reported"* and never as a
figure in a proposal. 🔴 **Nothing on this page acquired `PRIMARY` status this pass.**

| host | `curl` | `WebFetch` |
|---|---|---|
| `unu.edu` | 🔴 `000` | 🔴 `getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND` |
| `publications.iadb.org` | 🔴 `000` | — |

🔴 **`5 of 5`, and the control pair kills the obvious workaround.** `WebFetch`'s error text
*names the session allowlist* as a possible cause, which looked like the discriminator
`p576` wanted. It is not: a deliberately fabricated hostname returns the **same bytes**. 🔵

network-allowlist request, not a research task.** Registered as `589-D`.

🔵 **The LATAM thesis is therefore unchanged and now better evidenced: three IGOs
independently describe the same shape — high enthusiasm, high student use, weak governance
and thin in-language supply.** That gap *is* the engagement, and `p587`'s measured `33×`

## p587 — 2026-11-01 — the LATAM leg returns a measured `33×` asymmetry against APAC in the same instrument, and the regional market legs are reconciled against a spread this page already publishes

### 🟢 The region finding that is actionable: LATAM has no in-language supply to adopt

The repository-level in-language leg — the channel `p586` used to place its APAC rows —
run in Spanish and Portuguese, with a Chinese control in the same hour:

| leg | query | repos | best row |
|---|---|--:|---|
| LATAM | `educación ia stars:>20` | **0** | — |
| LATAM | `tutor educacion stars:>10` | **0** | — |
| LATAM | `educacao inteligencia artificial stars:>5` | **0** | — |
| LATAM | `educacao ia stars:>10` | **1** | `kaledbarreto/SophIA` — 15★, **no licence**, last push `2024-05-12` |
| LATAM | `escuela ia stars:>10` | **1** | `maxfloresv/FM849` — 14★, `NOASSERTION`, a **course repo** |
| 🟢 APAC control | `教育 AI stars:>10` | **67** | — |
| 🟢 APAC control | `教育 智能 stars:>20` | **42** | — |

🔴 **Two Iberian-language repositories above 10★ and neither is adoptable, against 67 in the
Chinese-language control — a ~`33×` asymmetry measured with one instrument in one hour.**
The 104 hits on `educacion inteligencia artificial` are coursework repositories at 1–2★
with no licence file.

🔵 **What follows for delivery, concretely.** An APAC engagement can **adopt in-language**
(`kaogong-skill`, `cram-engine`, `cn-math-taxonomy`, `OpenMAIC`, `DeepTutor` are all on
this base's shelves and all APAC-origin). A LATAM engagement **cannot** — it is a
localisation of a Global/English shelf row, or a BUILD. 🟢 That is not a lament: it is the
estimate line item that separates a LATAM proposal from an APAC one, and it is now measured
rather than assumed.

🟡 **Method caveat, quantified:** `educación` returns **5,929** and `educacion` **11,175** —
the unaccented form finds **1.9×** more. An accented-only query under-reports by half, so
both forms are required. (This base holds accent-control evidence from `p476`–`p477`; this
is the figure at a star floor.)

### 🟡 The regional market legs — what they returned, and why no figure here is new

All four mandated regional legs ran. 🔴 **Not one returned a figure this page can adopt
without widening its own published spread**, which `p583` already set at `2.6×` and `p584`
at `3.0×`:

| region | what the leg returned | disposition |
|---|---|---|
| North America | 36–38 % of global share; **$3.68 B (2026)** on a 36 % basis; a third source gives ~$951 M (2024) | 🔴 **Inconsistent with itself** — a 36 % share and a $951 M regional total cannot both hold. Not adopted. |
| EMEA | **56 %** of EMEA CEOs report AI readiness vs **48 %** Americas (Workday); **94 %** likely to invest in AI training in 2026 (CompTIA) | 🟡 **Enterprise-wide, not education.** Directional only, and labelled as such. |
| APAC | **$591.6 M (2024) → $1,848.1 M (2029), 20.9 % CAGR** (MarketsandMarkets) | 🟢 **The only internally consistent regional series the leg returned**, and the slowest CAGR on this page — notable against the 41 %+ global figures. |
| LATAM | *"Rest of Latin America"* **$18.2 M (2024) → ~$36 M (2029)** | 🔴 **Scope-incoherent**: "Rest of" excludes Brazil and Mexico, so it is not a LATAM total and must never be quoted as one. |

🔵 **The repo-level LATAM finding above is worth more than any of these four figures**,

### 🟢 Regulatory movement by region, dated

- **North America** — **134 bills across 31 states** tracked on AI in education as of
  **March 2026** (privacy, classroom use, curriculum); **$169 M** in US Department of
  Education awards released **January 2026** including responsible-AI-in-higher-ed support.
  🔴 Still no federal statute: the posture remains state-by-state, which is what makes
  `canvas-mcp`-style **attachment** rather than forking the right architecture here.
- **EMEA** — EU AI Act **AI-literacy obligations in force since `2025-02-02`**; the European
  Commission has **updated its ethical guidelines on AI in education**; the UK's first **AI
  Adoption Summit** committed **£200 m+**. 🔴 The Annex III high-risk compliance date
  remains contested across EU-facing sources (see the `p586` section) — unchanged this pass.
- **APAC** — 🔴 **Forrester: no common APAC-wide AI law is expected**, and the 2026 regional
  frame is **sovereignty, security and responsible deployment**. 🟢 This is why the APAC
  shelf rows that run **offline or on local weights** (`wordlink`'s fine-tuned 1.5B,
  `Kolibri`) are the strategically valuable ones, not the API-bound tutors.
- **LATAM** — a **UNESCO IESALC / UNU-IAS working paper (`2026-09-01`, Arianna Valentini)**
  maps AI use across LAC universities on five axes; **ILIA 2025** (ECLAC/CEPAL) published
  **2026**; the **AI in Higher Education LATAM Survey 2026** (Tec de Monterrey, February
  2026) reports the region as *more positive about AI in higher education than any other*.
  🔵 **Enthusiasm high, in-language open-source supply ~zero** — those two facts sit
  together and together they describe the opportunity.

## p586 — 2026-11-01 — all four mandated regional legs paid, APAC reversed from a declared nil, and the EU's own high-risk date for education AI is contradicted by the EU's own sources

🟢 **Four of four regional legs returned dated, attributable intelligence this pass.**
`p585` recorded three of four with **APAC a declared nil**; APAC is this pass's richest
leg, because the question was wrong before. Searching for *adoption* in APAC returns
market-research summaries; searching for *statutory instruments* returns three laws, two
of which name education explicitly.

🔴 **The finding an engagement lead must not be given wrong: the date on which EU
education AI becomes high-risk-regulated is contradicted by three EU-facing sources, and
the spread is 16 months.**

| Source type | Date given for high-risk obligations | Implication for an EU education engagement |
|---|---|---|
| Education-sector AI Act guides | **2 August 2026** | Already in force today |
| European Commission digital-strategy page | Enforcement by the AI Office and national authorities **from 2 August 2026**; omnibus amendments in force late July 2026 | Governance machinery live, obligations staged |
| Reported **Digital Omnibus** amendment | **2 December 2027** for stand-alone Annex III systems; **2 August 2028** for AI embedded in regulated products | ~16 months later than the guides say |

🔴 **Do not quote a date to a client from this page.** The honest statement is: *most
education AI is Annex III high-risk under the EU AI Act; enforcement machinery is live as
of August 2026; the compliance deadline for stand-alone systems is contested between
August 2026 and December 2027 and must be checked against the Official Journal for the
specific system.* 🟢 **The commercial consequence is the opposite of a blocker** — a
contested deadline with live enforcement is precisely the condition under which clients
buy compliance work. See gap `586-E`.

🟢 **The second structural finding: LATAM's governance gap is now quantified from two
independent instruments, and they agree.** The Digital Education Council's 2026 LATAM
survey (>30,000 responses, 29 institutions) and UNESCO IESALC's new regional study were
run separately and both report near-universal adoption against institutional governance
that has not kept up. **Two sources, one conclusion, no shared methodology** — that is as
strong as this page's evidence standard gets, and it makes governance-and-enablement the
clearest offer in the region.

🔵 **Market size, restated as a range because the sources still disagree.** A
ResearchAndMarkets-derived figure puts AI-in-education at **~$10.6B in 2026** growing at
**~40.9% CAGR to ~$42.5B by 2030**, with **North America leading and APAC growing
fastest**. A vendor blog attributes **$12.3B for 2026** to HolonIQ; that attribution could
not be verified against HolonIQ directly and is **not** adopted here. Treat the range as
**$10.6–12.3B** and cite the lower, sourced figure.

### (historical) Opportunities by region — superseded, retained for history

Refreshed **2026-11-01** (`p587`). One subsection per region, closed vocabulary. Figures
carry their source; where a region returned nothing on an axis, that nil is written down,
because an informed gap is information and silence looks exactly like coverage.

### North America

🆕 **`p591` — supply note.** English-language supply is abundant but files as `Global`, so a
North America brief is served by the `Global` tier rather than by region-placed rows; the
two NA-placed additions this pass are [`IdkwhatImD0ing/AdaptEd`](https://github.com/IdkwhatImD0ing/AdaptEd)
(MIT, real-time voice tutor with emotion-aware pacing) and
[`timothywarner-org/prompt-pro`](https://github.com/timothywarner-org/prompt-pro) (MIT,
publisher-backed enterprise prompt-engineering courseware — rare under a permissive grant).
🔴 Demand-side: nil new this pass, fifth consecutive serve of the retired `134`-bill figure.

🆕 **`p590` — the compliance surface is the sellable artefact, not the model.** US AI
regulation remains **state-led with no federal statute**, plus **COPPA** for under-13
products. 🟢 So a North America engagement's first deliverable is a **per-state
constraint matrix** the client's own counsel can sign off, wired to
[`yetanalytics/project-pan`](https://github.com/yetanalytics/project-pan) (Apache-2.0) and
[`get-bridge/scorm_engine`](https://github.com/get-bridge/scorm_engine) (MIT) for the
evidence trail. 🔴 **Do not price against the "134 bills / 31 states" figure** — retired at
`p455`, served again this pass, tenth sighting.

**Regulatory posture: the most legislatively active region in the world, and the activity
is restrictive rather than enabling.**

🆕 **`p588`:** 🔴 **Nil new.** Seven instruments returned by this pass's leg (state-bill volume, NC SL 2026-41, MD SB 720, the NYC playbook, SUNY, UC Berkeley Law, EO 14277) were **all already filed**. 🔵 This region is saturated on this channel; the next North America addition will have to come from a primary document, which this environment cannot open (`588-B`).

| Signal | Detail | Source type |
|---|---|---|
| Volume | **134 AI-in-education bills introduced across 31 states in 2026** | legislative tracker |
| Dual-mandate states | **Maryland, Idaho, Oklahoma, Virginia** require *both* state guidance *and* mandatory district policies; Maryland's package also mandates **district AI coordinators** | tracker / state packages |
| Restriction by grade | **New York A 9190** would limit classroom AI to **grade 9 and above**, with carve-outs for diagnostics and special education | bill text (pending) |
| Prohibited uses | **DC** education office's 2026-27 policy (non-mandatory) **bars AI from grading, discipline, IEP decisions and teacher evaluation** | district policy |
| Framework guidance | **NYC Public Schools** preliminary guidance (March 2026) on a **traffic-light** model, fuller playbook expected; Alaska, Missouri, North Carolina have K-12 guidance | district/state guidance |
| Federal | **K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act of 2026** advanced by the House Education and Workforce Committee | committee action |
| Institutional capacity | **≥29 states** have AI task forces or commissions (Education Commission of the States); Southern Regional Education Board's AI Commission issued **9 recommendations** | sector bodies |
| Canada | Workplace AI under the **Working for Workers Four Act, 2024**; Ontario AI **hiring-disclosure** rules pending. 🔴 No Canada-specific *education* instrument found | statute / pending |

**Adoption vs governance:** ~**60–61% of US teachers** use AI tools (Gallup-derived);
~**86% of higher-ed students**; **55% of high-school principals** say their school has
*not* blocked or restricted AI; ~**70% of institutions in Europe and North America** have
or are developing guidance. 🔵 An **April 2026 ADA web-accessibility deadline** for large
public institutions is independently pulling AI-enabled accessibility tooling into scope.

🟢 **The offer.** *Compliance-shaped AI.* The prohibited-use lists (grading, discipline,
IEP, teacher evaluation) are **unusually specific**, which means an engagement can be
scoped against them precisely: build the tutoring, feedback and content layers that are
permitted, and make the audit trail that proves the prohibited ones were not touched.
[`Terracotta`](https://github.com/terracotta-education/terracotta) (Apache-2.0) is the only
row on the shelf built for research-ethics constraints — consent hidden from the
instructor, identifiers stripped from exports — and it maps directly onto this posture.
Canvas is the higher-ed default: attach via
[`canvas-mcp`](https://github.com/vishalsachdev/canvas-mcp) (MIT, FERPA-aware), **never a
fork** (AGPL-3.0, and ~6 months since its last public push).


🆕 **`p587`:** **134 bills across 31 states** tracked on AI in education (March 2026) and **$169 M** in US Dept. of Education awards (January 2026). 🟢 Two assessment-tier rows measured here this pass: [`PrairieLearn`](https://github.com/PrairieLearn/PrairieLearn) (527★, UIUC, pushed `2026-11-01`) is 🔴 **split-grant — AGPL-3.0 CE, proprietary `ee/` subtree, AGPL client JS**, so it is a reference and not a shippable base; [`OS4ED/openSIS-Classic`](https://github.com/OS4ED/openSIS-Classic) is re-confirmed 🔴 **unlicensed** (no grant at 5 paths, 347★, pushed `2026-10-26`) — maintained and still `BLOCK`.

### EMEA

🆕 **`p591` — the opportunity here is localisation, and that is a measured claim.** `591-F`:
EMEA returned **`2`** adoptable in-language artefacts against APAC's **`11`** — a `5.5×`
asymmetry, and the thinnest in-region supply of any region this pass. 🔴 **Unlike LATAM,
the supply is absent rather than unlicensed**, so there is nothing to negotiate for. 🟢 **The
sellable engagement is therefore localisation of the `Global` tier into EU languages** —
start from [`PndaMan/cortex`](https://github.com/PndaMan/cortex) (Apache-2.0) or
[`Sae-l/tutor`](https://github.com/Sae-l/tutor) (MIT, German, the one in-language reference
that exists) over the permissive platforms of record (**Sakai** ECL-2.0, **OpenOLAT**
Apache-2.0). 🔵 Compliance framing: enforcement live since **2 Aug 2026**, high-risk duties
deferred to **2 Dec 2027 / 2 Aug 2028** per the Digital Omnibus — counsel must confirm.

🆕 **`p590` — assessment integrity in England now has a named regulator position to design
against.** **Ofqual has published principles for AI in high-stakes marking**, and the
**Commission updated its AI-in-education ethical guidelines on `9 June`** (EUR `25.4` m of
Advanced Digital Skills grants signed). 🟢 **EMEA is also where the permissive emit tooling
actually lives:** `LiaScript-Exporter` (BSD-3-Clause, Germany), `numbas/Numbas` (Apache-2.0,
Newcastle University), `scorm-h5p-wrapper` (MIT), `Didask/scol-r` (MIT, France),
`erseco/python-moodle` (MIT), `BerryCloud/xapi-java` (Apache-2.0) — 🟢 **`6` of the `10`
newly filed permissive rows this pass are EMEA-origin.** 🔴 Africa remains a declared nil
(`586-F`): no dated instrument, third pass running.

**Regulatory posture: the strictest *in principle*, and the least certain *in date*.**

🆕 **`p588`:** 🟡 **Two leads, neither verifiable here.** An **AUDA-NEPAD** guideline reportedly urging open datasets, open models and African-language content plus registries of approved AI education tools, and the **AU Continental Education Strategy 2026–2035**. 🔴 `www.nepad.org` does not resolve from this environment, so **both are recorded as leads, not facts** — see the `p588` section at the top of this page. 🔴 The contested Annex III dates (**2 Dec 2027** / **2 Aug 2028**) arrived again from a secondary source: **`586-E` is narrowed, not closed.**

- Most consequential education AI is **Annex III high-risk** under the **EU AI Act**.
  🔴 **The compliance date is contested across EU-facing sources — August 2026 vs
  December 2027, a 16-month spread** (table in the `p586` section above). Check the
  Official Journal per system; do not quote a date from this page.
- Enforcement machinery is **live**: the AI Office and national authorities began
  enforcement **from 2 August 2026**, with the omnibus amendments in force from late
  July 2026.
- **UK**: light-touch, principle-based, delivered through **existing regulators** rather
  than a dedicated AI statute — consultation ongoing.
- **Gulf**: **KHDA (Dubai)** has issued explicit responsible-AI guidance to private
  schools — the most concrete Middle East education instrument this pass found.
- 🔴 **Declared nil — Africa.** This pass found **no** Africa-specific education-AI
  regulation or adoption data. The regional searches returned EU and US material under an
  EMEA query. This is a **gap in the evidence, not a finding of absence**; it has now
  recurred across passes and is filed as `586-F`.

🟢 **The offer.** *Sovereign, auditable AI on a forkable platform.* EMEA is the only region
where the permissive platforms of record are also the locally governed ones:
[`OpenOLAT`](https://github.com/OpenOLAT/OpenOLAT) (Apache-2.0, Swiss-governed, DACH higher
education) and [`Sakai`](https://github.com/sakaiproject/sakai) (ECL-2.0) can be forked,
modified and shipped. Moodle dominates the installed base and is GPL-3.0 — attach via
**LTI 1.3** ([`ltijs`](https://github.com/Cvmcosta/ltijs), Apache-2.0) and the AI stays
outside the copyleft boundary. For data residency,
[`wordlink`](https://github.com/orionsheep/wordlink)'s **offline fine-tuned 1.5B model**
and [`Open TutorAI CE`](https://github.com/Open-TutorAi/open-tutor-ai-CE)'s Ollama-local
stack are the two shelf rows that answer the residency question *structurally* rather than
contractually — which is what a high-risk classification rewards.


🆕 **`p587`:** EU AI Act **AI-literacy obligations in force since `2025-02-02`**; EC **updated ethical guidelines on AI in education**; UK **AI Adoption Summit, £200 m+**. 🟡 **56 %** of EMEA CEOs report AI readiness vs **48 %** Americas (Workday) — enterprise-wide, not education, and directional only. 🔴 **The EMEA-origin tutor-evaluation benchmark has no grant:** [`eth-lre/mathtutorbench`](https://github.com/eth-lre/mathtutorbench) (ETH Zürich, EMNLP, 46★, `2026-08-27`) has **no licence file at 5 paths** — the region produces the evaluation science and licenses none of it.

### APAC

🆕 **`p591` — the one region where composing beats building.** `11` adoptable in-language
artefacts added this pass, across study workbenches
([`wansui976/CoursePilot`](https://github.com/wansui976/CoursePilot),
[`inbeomheo/insight-engine`](https://github.com/inbeomheo/insight-engine) — Korean, ko/en/ja),
**statutory-textbook ingestion**
([`DanielHuang-ning/smartedu-textbook`](https://github.com/DanielHuang-ning/smartedu-textbook),
MIT — the grounding corpus a K-12 tutor needs), high-stakes exam prep
([`1599570912/IELTS-Speaking-AI`](https://github.com/1599570912/IELTS-Speaking-AI)) and
assessment review ([`Jerryyy985/blind-review-skill`](https://github.com/Jerryyy985/blind-review-skill)).
🟢 **Pair that with the region's tightening obligations** — Vietnam names education
high-risk with automated assessment called out, Korea's high-impact duties live since
22 Jan 2026, TEQSA mandating institutional genAI plans — and the highest-value APAC offer is
**a compliant assessment pipeline assembled from permissive parts**, not a greenfield build.

🆕 **`p590` — in-language, locally-hosted models are the regional default, and the first
named education programme lands.** **Philippines DepEd + Google are certifying over one
million teachers** via **Project S.I.N.A.G.**, and Forrester's `60 %` locally-developed-LLM
finding is now at its **second independent sighting** with education named a target sector.
🟢 **Pair that with the supply gap:** the only genuinely new address the mandated legs
produced in `21` passes is
[`anandpatikat/guru-latihan-dinamis`](https://github.com/anandpatikat/guru-latihan-dinamis)
— Indonesian-language exam preparation, `115★` — and 🔴 **it is unlicensed, so the
in-language APAC supply is still not something a studio can build on.** 🔵 That is the
opportunity: **build it, in-language, permissively licensed.**

🟢 **Reversed from `p585`'s declared nil, and it is now the region with the most binding
education-specific law.** The nil was an artefact of asking about adoption; the statutes
were there.

🆕 **`p588`:** 🔴 **Nil new.** Korea's AI Basic Act, Vietnam's AI Law and its 18-month education transition, Japan's voluntary posture, Australia on existing law, the China/Japan/Korea/Singapore curriculum integration and the Ipsos Education Monitor 2026 were **all already filed**. 🔵 Saturated on this channel.

| Jurisdiction | Instrument | Status | Education-specific? |
|---|---|---|---|
| **South Korea** | **AI Basic Act** | 🟢 **In force 22 January 2026**; covers high-impact AI with transparency and human-oversight duties. Enforcement decrees still being finalised, **guidance-first grace period through 2026** | High-impact framework reaches education |
| **Vietnam** | **Law 134/2025/QH15** | 🟢 **Effective 1 March 2026**; risk-based framework | — |
| **Vietnam** | **Decision 33/2026/QD-TTg** | 🟢 In force | 🟢 **Yes — names education among six sectors holding high-risk AI systems, citing automated assessment explicitly.** The most specific education-AI classification found in any region this pass |
| **China** | Registration requirements + content-labelling rules | In force | Most comprehensive current framework |
| **Japan** | Voluntary, innovation-first | — | No binding education instrument |
| **Australia** | No AI-specific statute; **mandatory guardrails proposed**, covering education. **TEQSA** requires higher-ed providers to file **action plans addressing generative-AI risks** | 🟢 TEQSA requirement active | 🟢 Yes, via the regulator |
| **Singapore** | **AI Verify** toolkits (testing, not statute) | In force | — |
| **New Zealand** | Updates existing privacy/consumer rules rather than new AI law | — | — |

**Market:** China, India and Japan are the principal markets; APAC is the **fastest-growing
region** on the ResearchAndMarkets trajectory. 🔵 A single market-research vendor names
Google, Microsoft, IBM, Pearson and Byju's as key players — **indicative only, one source,
not adopted as a market map.** 🔴 Regional data-protection gaps on student data are flagged
by the same vendor; India's DPDP regime is cited for children's data and consent, phased
through 2026.

🟢 **The offer.** *Automated-assessment assurance.* Vietnam's Decision 33 names **automated
assessment** as high-risk, and Korea's Act imposes **human-oversight** duties — so the
sellable work is the oversight layer around grading, not the grading. This is the one
region where the shelf's assessment and psychometric rows are a regulatory requirement
rather than a nicety: [`catsim`](https://github.com/douglasrizzo/catsim) (BSD-3) to
simulate item exposure **before** learners see it,
[`pyKT`](https://github.com/pykt-team/pykt-toolkit) (MIT) for defensible learner models,
[`nbgrader`](https://github.com/jupyter/nbgrader) (BSD-3) for an auditable autograding
trail. 🔵 APAC also *produces* most of this shelf: DeepTutor, OpenMAIC, word-atlas,
Study-Mate, educhain, pyKT, EduCDM, gen-mentor, `kaogong-skill`, `cram-engine`, BCTK and
`universal-examprep-skill` are all APAC-origin — a partnering and contribution opportunity,
not just a market.


🆕 **`p587`:** 🔴 **Forrester expects no common APAC-wide AI law**; the 2026 regional frame is **sovereignty, security, responsible deployment**. 🟢 Market series **$591.6 M (2024) → $1,848.1 M (2029), 20.9 % CAGR** — the only internally consistent regional series the legs returned this pass, and the slowest CAGR on this page. 🟢 **The in-language repository supply is real and measured: 67 repos above 10★** (`教育 AI`), 42 above 20★ (`教育 智能`) — which is why APAC engagements can **adopt** rather than build (see the `p587` section above).

### LATAM

🆕 **`p591` — the gap is real but it is not the gap this base has been describing.** `591-C`
refines `p587`: LATAM **does** have in-language supply, and it splits by artefact type —
`12 of 12` courseware/curriculum repos permissive, 🔴 **`6 of 6` conversational AI tutors
with no licence at all** ([`Parlo`](https://github.com/andresmancilla08/Parlo),
[`fluir`](https://github.com/corleone000000/fluir), [`nexo-ia`](https://github.com/s64965264-code/nexo-ia),
[`linguaai-coach`](https://github.com/jccastel12-ctrl/linguaai-coach),
[`OSAPHLA`](https://github.com/CryptoJones/OSAPHLA),
[`questoesConcursos`](https://github.com/rodrigoborgesmachado/questoesConcursos)).
🟢 **So the engagement shape is: adopt the curriculum, build the tutor.** Adopt
[`lucianoon/llm-course`](https://github.com/lucianoon/llm-course) (Apache-2.0, Portuguese —
the largest in-language row found, `19★`),
[`tacianosilva/engenharia-software`](https://github.com/tacianosilva/engenharia-software)
(MIT, UFRN Brazil), [`kenzofujimoto/mackseguro`](https://github.com/kenzofujimoto/mackseguro)
(MIT, Mackenzie) and [`Escama-Code/jacarEV3`](https://github.com/Escama-Code/jacarEV3)
(MIT — the only physical-robotics teaching row in this base, and a strong K-12 STEM hook).
🔴 **Scale caveat, stated so this is not over-sold:** every in-language row is `≤ 19★` and
three sit at `0★`. 🟢 Demand remains the strongest of any region (IESALC `87 %`, DEC `92 %`
students / `79 %` faculty) against `45 %` institutional guidance — **the governance deficit
is the sellable artefact**, and `questoesConcursos` shows the public-exam demand that
APAC's `kaogong-skill` (MIT) already serves under a grant.

🆕 **`p590` — the demand-side number that completes this page's LATAM thesis.** The
**Digital Education Council's "AI in Higher Education LATAM Survey `2026`" reports `92 %`
of respondents using AI tools**, and describes the region as more positive toward AI in
higher education than any other (SECONDARY). 🟢 **Set against `p587`'s measured `33×`
in-language supply deficit, LATAM is the region with the highest appetite and the thinnest
Spanish/Portuguese supply** — which is an opening, not a risk. 🔴 The two IGO instruments
that would corroborate it (UNESCO IESALC + UNU-IAS, September `2026`; ECLAC, October `2025`)
are LEADS: both hosts return `000` (`583-E`, now `19` hosts). 🔴 The *"Rest of Latin
America"* USD `18` m → `36` m segment figure is **rejected as unquotable**.

🟢 **The region with the widest adoption-governance gap in the world, measured twice by
independent instruments that agree.**

🆕 **`p588`:** 🟡 **Two leads, neither verifiable here, and one of them would bear on this region's load-bearing finding.** **Piaí, Brazil** reportedly runs a *mandatory* weekly AI subject in state public high schools and released its teacher textbook and video lessons under **Creative Commons** — which, if it holds, is the first LATAM state-level AI curriculum with **openly licensed** materials in this base, against `p587`'s finding that in-language openly licensed supply is ≈ zero. 🔴 `link.springer.com` (AIED 2026) does not resolve here, so it is **a lead, not a fact**; the retry route that avoids the publisher is SEDUC-PI and the materials' own host. 🔵 Also noted: a Google.org commitment across nine LATAM countries targeting **1.25 M students by 2028** — a funding announcement, not an instrument, and it changes no verdict here.

| Measure | Figure | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Student AI use | **92%** (up from 86% global, 2024) | Digital Education Council **2026 LATAM survey**: >30,000 responses, 29 institutions, with Tecnológico de Monterrey / Institute for the Future of Education |
| Faculty AI use | **79%** (+18% vs the 2025 global figure) | same survey |
| Weakest use case | **Assessment** — cheating detection and feedback generation have the *lowest* adoption | same survey |
| Institutions using AI in ≥1 area | **87%** | **UNESCO IESALC** regional study (with UNU-IAS) |
| Governance | 🔴 **Adoption is driven by faculty, researchers and students — not by institutional policy or strategy** | UNESCO IESALC |
| National strategies | **85%** of 20 economies have an AI strategy in place or in development; 🔴 **only Brazil and Colombia explicitly allocate budget** in their national instruments | regional AI index |
| Curricula | **More than half** have incorporated AI into school curricula | regional AI index |
| Leaders | **Brazil, Chile, Uruguay** lead the regional index; **Colombia, Argentina** among adopters | regional AI index |
| 🔴 Headwind | Latin America faces widening **AI inequality** as computing and adoption gaps grow (October 2026) | regional tech press |

🟢 **The offer.** *Governance and enablement, not tooling.* Adoption is already near
universal, so nobody needs to be sold AI; what is missing is the institutional layer —
policy, assessment integrity, staff capability and procurement. 🟢 Two shelf rows are
**built in the region and curriculum-aligned**, which is worth more in a proposal than
their star counts suggest:
[`pedagogy-benchmark`](https://github.com/AI-for-Education/pedagogy-benchmark) (MIT),
built from exams by **Chile's Agencia de la Calidad de la Educación and CPEIP** — the only
instrument on the shelf that measures a model's *pedagogical* knowledge — and
[`libro-3d-interactivo`](https://github.com/csepulvedai/libro-3d-interactivo) (MIT,
Universidad de Los Lagos), aligned to Chilean curriculum objective **OA 13**. 🟢 Against
the computing-inequality headwind, [`Kolibri`](https://github.com/learningequality/kolibri)
(MIT, offline-first) and `wordlink`'s offline 1.5B model are the right substrate; the
assessment weakness is exactly where `catsim` (BSD-3, **Brazil**) and `nbgrader` apply.
🔴 Moodle and `rosariosis` dominate the installed base and are both copyleft — attach, do
not fork.


🆕 **`p587`:** 🔴 **The in-language open-source supply is effectively zero and now measured:** two Iberian-language education-AI repositories above 10★, **neither adoptable** (`SophIA` 15★ unlicensed and stale; `FM849` 14★ `NOASSERTION`, a course repo), against **67** in the Chinese-language control — a ~**33×** asymmetry. **A LATAM delivery is a localisation or a BUILD, never an in-language adopt.** 🟢 Demand evidence is strong and dated: **UNESCO IESALC / UNU-IAS working paper (`2026-09-01`, Valentini)**, **ILIA 2025** (ECLAC, published 2026), **AI in Higher Education LATAM Survey 2026** (Tec de Monterrey, Feb 2026) reporting the region as more positive on AI in higher education than any other. 🔴 Do **not** quote the *"Rest of Latin America"* market figure ($18.2 M → ~$36 M) as a LATAM total: its scope excludes Brazil and Mexico.

### Global

🟡 **Provenance note:** rows in this subsection are `Global` by scope; where an individual
figure's provenance is unknown or secondary it is labelled on the row itself. 🆕 `p592`
renamed this heading from *"Global / provenance unknown"*, which was outside the closed
region vocabulary and caused `gate-region-headings` to report both an `OUT-OF-VOCAB` finding
and a `MISSING-REGION` finding for `Global` on the same block.

🆕 **`p588`:** 🔴 **This page's regional legs are saturated at `20 of 24`** — `83 %` of everything this pass's six legs returned was already filed, and **none of the remaining four can be verified from this environment**. 🟢 **Operational reading: the marginal value of another education regional sweep is ≈ zero**, and the binding constraint is network policy (`588-B`), not research effort. See the `p588` section at the top of this page.

- 🟢 **Direction of travel, consistent across every source this pass:** from
  experimentation to **governance**. The question has moved from *whether* to use AI to
  *under what conditions*, with policies and data boundaries at the centre, and investment
  concentrating where instructional value is proven.
- 🟢 **Agents inside workflows**, not alongside them: lesson planning and assessment
  drafting embedded in the LMS rather than sold as separate tools.
- 🟢 **Skills-and-credentials pull**: AI used to map skill gaps and connect learners to
  verified digital credentials tied to workforce demand.
- 🔵 **Market size: $10.6B (2026) → ~$42.5B (2030) at ~40.9% CAGR**, North America leading,
  APAC fastest-growing (ResearchAndMarkets-derived). A **$12.3B** 2026 figure attributed to
  HolonIQ **could not be verified** and is not adopted.
- 🔴 **Source-quality warning that applies to this whole page.** Much of the available
  material is vendor and marketing content. Figures such as "83% of institutions" or "89%
  of new courses" appeared with **no primary citation** and were **excluded**, as was a
  LATAM enterprise article found to contain unfilled `[VERIFY]` placeholders. Every figure
  above carries its instrument.

# Education market intelligence

## p585 — 2026-11-01 — the mandated regional legs paid for the first time in 20 passes, and the APAC education leg is a declared nil

🟢 **Three of the four mandated regional legs returned usable, dated, attributable
intelligence this pass** — after a long run of nils recorded on this page. The one that did
not is written down as a nil below rather than left as silence, because an undeclared gap
is indistinguishable from coverage.

### Market sizing — the spread is the finding, not the midpoint

🔴 **Do not quote a single number for this market.** Six publishers reachable this pass
disagree by more than **3×** on the *current* year, which means the methodologies are
measuring different things (tutoring software only, vs. all institutional AI spend, vs.
consumer edtech included).

| Publisher / source | Current-year figure | Forward figure | Implied CAGR |
|---|---|---|---|
| Persistence Market Research | **US$3.7B (2026)** | US$14.1B by 2033 | ~21% |
| "AI in Education" aggregator (yoursocial) | **US$11.4B (2026)** | US$57.2B by 2033 | ~26% |
| Market-update secondary | **~US$11B (2026)** | — | — |
| Technavio / secondary | — | — | ~15.9% through 2029 |
| MarketsandMarkets (North America cut) | **US$3.68B (2026)**, 36% of global | — | ~31.1% through 2030 |
| Earlier baseline (various) | US$6.4B (2025) | — | — |

🔵 **The usable reading:** the global market is **single-digit to low-double-digit billions
USD in 2026**, growing in the **20–31%** range depending on scope, with **North America at
roughly 36–38% of it**. 🔴 **The vendor-only gap on this page does not close**: every figure
above is a vendor or market-research publication. No national statistics office or
multilateral body publishes a comparable series, so there is no independent anchor.

🟢 **One segment figure with a consistent source:** **K-12 is the largest segment at ~34%
share in 2026**.

### 🔴 Declared nil: the APAC education-specific leg

The mandated leg `AI education APAC 2026 adoption regulation players` returned **enterprise**
AI material almost exclusively — Boomi, TechRepublic, Forrester, OneTrust, Omdia — with no
education-specific regulation or market-player data. Written down explicitly:

- 🔴 **No APAC education-specific regulatory instrument was recoverable from the regional
  query form.** The only education-adjacent signal: Forrester expects **60% of firms and
  governments in major markets including India and China to run locally developed LLMs
  alongside global ones, naming education as a target sector**; and the **ASEAN Guide on AI
  Governance and Ethics** exists but is characterised as early-stage and non-binding.
- 🟢 **The reason, and the fix:** education regulation in APAC is promulgated **per
  jurisdiction**, not per region. The regional query form is the wrong instrument. This
  base's own earlier passes closed APAC coverage by querying *jurisdictions* (China, Japan,
  Korea, Philippines) in their own languages, which worked. **Future passes should run the
  per-jurisdiction form for APAC and treat the regional form as known-barren.**

## p584 — 2026-11-01 — **`583-D` closed: the publisher set enumerated at SEVEN, and the current-year spread is `3.0×` — plus four new jurisdictions, and the Philippines turns out to hold the most completely specified AI-in-education programme in this base**

### 🟢 `583-D` CLOSED — the publisher set, enumerated, with each one's scope beside its figure

`583-D` held that this base's market range was floor-truncated *because no pass had ever
enumerated the publishers*. Enumerated:

| # | publisher | figure carried | scope as stated by the publisher | first-hand? |
|--:|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Persistence Market Research** | **USD `3.7` bn (2026)** | AI in education; **K-12 ≈ `34 %` share**; forecast to 2033 | 🔴 no — `persistencemarketresearch.com` does not resolve here (`583-E`) |
| 2 | **The Business Research Company** | **USD `10.6` bn (2026)**, from **`7.52` bn (2025)** | AI in education global; implies **≈ `41 %` YoY** | 🔴 no — paywalled summary only |
| 3 | **New Market Pitch** | **≈ USD `11` bn (2026)** | built bottom-up from **AI tutors + educator copilots + assessment tools** | 🔴 no |
| 4 | **MarketsandMarkets** | **North America ≈ `36 %` share; ≈ USD `3.68` bn (2026)** | 🟡 **regional cut only** — not a global figure | 🔴 no |
| 5 | **Straits Research** | → USD `98.71` bn (2034), **`36.4 %` CAGR** | 🟡 **terminal year only**; carried from `p583` | 🔴 no |
| 6 | **Technavio** | — | AI in the education *sector*; scope not stated in reachable material | 🔴 no |
| 7 | **Grand View Research** | — | listed in report aggregators; figure not extractable here | 🔴 no |

🟢 **Current-year spread: USD `3.7` bn → `11` bn = `3.0×`.** 🔵 **Standing instruction,
replacing the old `9.6`–`11.4` bn floor:** quote **USD `3.7`–`11.4` bn for 2026, publisher
named every time**, and state that the spread is a property of scope disagreement, not of
measurement error — publisher 4 is a *regional* number and publisher 5 is a *terminal*
number, and both have been quoted in this market as though global and current.

🔴 **The mechanism that produced the false floor is now identified, and it is not
laziness.** The aggregator compilations this base's search legs return most often
(`azumo`, `techrt`, `eklavvya`, `aiindigo`) are **derived, not publishers** — they restate
other houses' figures without scope. A range assembled from them inherits whichever
subset they happened to restate. 🟢 **Rule: a figure enters this page only with a named
publishing house and a stated scope.** 🔴 **`583-D` is closed; the eighth-pass gap
"market sizing is vendor-only, no independent estimate exists" STANDS** — all seven
publishers are commercial, and the only non-commercial source located is the **OECD
Digital Education Outlook 2026**, which is qualitative (its finding: AI helps learning only
where pedagogy stays in control).

### 🟢 Four new jurisdictions, and the native-language method is now `17 of 17`

🔵 **Philippines is the headline, and it is the most completely specified programme in this
base** — instrument, scale, funding source, delivery partner and a stated policy failure
mode, all dated:

| element | detail |
|---|---|
| instrument | 🟢 **DepEd Order No. `003`, s. `2026` — "Foundational Guidelines on AI in Basic Education"**, issued **February 2026** |
| shape | **human-centred**; AI is a support tool, not a teacher replacement; 🟢 **graduated by learner independence** — more independent learners are given greater latitude |
| stated motive | 🔴 DepEd's own position: classroom AI use **had outpaced** the system's ability to put clear, unified and enforceable policy in place |
| capability | 🟢 **Google for Education partnership (June 2026): > `1` million educators and staff.** Three tiers — Fundamental, Advanced, and **"AI Mastery"** (Gemini + prompt engineering). Free; > `1` M already have Gemini access via official accounts |
| programme | 🟢 **Project AGAP.AI** — **ASEAN Foundation** funded, powered by **Google.org**; up to **`1.5` M participants in 2026** (`1.05` M learners, `300,000` teachers, plus parents). Launched by **President Marcos, January 2026** |
| still open | **August 2026:** DepEd preparing a **master plan** for AI implementation in schools |
| 🔴 declared unusable | a `40`-hour "ECAIR" figure and a "< `18 %` completion" figure, and a "$`3` bn bet" headline — all from a single developer-blog source, unverifiable against primary or news reporting |

🟢 **Why this matters structurally:** the Philippines pairs an **age/independence-graduated
obligation** (like Indonesia's SKB 7 Menteri) with a **>1 M-seat vendor capability
programme** (like Korea's, pre-downgrade). It is the first jurisdiction in this base where
the *obligation* and the *capability funding* are both specified and both current — which
makes it the cleanest reference engagement in APAC.

| 🆕 jurisdiction | instrument / programme | status |
|---|---|---|
| 🆕 **Türkiye** | MEB **Eğitimde Yapay Zekâ Politika Belgesi ve Eylem Planı** (AI in Education Policy Document and Action Plan); **YEĞİTEK** has stood up a **Yapay Zekâ ve Büyük Veri Uygulamaları Daire Başkanlığı** (AI & Big Data Applications Directorate); 2024 teacher handbook, 2025 AI-ethics recommendations, **2026 "YAZEK" ethics-declaration system**; TETZ summit June 2026 | 🔴 **Informed gap, stated:** the 2026–2027 circular signed by Minister Yusuf Tekin and sent to all `81` provinces **does not address AI**. Governance runs through a policy document, ethics guidance and an institutional unit — **not a binding circular**. Sell to YEĞİTEK, not to a compliance deadline |
| 🆕 **Thailand** | Ministry of Education × **AIS Academy for Thais** (July 2026): AI into classrooms, teacher upskilling, youth cyber-resilience. Targets **> `30,000` AI specialists** and **≥ `10` M people** with AI skills by **2570 (2027)**. **`5,855` IT teachers** trained, cascading to **> `430,000` students**. Policy, regulation and an ethics framework **planned** | 🔴 **Informed gaps, stated:** no full official ministry policy document located; and **minister attribution conflicts between sources** (ประเสริฐ จันทรรวงทอง cited 16 July 2026) — flagged, not resolved. The engagement here is **corporate-partnership-shaped**, not procurement-shaped |
| 🆕 **Bangladesh** | **UGC** (University Grants Commission) initiative, reported **23 April 2026**, to draft a **national policy** on ethical and responsible AI use in universities — teaching, research and administration. Workshop **British Council**-funded, co-hosted by **BUET** and **AIUB** | 🔴 **Informed gap, stated:** **higher education only.** No ministry gazette, directive or 2026 instrument found at school or college level; the country self-assesses as at an "emerging stage" with weak policy and regulation. 🔵 Donor-funded capability, same shape as LATAM's GENIAL |

### 🔴 Nineteenth consecutive nil on the mandated legs, fourth on the regional ones

`4` global + `3` regional mandated legs → **`0` new addresses**; every candidate already in
this base, checked by token first (`GarethManning/education-agent-skills` FILED with `398`
history mentions, `OPTIMETA/PAIDEIA` and `rohitg00/ai-engineering-from-scratch` both
already shelved). 🔴 **The APAC region-level leg returned enterprise AI only, with nothing
on education** — consistent with `580-F`'s retirement of that leg in favour of
per-jurisdiction queries, and declared rather than left silent.

🟢 **But the legs were not worthless this pass, and that is a change worth recording:**
they produced **`9` usable intel tokens** (below) against `0` addresses. 🔵 **Revised
reading of the nineteen-pass nil: the mandated legs are a demand-intelligence channel that
this base has been grading as a supply channel.** They have never been good at finding
repositories and they are consistently adequate at finding figures and instruments.

🟢 **Against the native-language legs: `4` new jurisdictions from `4` legs — now `17 of 17`
productive across three passes.**

### 🔵 Demand intelligence by region, with gaps declared

**North America** — 🆕 **`134` AI bills tracked across `31` states as of March 2026**
(privacy, classroom use, curriculum). 🆕 **US Department of Education released `$169` M in
FIPSE awards, January 2026**, supporting responsible AI use in higher education — a named,
dated federal funding line, which this base has had almost none of. 🆕 **Gallup, April
2026: > `60 %` of college students use AI weekly for coursework while roughly half of
their institutions officially discourage or prohibit it** — 🔵 the governance gap, now with
a US number attached. Market: **≈ `36 %`** of global spend, **≈ USD `3.68` bn (2026)**.

**EMEA** — 🆕 **The European Commission updated its ethical guidelines on AI in education
in June 2026** — education-specific, dated, and the most directly citable EU education
instrument this base has found. 🆕 **Three Advanced Digital Skills grants worth `€25.4` M**
signed. 🆕 **UK: `£200` M+ AI adoption commitment announced at its first AI Adoption
Summit**, plus partnerships with technology companies and trade unions for workplace AI
training. EU AI Act **AI-literacy obligations have been live since `2025-02-02`**.
**≈ `70 %` of institutions in Europe and North America have or are developing AI guidance.**

**APAC** — the Philippines table above. Regional-level: **ASEAN Guide on AI Governance and
Ethics** remains early-stage; **Australia's National AI Plan** and new **Chief AI Officer**
roles reshape adoption. 🔴 **No education-specific APAC regional instrument found** —
the region is governed jurisdiction by jurisdiction, as `Trend 23` predicts.

**LATAM** — 🆕 **Digital Education Council × Tecnológico de Monterrey, February 2026:
`92 %` of students and `79 %` of faculty actively using AI, `67 %` of students weekly,
across `29` universities.** 🆕 **UNESCO IESALC: `200` institutions in `19` countries
(Aug–Oct 2025)** — adoption/governance gap especially pronounced. 🆕 **ILIA (Latin America
AI Index) 2025**, published via the **IDB**, tracks readiness, adoption and governance
across `19` countries — a reusable regional baseline. 🆕 **ECLAC/CEPAL, October 2025:**
remaining gaps in data, training and regulation. 🔴 **Informed gap, stated: no 2026
education-specific AI *regulatory instrument* was found for any LATAM jurisdiction in this
leg, and nothing on national K-12 policy or on the regional edtech vendor landscape.** 🔵
That absence is itself the sales thesis — the region buys **policy capability**, because it
has adoption without governance.

### (historical) Opportunities by region — superseded, retained for history

### North America

🟢 **The buying unit is the state, the gate is an evaluation lab, and there is now a
federal funding line to name.** `28`+ states plus DC have K-12 AI guidance; Ohio (`HB 96`)
and Florida (`SB 1194`) **require districts to adopt formal AI policies by July 2026**;
Tennessee extends the duty to every public higher-education governing body, LEA and charter
school; **Delaware's AI Assurance Lab** tests tools against state guardrails before
districts may be advised to buy. 🆕 **`134` bills across `31` states (March 2026)** and
🆕 **`$169` M in FIPSE awards (January 2026)** for responsible AI in higher education.
🟢 **Offer:** the **conformance-and-assurance package** —
`adlnet/lrs-conformance-test-suite` (MIT) and `adlnet/CATAPULT` (Apache-2.0) as technical
evidence, the jurisdiction refusal-and-provenance defaults as policy evidence, bundled as
the artefact a district files. 🆕 **Add the guardian-facing tier this pass opened:**
`noestudios/lastbell` (MIT) is a ParentVUE+Canvas monitor, and parent-visible AI is the
part of the US stack nobody has shipped. 🆕 **And `academic-innovation/django-lti` (MIT,
University of Michigan) makes LTI integration quotable as a connector** for Django tools.

### EMEA

🟢 **Two motions, and one is a land-grab before the rules are written.** (1) **Poland:**
no AI guidance yet, curriculum reform lands `2026-09-01`, **12,000 AI labs** being
distributed — a vendor present before MEN publishes sets the de-facto default.
(2) **Saudi Arabia:** curriculum in force since SY 2025-26 with **learning outcomes counted
in overall assessment**, MCIT/SDAIA co-sponsored — so the engagement is **assessment
integration**, not awareness training. **Nigeria** is programmatic, not regulatory
(Experience AI's `1,142` educators across five states). 🆕 **The EC's June 2026 updated
ethical guidelines on AI in education are now the citable instrument for a pan-EU bid**,
and 🆕 **the UK's `£200` M+ commitment plus `€25.4` M in EU digital-skills grants are the
named funding lines.** EU: `2 August 2026` transparency duties live; education high-risk
route moves to `2 December 2027`. 🟢 **Offer:** provenance logging now, high-risk
conformity later. 🆕 **Build on `SafeExamBrowser` (MPL-2.0, ETH Zürich)** — EMEA-origin,
sector-standard, and the only licensed lockdown client anywhere.
🔴 **Standing regional constraint:** the maintained QTI renderer
(`Citolab/qti-components`, NL) and the maintained LTI 1.3 PHP core (`oat-sa/lib-lti1p3-core`,
FR) are both **copyleft** — the two EMEA components a client most likely already runs are
the two a studio cannot ship.

### APAC

🟢 **The region with the firmest instruments, and now with a complete reference
engagement.** 🆕 **The Philippines is the cleanest: DepEd Order No. `003`, s. `2026`
(graduated by learner independence) + a `1` M-educator Google capability programme +
AGAP.AI at `1.5` M participants.** **Vietnam:** AI content nationwide from SY 2026-27,
`12` periods/class/year, and **three named build obligations** — age-appropriateness
vetting, data/IP/infosec compliance, and **an AI incident-handling process**, which is a
product (logging, triage, escalation, audit trail), not a policy. **Indonesia's SKB 7
Menteri** covers formal, non-formal *and* informal education with **age-tiered limits on
duration and content type** — a single product needs **per-age-band enforcement**.
🆕 **Thailand** is partnership-shaped (AIS Academy; `5,855` teachers → `430,000` students).
🔴 **Korea remains the price of selling to a mandate:** `34.2 % → 5.2 %` in one year,
`₩1.4` tn spent, instrument **reclassified rather than repealed**. 🟢 **Rule: price off
funded programmes that survived a downgrade, never off a mandate's existence.**
🆕 **Supply note:** `PsychQuant/che-ntucool-mcp` (MIT) shows the attach-boundary pattern
working against a *named national university's* LMS tenancy — the reusable APAC demo.

### LATAM

🟢 **Adoption is high, governance is absent, and the money is increasingly external.**
🆕 **`92 %` of students and `79 %` of faculty actively using AI, `67 %` weekly, `29`
universities (Feb 2026)** against **only `30 %` of universities with a published AI
policy**; `55 %` of institutions with no AI budget, public universities at `4 %`;
🆕 **IESALC across `200` institutions in `19` countries**; 🆕 **ILIA 2025 (IDB)** as the
readiness baseline; 🆕 **ECLAC (Oct 2025)** on data, training and regulation gaps.
🟢 **The funding route is the finding: GENIAL, EU-funded, is standing up AI units inside
higher-education institutions in Guatemala, Ecuador and Colombia** — the buyer may be a
**European development programme**, not the university. 🟢 **Offer:** the
**policy-and-capability package** — a published AI policy, an assessment-integrity posture,
and the interchange layer on the Moodle/Chamilo estate the region already runs — sold into
donor-funded programmes and consortium procurement. 🆕 **Regional component added this

`douglasrizzo/catsim` (BSD-3-Clause, Brazil) as live, permissive, LATAM-maintained code to

### Global / provenance unknown

## p583 — 2026-11-01 — **a sixth publisher lands `2.6×` BELOW this page's published floor, five native-language legs add four jurisdictions, and Korea's reversal finally has a number**

🟢 **Ten legs run: five native-language per-jurisdiction, four mandated global, three
mandated regional** (APAC region-level **retired** under `580-F` and replaced by
per-jurisdiction queries). Records:
[`native-language-legs`](../evidence/native-language-legs.2026-11-01-p583.tsv),
[`host-reachability`](../evidence/host-reachability.2026-11-01-p583.tsv).

### 🔴 The market-sizing range on this page has a floor that is `2.6×` too high

This page prescribes: *"quote **USD `9.6`–`11.4` bn for 2026** as a publisher range, always
name the publisher"*. 🔴 **A sixth publisher sits below that floor:**

| publisher | 2026 | terminal | CAGR |
|---|--:|---|--:|
| Grand View Research | USD 11.4 bn | USD 57.2 bn (2033) | 25.9 % |
| Research and Markets | USD 10.6 bn | USD 42.48 bn (2030) | 41.5 % |
| Precedence Research | USD 9.58 bn | USD 136.79 bn (2035) | 34.52 % |
| IMARC | 🆕 **USD 9.0 bn** | USD 79.6 bn (2034) | 31.35 % |
| 🆕 **Straits Research** | — | 🆕 **USD 98.71 bn (2034)** | 🆕 **36.4 %** (2026–34) |
| 🆕 🔴 **Persistence Market Research** | 🔴 **USD 3.7 bn** | USD 14.1 bn (2033) | — |

🔴 **The current-year publisher range is therefore USD `3.7`–`11.4` bn — a `3.1×` spread
in 2026 itself**, not the `1.19×` the standing guidance implies. 🔵 The base's own
headline was *"terminal values differ by over `3×`"*; the **near-year** figures now differ
by as much, which is the more damaging fact, because a near-year number is the one quoted
in a proposal.
🟢 **Revised guidance, replacing the `9.6`–`11.4` prescription:** quote **USD `3.7`–`11.4`
bn for 2026**, name the publisher, and state the scope caveat — the cause remains the one
this page already identified, that an *"AI-core"* scope excludes general-purpose AI tools
and back-office systems, which is most of what an institution deploys.
🔴 **Unverified first-hand:** `persistencemarketresearch.com` **does not resolve from this
session** (`getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND`), so the `3.7` figure rests on a search-result summary
and is marked accordingly. It is reported because **excluding it is what produced a
falsely narrow range**, not because it is the better estimate.
🔴 **Still barred:** the `83 %`-of-institutions figure attributed to EDUCAUSE remains
**unusable**. 🔴 **New to the barred list:** a *"Students First Act"* described as a
national K-12 AI policy passed by students from all 50 states — one headline-only source,
no instrument, **unconfirmable → unusable**.

### 🟢 Demand evidence: four new jurisdictions with dated instruments

🟢 **The native-language channel paid for the fourth pass running.** `8` of `8` legs were
productive in `p582`; `5` legs this pass returned **4 instruments and 2 informed gaps**.

| jurisdiction | region | instrument | date | the procurement-relevant term |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 🆕 **Indonesia** | **APAC** | **SKB 7 Menteri** (joint decree of seven ministers) **No. 400.1-492 Tahun 2026** | signed `2026-03-12`, issued `2026-03-16` | national guidance across **formal, non-formal and informal** education, early-childhood to higher ed; AI **may not replace the teacher**; 🔴 **age-tiered limits on both duration and content type**, aligned to **PP TUNAS** |
| 🆕 **Vietnam** | **APAC** | **Công văn 5588/BGDĐT-GDPT** with **Quyết định 2422/QĐ-BGDĐT** | `2026-08-19` / `2026-08-18` | AI content **nationwide from school year 2026-27**, `12` periods/class/year core; schools must vet age-appropriateness, comply with personal-data, IP and infosec rules, and 🔴 **stand up an AI incident-handling process**; implements Politburo Resolution **71-NQ/TW** (`2025-08-22`) |
| 🆕 **Saudi Arabia** | **EMEA** | National Curriculum Center with Ministry of Education, **MCIT** and **SDAIA** — AI curriculum, all general-education stages | announced `2025-07`, effective SY **2025-26** | age-tiered units to model design, AI ethics and ML; 🟢 **learning outcomes counted in overall student assessment** — so assessment integration is in scope, not optional |
| 🆕 **Korea** | **APAC** | Aug 2025 statutory amendment + Nov 2025 State Council amendment to the Regulation on Textbooks | `2025-08` / `2025-11` | see the quantified reversal below |

🔴 **Two informed gaps — written down because silence looks like coverage:**

- 🔴 **Poland (EMEA) — no instrument exists.** MEN has **not** issued AI guidance.
  Responsible AI use and digital hygiene sit in the **2026/2027 education-policy
  directions** (direction 4) beside the **Kompas Jutra** curriculum reform; spokesperson
  **Ewelina Gorczyca** announced guidelines "still this year"; the reform reaches primary
  schools `2026-09-01` and upper-secondary a year later; **12,000 "AI labs"** are to be
  distributed. 🔵 **Poland explicitly diverged from the Danish school-AI blocking model** —
  so the posture is permissive-with-guidance, and the guidance is the thing not yet
  written. **A vendor arriving before the guidance sets the default.**
- 🔴 **Nigeria (EMEA) — no federal instrument found.** Governance is fragmented across the
  Federal Ministry of Education and other bodies; a 2025 curriculum reform reportedly made
  AI, robotics and digital literacy core (secondary source, body unnamed); a National AI
  policy and **3MTT** exist but education coordination is assessed as weak; a **Draft
  National Higher Education AI Framework** and an **NERDC** curriculum-revision
  recommendation are reported without status. 🟢 **The live channel is programmatic, not
  regulatory:** **Experience AI** (NerdzFactory with the Raspberry Pi Foundation and Google
  DeepMind) trained **1,142** public secondary educators across **five south-western
  states** in year one and expands in 2026, with state-ministry backing.
- 🔴 **UAE — DECLARED UNUSABLE.** The only source for a standalone KG–12 "AI and
  Technology" subject in SY 2026-27 is a **vendor marketing page**. No ministry or
  news-agency confirmation. Not carried as intelligence.

### 🟢 Korea: the first measured magnitude of a mandate reversal

🟢 **`p582`'s third priority, executed — and it is confirmed with numbers this base did not
have.** `p582` recorded the reclassification and that provincial budgets were cut; it had
**no adoption figure**. Measured this pass:

| semester | schools using AI digital textbooks | share |
|---|--:|--:|
| 1H 2025 | **4,095** | **34.2 %** |
| 1H 2026 | 🔴 **622** | 🔴 **5.2 %** |

🔴 **An `85 %` collapse in adoption in one year, after roughly `₩1.4` trillion over three
years.** 🟢 **The mechanism, now specific:** the August 2025 amendment narrowed 교과용 도서
(textbook) to books and e-books, reclassifying AI learning-support software as 교육자료
(educational material); the certification and approval provisions were **deleted from the
enforcement decree**; a November 2025 State Council amendment moved the definition and
scope out of the decree and into the statute. 🔴 **The procurement consequence is the
funding route, not the label:** schools now subscribe **from existing school operating
budgets**, choosing against every other material — so there is no line item to win.
🔴 **The primary document is still not obtained**, and the reason is measured, not a
research failure: `law.go.kr` and `moe.go.kr` return `000`, **as does a fabricated control
host**, so the probe is non-discriminating. Effective dates differ between sources and the
gap is carried, not closed.

### 🔵 The mandated legs, and what they were worth

| leg class | legs | new addresses | new intel tokens |
|---|--:|--:|--:|
| 🔴 mandated global (listicle) | 4 | **0** | `1` (unusable) |
| 🔴 mandated regional | 3 | **0** | **`2`** |
| 🟢 native-language per-jurisdiction | 5 | n/a | **`10+`** |

🔴 **Eighteenth consecutive nil on the mandated supply legs.** The two usable tokens from
three regional legs: **Delaware's AI Assurance Lab**, which tests AI tools for K-12 inside
state-set guardrails before recommending them to districts — 🟢 **a state acting as the
evaluation authority, which is a procurement gate a vendor must pass**; and **GENIAL**, an
EU-funded project standing up AI units in higher-education institutions in **Guatemala,
Ecuador and Colombia** — 🟢 EU money building LATAM institutional capacity, i.e. an EMEA
budget with a LATAM delivery surface. 🔵 Everything else the regional legs returned was
already on these shelves: Regulation (EU) `2026/1744` and `2 December 2027`, the Digital
Omnibus, the emotion-recognition prohibition, Ohio `HB 96`, Florida `SB 1194`, California
`AB 2876`/`AB 1159`, the `134`-bill count, UNESCO IESALC's `200` institutions, the Digital
Education Council LATAM survey, the `55 %`-no-AI-budget figure.

### (historical) Opportunities by region — superseded, retained for history

### North America

🟢 **The buying unit is the state, and the gate is an evaluation lab.** More than `28`
states plus DC have K-12 AI guidance; Ohio (`HB 96`) and Florida (`SB 1194`) **require
districts to adopt formal AI policies by July 2026**; Tennessee extends the duty to every
public higher-education governing body, LEA and charter school. 🆕 **Delaware's AI
Assurance Lab tests tools against state guardrails before districts may be advised to buy**
— so "passes a state assurance review" is becoming a shippable property, not a marketing
line. 🟢 **Offer:** a **conformance-and-assurance package** — `adlnet/lrs-conformance-test-suite`
(MIT) and `adlnet/CATAPULT` (Apache-2.0) as the technical evidence, the four-jurisdiction
refusal-and-provenance defaults as the policy evidence, bundled as the artefact a district
files. 🔵 Region is also the largest market at **`36`–`37.5 %`** of global spend, and
**K-12 is the leading segment at ~`34 %` (2026)** — the segment where the binding duties
actually landed.

### EMEA

🟢 **Two distinct motions, and one of them is a land-grab before the rules are written.**
(1) 🆕 **Poland: no AI guidance exists yet**, the curriculum reform lands `2026-09-01`,
and **12,000 AI labs** are being distributed — 🟢 **a vendor present before MEN publishes
sets the de-facto default**, and Poland has already rejected the Danish blocking model.
(2) 🆕 **Saudi Arabia has a curriculum in force since SY 2025-26** with **learning outcomes
counted in overall assessment** and MCIT/SDAIA co-sponsorship — 🟢 so the engagement is
**assessment integration**, not awareness training, and it has a national sponsor.
🆕 **Nigeria is a programmatic market, not a regulatory one**: Experience AI's `1,142`
educators across five states with state-ministry backing is the channel; the federal
instrument does not exist. 🔵 EU: `2 August 2026` transparency duties are live and the
education high-risk route moves to `2 December 2027` under Regulation (EU) `2026/1744`, so
EMEA engagements price **provenance logging now, high-risk conformity later**.
🔴 Note the permissive-licence consequence for this region specifically: the maintained
QTI **renderer** (`Citolab/qti-components`, Netherlands) and the maintained LTI 1.3 PHP
core (`oat-sa/lib-lti1p3-core`, France) are both **copyleft** — the two EMEA-origin
components a client is most likely to already run are the two a studio cannot ship.

### APAC

🟢 **The region with the firmest instruments and the sharpest cautionary tale.**
🆕 **Vietnam is the cleanest near-term engagement in the base:** AI content nationwide from
**SY 2026-27**, `12` periods/class/year, and **three named build obligations** — age-
appropriateness vetting, personal-data/IP/infosec compliance, and **an AI incident-handling
process**. 🟢 That last one is a product, not a policy: logging, triage, escalation and
audit trail. 🆕 **Indonesia's SKB 7 Menteri covers formal, non-formal *and* informal
education** with **age-tiered limits on duration and content type** — so a single product
needs **per-age-band enforcement**, which is a build requirement most vendors do not have.
🔴 **And Korea is the price of selling to a mandate:** `34.2 % → 5.2 %` adoption in one
year, `₩1.4` tn spent, because the instrument was **reclassified rather than repealed** and
the funding moved into general school budgets. 🟢 **Rule for the region: price off funded
programmes that survived a downgrade** (Korea's K-education AI and its `1,000`
AI-focused schools), **never off a mandate's existence.**

### LATAM

🟢 **Adoption is high, governance is absent, and the money is increasingly external.**
Already on these shelves: `92 %` of students using AI regularly and `67 %` weekly against
**only `30 %` of universities with a published AI policy**; `55 %` of institutions with no
AI budget and public universities at `4 %`; UNESCO IESALC across `200` institutions in `19`
countries. 🆕 **The new fact is the funding route: GENIAL, EU-funded, is standing up AI
units inside higher-education institutions in Guatemala, Ecuador and Colombia** — so the
buyer of institutional AI capability in LATAM may be a **European development programme**,
not the university. 🟢 **Offer:** the **policy-and-capability package** — a published AI
policy, an assessment-integrity posture, and the interchange layer on the Moodle/Chamilo
estate the region already runs — sold into donor-funded programmes and consortium
procurement rather than single-institution budgets. 🔵 `douglasrizzo/catsim`

### Global / provenance unknown

🔵 **Read "Global" on any row in this base as *provenance unknown*, never as
