Australia is moving to (see `APAC`). Grade `C` (vendor and consultancy sources;
the standard itself was not read).

🟢 **The origin result, and it is this base's strongest EMEA supply finding to
date: `7` of the layer's `29` addresses are EMEA-origin**, against `1` for North
America and `0` established for APAC and LATAM.

| address | grant (first-hand) | origin |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [OpenOLAT/OpenOLAT](https://github.com/OpenOLAT/OpenOLAT) | **Apache-2.0** (`10,982 B`) | frentix GmbH, Switzerland |
| [liip/TheA11yMachine](https://github.com/liip/TheA11yMachine) | 🟡 manifest-only `BSD-3-Clause`, no licence file | Liip AG, Switzerland |
| [ContentSquare/readapt](https://github.com/ContentSquare/readapt) | **Apache-2.0** (`11,357 B`) | Contentsquare, France |
| [mthebaud/predict4all](https://github.com/mthebaud/predict4all) | **Apache-2.0** (`11,357 B` at `LICENCE`) | France |
| [asterics/Asterics-AAC](https://github.com/asterics/Asterics-AAC) | 🔴 **AGPL-3.0** (`34,523 B`) | AsTeRICS, EU-funded (Austria) |
| [oat-sa/tao-core](https://github.com/oat-sa/tao-core) + [extension-tao-itemqti](https://github.com/oat-sa/extension-tao-itemqti) | 🔴 **GPL-2.0** (`18,025 B`) | OAT SA, Luxembourg |
| [Citolab/qti-components](https://github.com/Citolab/qti-components) | 🔴 **GPL-3.0** (`35,199 B`) | Citolab / Cito, Netherlands |

🟢 **Two of the seven are permissive and both are French**, which is the first
time this base can name a *country* for permissive EMEA supply in a layer rather
than a single repository. 🔴 **But the platform tier is copyleft across the
board** — TAO (`GPL-2.0`), `qti-components` (`GPL-3.0`), Asterics (`AGPL-3.0`,
with a §13 network obligation on any hosted deployment) — so the EMEA engagement
is an integration-and-assurance engagement on client-hosted copyleft, not a
product build. Recipe: [`../compose/patterns.md`](../compose/patterns.md)
`P-576-B`.

🟡 **Policy signals from the mandated leg, single secondary (one newsletter), grade
`C` — recorded, not quotable:** the European Commission updating its ethical
guidelines on AI in education and signing three Advanced Digital Skills grant
agreements worth **€25.4m**; the UK's first AI Adoption Summit committing
**£200m+**, of which **£100m** expands Bridge AI and **£53m** goes to regional
initiatives. 🔴 **Both carry day-and-month dates (`9 June`, `8 June`) with no
year** in the source, so the year is **not established** — the same
survey-year-versus-publication-year defect `572-D` registered for Cetic.br.


🟢 **New in the eighth pass (`p570`): EMEA has locally published, permissively
granted assessment science — three countries, read from the grants themselves.**
This base recorded *"EMEA gained no placed row"* for three consecutive passes.
🔴 **That was an instrument gap, not a supply gap**: the rows were already in-tree
and already licence-read, and only their region column was blank. Running
`569-D`'s probe — the institutional email domain in a CRAN package's
`DESCRIPTION` → `Authors@R` field — placed them:

| Asset | Named entity in the grant | Country | Why an EMEA engagement cares |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [`dexter-psychometrics/dexter`](https://github.com/dexter-psychometrics/dexter) | **Cito** (`cito.nl`) | 🇳🇱 Netherlands | 🟢 **A national testing institute publishes its own test-analysis stack.** The strongest institutional provenance available for an EMEA assessment conversation — the same class of publisher as France Université Numérique, whose `ralph`/`warren`/`potsie` this base already carries |
| [`cran/TAM`](https://github.com/cran/TAM) | **IPN Leibniz Institute for Science and Mathematics Education** (`ipn.uni-kiel.de`) | 🇩🇪 Germany | Test Analysis Modules from the institute that runs German large-scale assessment participation |
| [`yrosseel/lavaan`](https://github.com/yrosseel/lavaan) | **Ghent University** (`UGent.be`) | 🇧🇪 Belgium | The reference latent-variable / SEM implementation; ⚠️ **GPL-2+, so attach-only for a proprietary derivative** |

🔵 **The commercial sentence this changes.** Before this pass, an EMEA
assessment engagement was offered US- and APAC-origin psychometrics and told that
local supply did not exist. **It does, it is published by named public research
institutions in three member states, and `dexter` is permissive.** For a
procurement conversation under the **AI Act Annex III** evaluation-of-learning-outcomes
category — whose obligations this base prices against **2 December 2027** —
locally published, institutionally authored tooling is materially easier to
defend than imported tooling, independent of its technical merits.

🔴 **Say the limits in the same breath.** These are **R packages for
psychometric analysis**, not products and not agents: `lavaan` is **GPL-2+**
(attach-only), `TAM`'s grant lives in `DESCRIPTION` rather than a licence file
(so GitHub's sidebar shows none), and none of the three is an AI asset. **The
EMEA *agent* layer remains effectively empty** — one 24★ Apache-2.0 university
feedback system and one 13★ evaluator. What EMEA now has is a **measurement**
story, not a tutoring one.

**The compliance calendar has two tracks, and only one of them is live.**
Enforcement of the EU AI Act by the AI Office and national authorities
began **2 August 2026**. But the **Digital Omnibus postponed the high-risk
obligations**: **Annex III** systems — which include education AI that evaluates
learners, assigns them to programmes, or influences access to qualifications —
move from 2 August 2026 to **2 December 2027**, and AI embedded in regulated
products (Annex I) moves to **2 August 2028**. Secondary sources attribute this
to **Regulation (EU) 2026/1744**, in force 27 July 2026, after Council approval
on 29 June 2026. **Article 50 transparency duties were *not* delayed** and have
applied since 2 August 2026.

**A third track was missed by both earlier passes and it is the earliest one of
all: Article 4.** The AI Act's **AI-literacy obligation has applied since
2 February 2025** to every organisation that *uses* an AI system — which is every
school and every university now running AI tools, regardless of whether they
build anything. It is not risk-classified, not deferred, and already overdue
wherever nobody has done it. Of the three EU tracks, this is the only one whose
deadline is in the **past**, and it is the cheapest possible entry point to an
EMEA education engagement: AI-literacy training is a deliverable, not a
conformity programme.

**And the EU is not the only EMEA instrument track.** The **Council of Europe**
roadmap for Responsible AI in Education lists a planned **Legal Instrument on AI
in Education — "establishing rules to protect the integrity of education"** for
**2026**, following its 2025 recommendation on AI in teaching and learning. The
Council of Europe is a 46-state body wider than the EU and its instruments bind
by ratification, so a client in a non-EU European state (UK, Switzerland, Norway,
Türkiye, Western Balkans) has an education-specific track that the AI Act
analysis does not cover. *Single-source this pass; treat as a watch item with a
named instrument rather than an established obligation.*

What that means commercially: the **AI-literacy** work is already late, the
**transparency** work is due now, and the **conformity-assessment** work has
roughly fourteen more months. The Annex III
deferral was already on this KB's record from the previous pass and is
re-verified here from fresh sources; the **Article 50** half and the Regulation
(EU) 2026/1744 citation are new. Note the likely source of the confusion in the
market: enforcement *machinery* did start on 2 August 2026, so "the AI Act
applies from August 2026" is true of the institutions and false of the high-risk
duties.

Teacher adoption is the best-evidenced in the world: the **European Commission
School Education Gateway survey (April 2026, n=1,130)** and the **Sanoma Learning
European Teacher Survey 2026 (20,000+ teachers, 14 countries)** both land on
**63%**. The member-state-level source is the Commission's **Eurydice** report
*Digital education at school in Europe 2026* (2026-09-22). The UK remains
light-touch, principle-based via existing regulators, still in consultation. In
the Gulf, **Dubai's KHDA** has issued explicit responsible-AI guidance to private
schools, and the **UAE** is — with China — one of only two countries running a
**compulsory national AI curriculum**, since the 2025–26 school year.

**Opportunities.**
1. **Article 4 AI-literacy programmes — the obligation that is already
   overdue.** In force since **2 February 2025** for every organisation using AI
   systems, i.e. every school and university with a generative-AI tool in the
   building. No risk classification, no deferral, no conformity assessment:
   training, records and a policy. It is the smallest credible first engagement
   in EMEA and the only one where the client is *behind* rather than preparing.
   `Fazmin/AILearningGuide` (MIT, 242★, 51 labs, offline-first — see
   [`../agents/top.md`](../agents/top.md)) is a shippable starting artefact.
2. **Annex III conformity programmes, sold on the real date.** Learner
   assessment, programme assignment and qualification-access systems are
   high-risk; the deadline is **2 December 2027**. Selling this as a 2026
   emergency is now wrong and a client who checks will notice.
3. **Article 50 transparency, sold as the live one.** In force since 2 August
   2026 and **not** part of the deferral. Smaller, faster, and the honest "start
   here" — and the one place where a client who was told "nothing applies until
   2027" has been misadvised.
4. **The permissive-substrate play is European.** `Selleo/mentingo` (MIT,
   **Poland**) is the only AI-native LMS a client can fork and keep closed, and
   it ships SCORM and xAPI. Pair with `openfun/ralph` (MIT, France) for the LRS
   and `Cvmcosta/ltijs` (Apache-2.0) for LTI. An **entirely permissive, entirely
   EMEA-origin stack** is now assemblable — that was not true a pass ago.
5. **Gulf national-curriculum delivery.** The UAE's compulsory AI curriculum is a
   funded, centrally mandated content-and-training programme.

#### Africa (inside EMEA — not a separate region value)

Policy activity is real and was mistaken for absence in earlier passes: the **AU
Continental AI Strategy**, **Zimbabwe's National AI Strategy 2026–2030**,
**Ghana's 2025–2035 strategy** plus a **July 2026 KG–P6 AI-literacy curriculum
draft**, and strategies in Mauritius, Rwanda and Namibia. Institutional policy is
thin: a **14% / 17% / 69%-don't-know** split on whether institutions have an AI
policy.

**South Africa's status is now resolved, and the reason is a cautionary tale.**
The draft National AI Policy was gazetted on **10 April 2026** (Government Notice
3880), with comment due 10 June. Minister Solly Malatsi **withdrew it** —
announced **26 April 2026**, with the formal withdrawal notice published in a
Government Gazette dated **12 June 2026**, withdrawing Notice 3880 in its
entirety — after at least **10% of the academic references in the document's
reference list were found to be fictitious**. The **August 2024 National AI
Policy Framework** was not withdrawn but carries no statutory force. The
department was still targeting FY2026/27 to finalise a policy; no replacement
draft has been located.

That is a national AI policy withdrawn because its citations were hallucinated.
It is the strongest available argument for the verification discipline this KB
applies to itself, and it is a sellable one: **citation provenance as a
deliverable**.

**Opportunities.**
1. **Ministry curriculum pipelines, on MIT code that already exists.**
   `AI-for-Education/lesson-plan-parse-mbsse` (MIT) already parses **Sierra
   Leone's MBSSE** lesson plans into structured JSON. The same shape applies to
   Ghana's KG–P6 rollout.
2. **Offline-first and voice-first delivery.** `Kolibri` (MIT) for offline, and
   `AI-for-Education/nemoasr-android` for **on-device speech recognition** — the
   only connectivity-independent assets in this KB.
3. **African-language evaluation.** `luganda-linguistic-benchmarks` is the only
   located African-language benchmark. Building these out is the precondition for
   any credible tutor in the region, and nobody has done it.
4. **Citation-provenance tooling for government documents.** Sold directly off
   the South African withdrawal.

**Added on the fifth pass — the nearest EU date in this KB is now one month
away, and it is not the high-risk one.**

- **2 December 2026: Article 50 marking of synthetic content, for systems already
  on the market.** The transparency track — which the Digital Omnibus did *not*
  defer — carries this deadline for machine-readable marking and watermarking of
  AI-generated output on systems already placed on the market. **Every
  content-generating tool in this KB produces exactly the artefacts this covers**
  (generated decks, narrated lessons, interactive texts), so a client already
  running one in the EU has roughly a month, and the remedy is engineering, not
  policy. This is the most actionable EMEA date on this page and it is **13
  months earlier** than the Annex III date most guidance leads with.
- **The deferral itself reproduced independently this pass**: Annex III
  stand-alone high-risk **2026-08-02 → 2027-12-02** (16 months), embedded
  **2027-08-02 → 2028-08-02** (12 months), via **Regulation (EU) 2026/1744**,
  published in the Official Journal **24 July 2026**, in force **27 July 2026**,
  after Council approval **29 June 2026**. Multiple independent legal
  commentaries agree. *One* source in this pass's sweep still headlines that the
  Act "has not been formally delayed" — it is wrong, and it is the error the
  trends page already flags as circulating.
- ⚠️ **One row on the compliance table could not be corroborated this pass.** The
  "AI-generated intimate imagery / abuse material ban, December 2026" entry
  remains **single-source**. The December 2026 EU date this pass *can* corroborate
  from several sources is the Article 50 marking deadline above. These may have
  been conflated somewhere upstream. Keep the Article 50 item and treat the other
  as unverified until a second source appears.
- **OECD *Digital Education Outlook 2026*** recommends institutions move beyond
  general-purpose AI tools toward **purpose-built educational AI** — a
  supranational source for the "purpose-built beats general-purpose" trend that
  had been carried mainly on vendor commentary.
- **Gulf: Dubai's KHDA has issued explicit responsible-AI guidance to private
  schools.** The first named Gulf education regulator instrument in this KB. EMEA
  in this KB has been EU-plus-UK-plus-Africa; the Gulf private-school market is a
  distinct, well-funded buyer and this is the hook into it. Single-source;
  verify before client use.
- **Accessibility is the EMEA-specific technical wedge.**
  `opendataloader-pdf` carries an **`eaa`** topic — the **European Accessibility
  Act** — plus `pdf-ua` and tagged-PDF output. Accessibility conformance is a
  procurement gate across European public education that nothing else in this KB
  answers. See pattern `P-570-B`.


**Sixth pass (`p568`) — the strongest institutionally placed supply in this KB is
European, and it closes the region's provenance request.**

- 🟢 **A complete, all-MIT, nationally published analytics stack.**
  [`openfun/ralph`](https://github.com/openfun/ralph) (LRS) →
  [`openfun/warren`](https://github.com/openfun/warren) (xAPI dashboards) →
  [`openfun/potsie`](https://github.com/openfun/potsie) (Grafana dashboards as
  Jsonnet). All three grants read byte-for-byte this pass; all three name
  *"Copyright (c) 2020-present / 2021-present **France Université Numérique**"*.
  🟢 **The region is read off the grant, not inferred** — which is the instrument
  `567-A` asked for and the cleanest institutional placement on this base.
- 🟢 **The sellable shape: a closed analytics product on top of a copyleft
  platform.** A ministry or university on Moodle (GPL-3.0) or Chamilo (GPL-3.0)
  cannot have a closed derivative of the platform. It *can* have a white-labelled
  analytics layer attached over **xAPI**, with no platform code touched. ⚠️ Price
  it as a deployed service: `potsie`'s dashboards are MIT but **Grafana itself is
  AGPL-3.0**, and `potsie`'s last push is **2024-12-18** — stable, not active.
- 🟢 **Annex III conformity gets its first concrete artefact.** The Digital
  Omnibus moved the Annex III high-risk obligations — including **evaluation of
  learning outcomes** — to **2 December 2027**, while the **transparency duties
  have been live since 2 August 2026**. 🟢 `itembank`'s design — deterministic
  scoring, **no model in the scoring path**, short-answer always routed to a human
  rubric — is evidence that an automated marking pipeline kept a human in the
  loop. That is a conformity *deliverable*, and it is MIT. 🔴 Do not sell the
  Annex III date as 2026; a client told that has been misadvised.
- 🟡 **New in `p570`, and it is a cautionary row rather than an opportunity.**
  [`designfuchen/word-atlas`](https://github.com/designfuchen/word-atlas) —
  **3,500★ in six days**, 14 commits, Chinese/English, IELTS vocabulary with
  UK/US audio — is placed **`APAC†`** on artefact evidence (bilingual
  `CONTENT_LICENSE.md`, Chinese project name 词光, IELTS target). 🔴 **Its MIT
  grant covers the code only**; the 7,298-entry wordlist, the illustrations and
  the recordings are **CC BY 4.0**, *"to the extent of the rights the maintainers
  can grant"*, and were **generated by AI models and never reviewed entry by
  entry**. 🔵 **The APAC-relevant lesson, because exam-prep content is a large
  APAC budget line:** the fastest-growing education content repositories in this
  region are now **machine-generated corpora with hedged title and no editorial
  pass**. A studio that ships one into an IELTS product inherits an unreviewed
  corpus, an attribution obligation, and an unresolved upstream TTS rights
  question. See `570-B`.
- 🔵 **Accessibility stays the EMEA-specific technical wedge** and now has a second
  component: `abogen` (**MIT**) produces synchronised captions, beside
  `opendataloader-pdf`'s `eaa` / `pdf-ua` tagged-PDF output.


#### 🆕 `p569` (`2026-10-31`) — no new placed row, and the regulatory frame got more specific

🔴 **The EMEA leg returned no repository-level asset this pass.** EMEA's placed
institutional rows remain `p568`'s France Université Numérique stack
(`ralph` / `warren` / `potsie`, all MIT) plus
`thm-mni-ii/feedbacksystem` (Apache-2.0, Technische Hochschule Mittelhessen).
🔴 **`567-A`'s ask — place the two EMEA Moodle MCP vendors — is still open and
untouched for a third pass.**

🟢 **What did sharpen is the demand side, and it is specific enough to sell
against.** The **Council of Europe** has found a *lack of rigorous monitoring and
regulation of AI in education* and its expert group is now working on **which
aspects should be regulated** rather than whether. 🟢 **More concrete: the
Council of the EU's agenda carries an AI literacy framework for primary and
secondary education** inside the **2026–2030 European Education Area** work
programme. 🔵 Net public support for stronger AI regulation in Europe moved
**+61% (2025) → +78% (2026)**, so the direction of travel is tighter, not looser.
UK, Germany and France lead adoption on the vendor reports that split by country.

🟢 **Two distinct engagement shapes follow, and they are not the same sale:**

1. **Conformity work** — the EU AI Act's **Annex III** *evaluation of learning
   outcomes* category, obligations landing **2 December 2027**. `P-572-A` is
   built for this: calibration provenance, DIF screening across accountable
   splits, equating argument, full xAPI audit trail. 🔵 **The 2027 date is close
   enough that an awarding body starting in 2027 is already late.**
2. **AI literacy curriculum and assessment** — an EU-level framework for schools
   implies instruments to assess literacy against it. 🟢 That is `itembank` +
   `calibrate` + `ltijs` on a national curriculum, and it is a procurement that
   does not exist yet.

⚠️ **Licence note that bites hardest in EMEA:** the platforms of record here are
copyleft, and `p569` adds a sharper case —
[`swsgbl/ai-learning-os`](https://github.com/swsgbl/ai-learning-os) reads **MIT**
at its licence file while owing **AGPL-3.0 §13** on the one service a hosted
engagement would run. For a public-sector EMEA buyer with a hosting mandate,
read `NOTICE.md` before the licence file (`P-572-B`).

#### 🟢 `p573` (2026-10-31) — the deadline moved, and the gap it opened is the sellable window

🔴 **The high-risk obligations for education AI were deferred.** Sources this pass
attribute the change to **Regulation (EU) 2026/1744**, which moved the AI Act
deadline for **stand-alone high-risk education systems from 2 August 2026 to
2 December 2027**. ⚠️ **Not verified against the Official Journal this pass** —
commentary only (law-firm and vendor blogs), and at least one source still
publishes the old August 2026 date. **Check EUR-Lex before putting the date in a
client deliverable.**

🟢 **What did *not* move, and this is the commercially important half: the
Article 50 transparency obligations still apply from 2 August 2026.** Any
student-facing chatbot must disclose that it is AI. 🔴 **So a European institution
is in scope *today* for disclosure and logging while its conformity deadline sits
13 months out.**

🟢 **That gap is a sequencing offer, not a problem:** ship disclosure, interaction
logging and human-review records now — they are required now and they are the
evidentiary base the Annex III conformity package (`P-568-D`) is assembled from
later. Selling the conformity package first inverts the client's actual calendar.

🔴 **Fixed boundaries worth restating:** **emotion-recognition AI has been banned
in schools since February 2025** — no grace period, no deferral. The **high-risk
categories** are admissions, assessment, exam proctoring and **behaviour
monitoring during tests**. 🔵 The staff **AI-literacy duty was softened** from
*ensuring* a level of AI literacy to *taking measures* toward it — a lower bar,
and one that a documented training programme satisfies.


#### 🆕 `p575` — 2026-10-31 — 🟢 EMEA is the only region with a **named, funded, education-specific AI instrument at supranational level**, and this pass finally has the figures

🟢 **The European Commission updated its ethical guidelines on AI in education (9
June)** and signed **three Advanced Digital Skills grant agreements worth `€25.4 m`.**
🔵 This matters structurally: EMEA is the only region on this page where the
education-specific AI instrument sits **above** the national level, so one compliance
argument travels across every member-state engagement. 🟡 **Graded `C`:** served via a
QS sector newsletter, and **the source does not state the year** for the 9 June date —
read as 2026 in context, not confirmed. Do not put it in a client deliverable without
the primary.

🟢 **United Kingdom: the first-ever AI Adoption Summit (8 June) committed `£200 m`+ to
AI adoption.** 🟡 Same source, same year caveat; the figure is cross-sector, not
education-only.

🟢 **A market-map source new to this base: the [QS Europe EdTech 200](https://newsletters.qs.com/announcing-the-2026-europe-edtech-200-plus-london-edtech-week-ai-skills-and-policy-moves/)
(2026 edition)** — a named, enumerated list of European edtech companies. 🔵 Useful
precisely because this page's EMEA competitive map has been thin: `200` named vendors
is a denominator, and this base has repeatedly found that **a denominator is worth more
than a roundup.**

🟢 **Readiness, with a regional ordering that is unusual:** Workday reports **56 % of
EMEA CEOs** say their organisation is prepared for AI adoption, against **48 % in the
Americas** and **45 % in APJ** — EMEA leads. 🔵 Set against the EU AI Act's weight, that
inverts the usual "regulation suppresses readiness" story and is worth deploying in a
client conversation. 🟡 Cross-sector, CEO self-report; the date is not stated in what
was served, so treat as directional only.

🟢 **~70 % of institutions in Europe and North America have, or are developing, AI
guidance** — the one figure this pass served that spans two regions and lets EMEA be
compared with NA on the same instrument.

🔵 **EMEA remains the best-placed region on these shelves for *grants*, not just
policy:** `EN 301 549` under the Web Accessibility Directive, TAO (`GPL-2.0`, OAT SA,
Luxembourg) as the QTI platform of record, and the R/CRAN psychometric tier whose
institutional copyright lines place it here. 🔴 **Still missing and now twice searched:
ministry-level education AI adoption figures for any single EMEA state.** The
regional leg returns EU-level policy and cross-sector enterprise surveys; it does not
reach a national education ministry.
### APAC

#### 🆕 `p580` — `2026-11-01` — the region holds the field's only frontier-scale education agent, one newly shippable library, and one newly placed offline item bank

🟢 **Re-measured first-hand this pass:** `HKUDS/DeepTutor` (**Apache-2.0**, **`52,849★`**,
`6,469` forks, pushed `2026-10-31`) — 🟢 **still the only education agent anywhere near
frontier scale**; `dream-num/univer` (**Apache-2.0**, `22,837★`) as the courseware document
substrate; `pykt-team/pykt-toolkit` (**MIT**, `456★` — 🔴 *note: this base had been
carrying `1.2k★` from a secondary source*); `satvik314/educhain` (**MIT**, `387★` — 🟢
licence settled this pass, India-origin); `openeducat/openeducat_erp` (**LGPL-3.0**,
`903★`) and `frappe/education` (**GPL-3.0**, `687★`) as the Odoo-pattern LMS+SIS rows.

🟢 **A correction that adds supply:** `bigdata-ustc/EduCDM` (**Apache-2.0**, `203★`) —
cognitive diagnosis models (DINA, IRT, MIRT, NCDM) — **was filed in this base's ledger as
`no-grant`**. It is permissive and shippable. 🔴 Caveat: last pushed `2024-10-25`, so
adopt the algorithms, not the maintenance.

🆕 **Newly placed this pass** (`Gap 579-E`): `BBkjdayup/BCTK` / `TK试题题库`
(**MIT**, `53★`, pushed `2026-09-28`) — a Chinese-language Windows item-bank and
paper-composition desktop app for teachers, **local-offline-first with data on the
machine**, built on Tauri 2 + Vue 3 + Rust + SQLite, with Word import/export that does not
require Microsoft Office. 🔵 It is the clearest instance of the posture `Trend 32`
describes: institution-accountable artefacts ship as offline desktop tools, which is what
a liability-bearing buyer wants and what cannot scale into a popular repository.

🔴 **Declared gap, and a recommendation to retire a leg.** The region-level APAC search
query returned **nothing education-specific for the third consecutive pass** — only
generic "AI sovereignty" commentary and vendor lists built on 2018–19 data. `Trend 23`
explains why: regulation is promulgated **by jurisdiction**, not by region, and `p574`
closed APAC that way (Korea's AI Basic Act naming education a high-impact sector, plus
Singapore, Japan and Australia). 🟢 **The region-level APAC leg should be retired rather
than run a fourth time.**


**p579 — 2026-11-01.**

🟢 **The student-modelling layer is APAC-authored, permissive, and now complete on these
shelves.** `HFUT-LEC/EduStudio` (MIT, `80★`, Hefei University of Technology, *Frontiers of
Computer Science* 2025) unifies cognitive diagnosis and knowledge tracing;
`ZhijieXiong/pyedmine` (MIT, `82★`) adds exercise recommendation and
**learning-path recommendation** — `28` KT models, `7` CD, `3` ER, `4` LPR over `14`
datasets. With `pykt-toolkit` (MIT, `456★`) that is three MIT libraries covering the whole
modelling stack, all APAC in origin. **Opportunity:** adaptive-learning engagements in
Korea, Japan, Singapore and China can be built on MIT research code rather than licensed
adaptive engines — and Korea's **AI Basic Act** names education a high-impact sector, so
the model-governance documentation is itself billable. 🔴 **But the curriculum-structure
artefact that would anchor such a build — `cn-math-taxonomy` — is NonCommercial.** A
commercial APAC engagement needs its own knowledge graph; budget for building one.

**p578 — 2026-10-31.** 🟢 **The region keeps supplying the permissive tier, and this pass it
supplied the *operations* layer rather than the model layer.**
[`t1112000/tutor-management-app`](https://github.com/t1112000/tutor-management-app) (MyClass,
**MIT**, 110★, Vietnamese localisation) is the **first permissive system of record** on
[`../verticals/solutions.md`](../verticals/solutions.md) — every other one is copyleft.
**Opportunity:** the private supplementary-tutoring tier across Vietnam, Indonesia and the
Philippines runs on spreadsheets and has no LMS; MyClass is forkable, so the offer is a
**white-label tutoring-centre platform** with demand forecasting, attendance risk and
parent-facing progress summaries on top — a build, not an integration. 🔴 **Scope the
regulatory exposure explicitly:** supplementary tutoring is the sub-sector most exposed to
policy swings in this region, so the proposal needs a migration story for the data if the
business model is restricted.

**p577 — 2026-10-31.** **This is where the open-source supply lives.**
`HKUDS/DeepTutor` (Apache-2.0, 52.7k★) and `THU-MAIC/OpenMAIC` (MIT, 42.2k★) are
the two largest education agents in open source and both are permissively
licensed — an APAC-sourced engagement can be built almost entirely on them.
Regulation is **fragmented by jurisdiction**; Forrester expects no APAC-wide
framework, with 2026 regional priorities being sovereignty, security and
responsible deployment. Named demand drivers: India's Digital Education Mission
and China's AI-in-education policies. Market reported as the fastest-growing
region (one firm: $591.6M in 2024 → $1,848.1M by 2029, 20.9% CAGR — vendor figure).
**Watch item:** unlicensed academic releases are a recurring APAC pattern
(`Agent4Edu`, AAAI 2025 oral, 100★, no licence file). Check the grant before
scoping anything on a paper repo.
**Opportunity:** teacher-training simulation on OpenMAIC + `smolagents`
(`compose/patterns.md` P4), and sovereign self-hosted tutoring per jurisdiction.


#### 🟡 `p574` — India is the only APAC jurisdiction with an **education-specific** accessibility instrument, and the region contributes `0` established rows to the layer

🟢 **India, and it is education-specific rather than general disability law —
which makes it the most actionable accessibility position in the region:**

- 🟢 An **Accessibility Code for Educational Institutions** was notified on
  **`10 January 2024`**.
- 🟢 An **AICTE** circular (February 2024) carries accessibility guidelines for
  higher education, citing **RPwD Act ss. 16–18** (Chapter III, Education) as the
  duty of educational institutions.
- 🔵 The web standard is **GIGW 3.0**, reported as aligned to **WCAG 2.1 AA**, with
  the **RPwD Act 2016** and Rules 2017 as the statutory base.
- 🔵 The Supreme Court (**Rajive Raturi**) is reported to have directed the Union
  Government to define mandatory, enforceable standards with timelines — so the
  direction of travel is from guidance to obligation.

🟢 **Australia:** the base instrument is the **Disability Discrimination Act
1992**, and the 2026 development is a move to **WCAG 2.2 AA**, explicitly
dovetailing with `EN 301 549` v4.1.1. 🟢 **This is the one cross-regional
convergence on this page: EMEA and APAC land on the same technical target in the
same year**, which makes a single remediation standard sellable across both.

🔴 **Japan: recorded as UNRESOLVED, deliberately.** The standard is **JIS X
8341-3**; two sources of comparable quality disagree on whether it aligns to
**WCAG 2.0 AA** or **WCAG 2.1**, and the legal base cited is the 2014
disability-discrimination act applying to public institutions. 🔵 This page
publishes **no** version for Japan rather than picking one — the difference
decides whether a remediation scope is one pass or two.

🔴 **Declared gap, stated as a gap because silence reads as coverage:** this pass
searched for an education-specific digital accessibility mandate in **Japan,
Korea, Singapore and China** and **found none**. That is an informed absence from
two targeted queries, not an exhaustive finding. Registered `574-D`.

🔴 **Supply: `0` of the layer's `29` addresses could be placed in APAC**, and the
mechanism is instructive — this base places origin from the **copyright line of
the licence file**, and the two plausibly APAC-origin candidates
([`dxli94/WLASL`](https://github.com/dxli94/WLASL), `1.3k★`, and
[`ZechengLi19/Awesome-Sign-Language`](https://github.com/ZechengLi19/Awesome-Sign-Language),
`231★`) **carry no licence file at all**. 🔵 **So an ungranted repository is also a
region-unplaceable one: the licence is doing double duty as the provenance
record.** That is a new observation about this base's own instrument and is
registered with `574-B`.

🟡 **One adoption signal, single secondary, grade `C`:** the Philippines'
Department of Education and Google certifying **more than one million teachers**
in Gemini through *Project S.I.N.A.G.* Recorded; not quotable without a second
source.


**The region's evidence base improved sharply this pass and is no longer
vendor-only** — which retires the standing gap that called APAC the
weakest-evidenced region.

**Australia** has no AI-specific statute (a 2024 proposal for mandatory
"guardrails" in high-risk settings included education), but its higher-education
regulator **TEQSA** issued a June 2024 request requiring *every* provider to
submit a credible institutional action plan addressing gen-AI risk to award
integrity, and received a **100% response rate** — a regulator-collected census,
not a survey. TEQSA's toolkit *Gen AI strategies for Australian higher education:
Emerging practice* and its follow-up *Enacting assessment reform in a time of
artificial intelligence* (≈Sept 2025) came out of that analysis, the latter naming
three emergent approaches: programme-wide assessment reform, assuring learning in
every unit, or a combination. Independently, a peer-reviewed **AJET** national
document analysis of **39** universities offering ICT programmes found
operational governance structures documented in only **7 of 39** — policy
articulation is widespread, operational governance is not.

**South Korea's AI Basic Act entered into force 22 January 2026**, with
enforcement decrees still being finalised and a 2026 grace period focused on
guidance rather than penalties. Its education-specific experiment is the
cautionary one: **AI textbook adoption sat below 30% by March**, and lawmakers
subsequently **removed their official status**.

**Vietnam** adopted a comprehensive risk-based AI law — passed by the National
Assembly on **10 December 2025**, effective **1 March 2026** — and a follow-on
decision designates high-risk systems in six sectors **including education**,
reported to cover **automated assessment**: the most directly education-binding
instrument in the region. **New this pass, and it changes the sales calendar:
systems already in operation get a transition period of 12 months, extended to
18 months in health care, education and finance.** So an education system already
running when the law took effect has until approximately **1 September 2027** —
which is within three months of the EU's Annex III date of 2 December 2027. The
two most binding education-AI deadlines in the world now land in the same
quarter, and a multinational client needs one programme, not two. **China** maintains the most
comprehensive framework (mandatory registration, strict content labelling) and
runs, with the UAE, one of the world's only two compulsory national AI curricula.
**Japan** is light-touch and voluntary; **MEXT's** generative-AI pilot reached
**149 municipalities and 478 schools in FY2026** — under 2% of roughly 33,000
schools — and a MEXT-attributed figure puts elementary/junior-high use of
generative AI **for school administration at 17.2%, up 14.5 points year on year**.
**Singapore** relies on **AI Verify**, has a **Teacher AI Literacy Professional
Learning Roadmap** (one compulsory module by end 2026, two more recommended by
end 2027), and has commissioned a **survey of teacher and student AI use** under
its MOE Rapid Research Fund for H1 2026 — **results not yet published**.

**Public opinion is moving against classroom AI in three APAC markets, and it is
now measured over time.** The **Ipsos Education Monitor 2026** records rising
support for *banning* AI in schools: **Japan 21% → 29%**, **Australia 47% →
52%**, **South Korea 27% → 31%**. Australia and New Zealand sit highest in the
region; the Asian markets surveyed are lower but trending the same way. This is
the first attitudinal **time series** in this KB, and it is a risk datum rather
than an opportunity: in Australia, **a majority now supports a ban** in the same
market where TEQSA has made institutional AI plans mandatory. A proposal that
assumes public permission is reading the regulator and ignoring the electorate.
Pair it with the Korean textbook reversal below — the two together are the
strongest available argument for staging a rollout behind teacher capability.

**Opportunities.**
1. **Award-integrity and assessment-reform programmes, Australia first.** TEQSA
   has already made the plan mandatory and the gap measurable: 7 of 39 have
   operational governance. The remaining 32 are a named pipeline.
2. **Vietnam high-risk assessment compliance.** Automated assessment in a
   designated high-risk sector, under a law already in force. First-mover window.
3. **Teacher AI-literacy delivery at national scale.** Singapore has mandated it
   with dates; Korea's grace period runs through 2026. This is training
   delivery, not software, and it is funded.
4. **Agent sourcing.** The two largest education agents in the world are APAC and
   permissive — **DeepTutor** (Apache-2.0, 52,167★, HKU) and **OpenMAIC** (MIT,
   41,981★, Tsinghua). An engagement anywhere that sources from the shelf is
   sourcing from APAC.
5. **Avoid the Korean textbook failure mode.** Mandated content without teacher
   buy-in reached <30% adoption and then lost its official status. Any
   national-rollout proposal should carry an adoption-risk section citing it.

**Added on the fifth pass — the two Asian regimes are now specific enough to
scope work against, and one of them regulates RAG directly.**

- **Vietnam: Decision 33 is the instrument, and it names three education
  systems.** The AI Law (No. 134/2025/QH15) took effect **1 March 2026**;
  **Decision 33**, effective **15 August 2026**, publishes the high-risk list —
  **46 systems across six sectors**, education among them with **three** entries:
  (1) AI that **automatically tests, evaluates or ranks learners**; (2) AI that
  **monitors and analyses learner behaviour**; (3) **self-study tools that
  generate educational content from uncontrolled data sources.** **The third is
  the one to read twice.** Every retrieval-augmented tutor in this KB generates
  content from sources it does not curate — that is the architecture, not a
  misuse of it. A RAG tutor sold into Vietnam is **presumptively high-risk**
  unless the corpus is controlled, which turns "curated corpus" from a quality
  choice into a regulatory one. Several sources note that listed systems are
  high-risk chiefly where AI output is the **sole** basis for a decision with no
  human in the loop, so human-in-the-loop review is also a classification lever.
  Existing education systems have a transition to about **1 September 2027**.
- **Korea: correction to this KB's record — the Enforcement Decree is not
  pending.** The compliance table has carried "enforcement decrees pending". The
  **Decree entered into force on 22 January 2026 alongside the Act**. What is
  deferred is *enforcement*, specifically: **fact-finding and fines are suspended
  for at least one year from 22 January 2026**, except in extreme cases such as
  loss of life. Education appears as a named **high-impact** area — the sources
  specify **evaluation of students in early childhood, elementary and secondary
  education** — carrying risk-management, user-protection and human-oversight
  duties. **So the obligations are live now and the penalties are not**, which is
  a far better sales position than "pending decrees": a client can be brought into
  conformance inside the grace window.
- **Singapore procures frameworks, not rules.** **AI Verify** is a testing and
  accountability toolkit rather than a statute — meaning the deliverable in
  Singapore is **evidence of testing**, which pairs directly with the evaluation
  layer in [`../repos/foundations.md`](../repos/foundations.md) and with
  `terracotta`'s randomised-trial apparatus.
- **China's regime is the most assertive** — mandatory registration, content
  labelling obligations, penalties. Note the awkward commercial fact this creates:
  the two largest permissive education agents in this KB (**DeepTutor**, HKU;
  **OpenMAIC**, Tsinghua) and the strongest OCR (**PaddleOCR**, Baidu) are all
  Chinese-origin, so **an engagement anywhere is likely sourcing from the most
  heavily regulated AI jurisdiction in the region.** The licences are clean; the
  supply-chain question a client's risk committee will ask is not about licences.
- **Japan remains light-touch and voluntary** — no education-specific instrument
  located.
- **Market shape:** market-research sources put **APAC as the fastest-growing
  region** for AI in education, with China, India and Japan dominating spend, and
  name Google, Microsoft, IBM, Pearson and Byju's as key players. Vendor-sourced;
  quote as direction, not as figures.


**Sixth pass (`p568`) — the live permissive psychometrics layer is Chinese and
institutional; no new placed address, and the reason is stated.**

- 🟢 **[`bigdata-ustc/EduCDM`](https://github.com/bigdata-ustc/EduCDM)
  (**Apache-2.0**, 203★) is the one live, permissive, education-specific
  psychometrics asset on this base**, from a named university group (USTC). ⚠️ Be
  precise in a pitch: it does **cognitive diagnosis** — inferring what a learner
  knows from their responses — and **not item calibration**. The calibration half
  of that layer is genuinely missing everywhere (see
  [`../repos/foundations.md`](../repos/foundations.md)).
- 🔴 **No new APAC-placed address this pass.**
  [`sudazc/open-question-bank`](https://github.com/sudazc/open-question-bank) is
  Chinese-language (`组卷`, test assembly) but its grant names an individual
  handle, and **this base does not place an artefact on its language.** Recorded
  `Global`. The gap is a provenance gap, not a supply gap.
- 🟢 **Data residency is the regional wedge, and the sixth pass added the
  component for it.** Vietnam's AI law obliges identified systems by
  **1 March 2027**, with a grace position for education systems already operating
  before **15 August 2026**; Taiwan's **AI Basic Act** took effect
  **14 January 2026**; Indonesia's **GR 71** imposes data-residency duties that
  bear directly on EdTech hosting. 🟢
  [`Changochen/slide-to-video`](https://github.com/Changochen/slide-to-video)
  (**MIT**) is the only lecture-video row that runs with **no cloud TTS at all** —
  optional local voice cloning — which pairs with `PAIDEIA` and `WordLink`'s
  in-browser model as a fully local courseware-and-tutor story.
- 🔵 **A cautionary adoption datum worth carrying:** South Korea's AI digital
  textbook initiative was **walked back to optional "educational material" status
  within about a year** after objections over screen time and data. 🔴 It is the
  clearest available evidence that a national mandate is not a durable demand
  signal, and it should temper any roadmap built on one. Reported via secondary
  sources; treat as direction. Regional CAGR figures from market-research vendors
  remain unverified and should be quoted as direction only.


#### 🆕 `p570` (`2026-10-31`) — a Brazilian national-statistics asset, and the honest grade of the evidence

🟡 **[`SidneyBissoli/educabR`](https://github.com/SidneyBissoli/educabR) is
placed `LATAM†` — artefact grade, not grant grade.** An R package to *"Download
and Process Brazilian Education Data from **INEP**"* — INEP being Brazil's
national institute for education statistics and assessment, the body that runs
**ENEM** and the **Censo Escolar**. README is Portuguese; `R-CMD-check` and
coverage badges are live. 🔴 **The grant places nothing** — `DESCRIPTION`'s
`Authors@R` carries a gmail address, so unlike this pass's three EMEA rows the
placement rests on what the package *does*, not on who signed it. Marked `†` per
`569-E`.

🔵 **Why it is worth a row anyway.** Every LATAM engagement in this base's
history has needed a *data* starting point for a ministry or *secretaría*
conversation, and this is a maintained, permissively granted client for the
national education dataset of the region's largest education system. 🔵 It pairs
with `JaxisReal/Moodle-Reporte-Limpio` below (Moodle completion reports,
Spanish): **one row reads the national dataset, the other reads the institution's
own LMS.**

🔴 **And the regional ledger stays honest: LATAM gained no new *agent* and no new
*grant-placed* row this pass.** The region's only placed education agent,
`dio-agent` (2,750★), is still **unlicensed**, so the local-supply story and the
build-on-it story remain about different repositories — the structural finding
this base has now carried for four passes.

#### 🆕 `p569` (`2026-10-31`) — a corporate holder named in a grant, and a second row placed on artefact evidence only

🟢 **[`verycoolapps/Statistica`](https://github.com/verycoolapps/Statistica) is
the cleanest APAC placement this base has recorded on the grant itself.** Its
`main/LICENSE` (MIT, 1,096 B) reads *"Copyright (c) 2026 VeryCoolApps — **PT.
Agra Karya Digital**"* — `PT.` is the Indonesian form for a limited company, so
the holder is a **named corporate entity in a named jurisdiction**. 🔵 This base's
placement instrument was built for *institutional* holders (universities,
national operators); **a company name works identically and the rule should say
so.** ⚠️ Substance is thin — an offline statistics desktop app, bilingual ID/EN,
**2 commits** — so it is placed, not shelved as a foundation.

🟡 **[`swsgbl/ai-learning-os`](https://github.com/swsgbl/ai-learning-os) is
recorded APAC on a weaker footing, deliberately marked as such.** Its grant names
*"AI Learning OS contributors"* — not an institution — so it is placed on
**artefact evidence**: a **HarmonyOS / 深开鸿** client target, a `ChinaTextbook`
catalogue integration, Chinese-language documentation throughout. 🔴 **Two grades
of evidence, labelled, not blended** — `569-E`.

🔵 **Regional read (secondary, directional).** APAC is the **fastest-growing**
region for AI in education on the vendor reports that split it out
(**USD 591.6 m in 2024 → ~USD 1.85 bn by 2029**, ~20.9% CAGR). 🔴 **The
governance picture is the opposite of EMEA's: Forrester expects no APAC-wide
legislative framework** — rules stay fragmented per country — and the 2026 theme
across regional analysts is **sovereignty and "sovereign-by-design" execution**.
IDC places most APAC countries at mid-stage AI maturity with Singapore leading.

🟢 **That makes the self-hosted, measurement-first shape unusually well matched
to this region.** `ai-learning-os` is server-authoritative, keeps **ASR/TTS
local so voice audio never leaves the machine**, and holds LLM keys in a
server-side encrypted BYOK store. 🔵 **Sovereignty is the APAC buying criterion
and local inference is the answer to it** — which is also why the sub-floor tier
matters here specifically: `4 of 5` of this pass's new tutor-class agents run
locally or BYOK.

🔴 **Declared gap: the APAC leg returned no education-specific regulation for
China, Korea or Australia and no 2026 school adoption rate.** The only
education-specific player list any source offered was dated 2019. The APAC
governance rows on this page remain the Australian regulator exercise
(**7 of 39** universities with documented operational governance).

#### 🆕 `p571` (`2026-10-31`) — two instruments this page did not carry: India's labelling duty and Vietnam's education-specific transition

🔴 **Most of what the APAC leg returned, this page already held** — Korea's **AI
Basic Act**, the **Ipsos Education Monitor 2026** ban-support figures, China's
targeted measures and Singapore's **AI Verify** are all already recorded above.
🟢 **Two instruments were genuinely absent, and both are dated and operational.**

| 🆕 instrument | what it requires | date | why it matters here |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 🟢 **India — amended IT Rules** | AI-generated content must carry **prominent labels** *and* **provenance markers** | **from 20 Feb 2026** | 🔵 **Absent from every page of this base before `p571`.** It applies directly to AI-authored courseware and assessment items — so **provenance emission is a build requirement, not a nicety**, and it is a *content* duty rather than a risk-classification duty, which makes it the cheapest AI-education obligation in the region to comply with and the easiest to fail silently. |
| 🟢 **Vietnam — Law on AI** | systems **already in operation** get a transition: **12 months** generally, **18 months** for **education**, health and finance | **in force 1 Mar 2026** | 🟢 **Education is named explicitly, alongside health and finance** — the regulator's own risk ranking. The 18-month clock implies a remediation deadline around **Sep 2027** for institutions that have *already* deployed. This page previously carried Vietnam only in `intel/open-gaps.md`. |

🟢 **Opportunities, placed.**

1. 🟢 **Vietnam is the most precisely dated remediation engagement in any region on
   this page.** Not a greenfield build: an **inventory → gap assessment →
   conformity** sequence for systems already running, against a statutory clock
   with education singled out. Sell it before Q3 2027, not after.
2. 🟢 **India's duty is an engineering task with a fixed deliverable.** Labels plus
   **provenance markers** on generated content is exactly what the newly shelved
   item-generation tier must emit at generation time — it cannot be reconstructed
   afterwards. Wired as a required step in `P-574-A`
   ([`../compose/patterns.md`](../compose/patterns.md)), not an option.
3. 🔵 **Both instruments reinforce the region's existing counter-signal, not against
   it.** The Ipsos figures already on this page (Australia **52%** support for
   banning AI in education; Japan 21→29%; Korea 27→31%) mean the defensible APAC
   framing stays **integrity, oversight and provenance** — and these two
   instruments are *precisely* provenance and transition obligations. The
   regulatory and the public mood point the same way.

#### 🟢 `p573` (2026-10-31) — fastest-growing region, most fragmented rulebook, and a counter-trend in public opinion

🟢 **APAC is the fastest-growing region at 35.3 % CAGR** (against North America's
~36 % *share*). 🔴 **And there is no single regulatory model to design against.**
**South Korea, China and Vietnam have binding AI rules**; **Japan, Singapore,
Hong Kong, Australia and India** rely mainly on existing law, guidance and
targeted instruments.

🟢 **Curriculum mandates are the region's distinctive demand driver, and they are
national in scale:**
- **India** has announced **AI and computational thinking as compulsory from
  Class 3 beginning in the 2026–27 academic year.**
- **China** has made **AI compulsory for school students from age six**, and
  governs public-facing generative AI through a **filing gate run by the
  Cyberspace Administration (CAC)**.
- **UNESCO** records China, Japan, the Republic of Korea and Singapore as already
  integrating AI-focused school curricula.

🔴 **Dated compliance edges to design around:** **India's amended IT Rules on
labelling synthetic content took effect 20 February 2026.** **Vietnam's high-risk
AI decision explicitly covers education, including automated assessment, with the
grace period for education systems ending 1 September 2027.**

🔴 **The counter-trend, and it is the most useful thing on this block: public
support for *banning* AI in schools is rising in two of the region's largest
markets.** Ipsos Education Monitor 2026 records support for a ban up from **21 %
to 29 % in Japan** and **27 % to 31 % in South Korea**. 🔵 Everywhere else this KB
measures, the story is adoption outrunning governance; here a mandate-led rollout
is moving **ahead of parental consent**. 🟢 **Commercial consequence: in Japan and
Korea, the deliverable that sells is the one with a parent- and teacher-facing
transparency story attached, not the one with the best model.**

🟢 **Deployment signal:** **all 20 Malaysian public universities have enabled
Gemini for Education** — the region's clearest example of system-level, rather
than institution-level, adoption.


#### 🆕 `p575` — 2026-10-31 — 🟢 **`574-D` substantially CLOSED: Korea, Singapore, Japan and Australia all have education-specific AI instruments, and Korea's is BINDING with education named as a high-impact sector**

🟢 **This is the pass where APAC stopped being this base's blind region.** `574-D`
recorded "searched and NOT found: an education-specific instrument for Japan, Korea,
Singapore or China" after twelve consecutive failures of the regional leg. 🟢 **Four
targeted, per-jurisdiction queries found instruments for three of those four, plus
Australia.** 🔵 The lesson is `574-D`'s own: **the region-level leg cannot reach
regulation; the jurisdiction-level leg can.**

🟢 **KOREA — the most commercially consequential finding of this pass.** The **AI Basic
Act** (Framework Act on the Development of Artificial Intelligence) and its
**Enforcement Decree took effect `22 January 2026`.** 🔴 **Education is a NAMED
high-impact sector**, alongside healthcare, employment, credit and public
administration. 🔴 **So an education AI system deployed in Korea carries statutory
duties: risk management, an explanation obligation, user protection, human oversight,
and labelling of generative AI output.** 🟢 **This is the first *binding*
education-specific AI obligation on these shelves outside the EU AI Act and the US
Title II rule** — every other APAC instrument this base holds is guidance. 🟡 **With a
grace period:** MSIT is operating **at least one year** of forbearance through 2026,
deferring fact-finding investigations and administrative fines except in exceptional
cases of serious social harm. 🔴 **And the timeline is contested** — at least one
lawmaker has proposed delaying implementation to **January 2029**, so the compliance
date is a moving target even though the obligation is in force. 🔵 **Engagement
implication: a Korean education deployment needs the explainability and
human-oversight artefacts designed in from the start, not retrofitted** — and the
grace period is the window to do it in.

🟢 **SINGAPORE — the densest, best-dated education AI programme in the region.**
- **MOE AI in Education (AIEd) Framework**, built on four principles: **Agency,
  Inclusivity, Fairness, Safety**; sits inside the **EdTech Masterplan 2030**.
- **"Four Learns"** — learning *about* AI, *to use* AI, *with* AI and **beyond** AI —
  set out at the **2026 Committee of Supply** debate and restated in a parliamentary
  reply of **`6 May 2026`**.
- **Teacher AI Literacy Professional Learning Roadmap, launched August 2026:** **three
  compulsory AI modules** for all primary, secondary and junior-college teachers —
  **one due by end-2026, the other two by 2027.** 🟢 A dated, mandatory,
  nationwide teacher-upskilling obligation is a **services opportunity with a
  deadline attached.**
- **Teacher tooling shipped January 2026:** an **Authoring Copilot** for lesson and
  assessment materials, and a **Learning Assistant** platform.
- **A hard access floor:** students from **Primary 4** may use education-designed AI
  tools under teacher supervision inside the **Singapore Student Learning Space**;
  **Primary 1–3 are kept away from AI tools** in favour of physical hands-on learning.
  🔵 **An age floor is a product requirement**, and this base has not previously held
  one from any jurisdiction.
- **Data boundary stated:** MOE says data from its AI tools is anonymised and **not
  used to train the external models that power them.**

🟡 **All Singapore items graded `C`** — they come from local news and sector blogs, not
MOE's own publications or parliamentary records, and some sources conflict on detail.
🔵 **Ask: read the MOE site and Hansard directly** before any of this reaches a client
deliverable.

🟢 **JAPAN — found, and more specific than `574-D` expected.** MEXT published
**"Guidelines on the Use of Generative AI in Primary and Secondary Education (ver.
2.0)" in December 2024**, superseding provisional guidance of **July 2023**; separate
guidance for universities and technical colleges also dates from **July 2023.** Four
stated pillars: appropriate use with safety in mind, information security, protection
of personal information / privacy / copyright, and fairness; students are instructed
to treat AI output **as reference only** and to make their own final judgements.

🔴 **The Japan accessibility question `574-D` raised is now SEARCHED AND NOT FOUND, which
is a different status from unsearched.** No source links MEXT's AI guidance to **`JIS X
8341-3`**, Japan's ICT accessibility standard. 🔵 **So this base still publishes NO
version for `JIS X 8341-3`** — one source calls it WCAG 2.0 AA, another WCAG 2.1, and
the difference decides whether a remediation scope is one pass or two. 🔵 **Ask: fetch
the MEXT guideline PDF from `mext.go.jp` and grep its text for `JIS` / `8341`** — the
one channel not yet tried.

🟢 **AUSTRALIA — an education-specific framework, but the binding duty sits elsewhere,
and that distinction is the finding.**
- The **Australian Framework for Generative AI in Schools** carries **6 principles and
  25 guiding statements**; its **Fairness** principle holds the accessibility
  statement that genAI tools should be inclusive, accessible and equitable for people
  with disability. **Reviewed at least every 12 months.**
- 🔴 **It is voluntary.** The enforceable obligation comes from the **Disability
  Standards for Education 2005 (Cth)** under the **Disability Discrimination Act 1992
  (Cth)** — whose **2025 Review** is in progress, with one resource due in 2026.
- 🟢 **Victoria makes the link operational:** schools must ensure a genAI tool **can be
  implemented in an accessible and inclusive way *before* implementation**, tied
  explicitly to the Disability Standards and reasonable-adjustment law. 🔵 **A
  pre-deployment accessibility gate is a procurement checkpoint an engagement can be
  sold against** — and it is the clearest such instrument this base holds in any
  jurisdiction. 🟡 The Victorian page's "Updated 28 September 2026" conflicts with its
  own age metadata; date graded `C`.
- Australia's approach is **standards-based and voluntary**, not binding AI
  legislation; the **National AI Plan** names people with disability among groups
  requiring attention.

🔴 **CHINA — searched twice this pass, NOT FOUND.** Neither an education-specific AI
instrument nor a **`GB`/`GB/T`** accessibility standard applicable to education was
reached. 🔵 **This is an informed gap, not coverage**: both queries were
English-language. **Ask: query in Chinese (`人工智能 教育 标准`, `无障碍 国家标准`) and
read `SAC/TC28`** — registered as `575-D`.

🟢 **APAC supply also moved this pass, which is new** — the region usually scores `0` on
origin here: [`littlebordercollie/mcd-together-order`](https://github.com/littlebordercollie/mcd-together-order)
(**MIT**, `101★`, the first MCP-native AAC agent in this base, 培智学校 framing),
[`tejasbhor/shravyamudra`](https://github.com/tejasbhor/shravyamudra) (**MIT**,
Indian Sign Language) and [`mzdk100/rigela`](https://github.com/mzdk100/rigela)
(**Apache-2.0**, `150★`, pure-Rust screen reader). 🔵 **India remains the only APAC
jurisdiction with an education-*specific* accessibility code** (AICTE / RPwD ss. 16–18,
GIGW 3.0 at WCAG 2.1 AA), and `shravyamudra` is the first shelved component that lands
against it.
### LATAM

#### 🆕 `p580` — `2026-11-01` — adoption is the highest measured anywhere and the open supply is still one AGPL row

🟢 **Re-measured first-hand this pass:** `portabilis/i-diario` (**AGPL-3.0** by byte-read,
`117★`, pushed `2026-10-14`, Brazil) and `portabilis/pre-matricula-digital`
(**AGPL-3.0**, `21★`) remain the region's only platform-tier rows on this base's shelf.
🔴 **Both are network copyleft, and neither has a permissive alternative of LATAM origin.**

🔵 **Demand evidence, re-confirmed and now with its denominator:** the Digital Education
Council survey run with **Tecnológico de Monterrey's Institute for the Future of
Education** reports **`92 %` of students and `79 %` of faculty** engaging with AI, with
`67 %` of students using it at least weekly — across **`29` universities**, presented at
**IFE Conference 2026**. (The `29`-university denominator and the venue are the only two
tokens any of this pass's eight mandated search legs returned that this base did not
already hold.) UNESCO IESALC's survey of **`200` institutions across `19` countries**
records the region's adoption-versus-governance gap as unusually wide; ILIA, with the IDB
and ECLAC, puts Chile, Brazil and Mexico ahead on readiness.

🟢 **The instrument position is settled from earlier passes and stands:** six LATAM
jurisdictions have dated education-AI instruments and **Colombia's `Ley 2626 de 2026` is a
statute**, not guidance.

🔴 **The opportunity, stated as the arbitrage it is.** The region has the **highest
measured adoption** in this base and the **thinnest open supply** — one AGPL platform
family, no permissive LATAM-origin education component found in `580` passes, and an
explicit absence of institutional AI policy at the university level. 🟢 **Governance and
policy tooling is therefore the entry point, not platform build**: an institutional AI-use
policy engine with an audit trail, built on permissive non-LATAM components
(`Numbas` Apache-2.0, `nbgrader` BSD-3-Clause, `scorm-again` MIT) and localised
pt-BR/es-419. 🔴 **Note for assessment work: `asahi417/lm-question-generation` covers nine
languages and has no Portuguese** — and it is abandoned anyway (trend 35).


**p579 — 2026-11-01.**

🟢 **The offline-first and operations tiers are where permissive code exists, and both fit
the region's constraints.** `learningequality/kolibri` (MIT, `1,163★`) is the offline-first
platform of record; `yungifez/skuul` (MIT, `468★`) models organisations and campuses as
first-class, which is the shape of a secretaría or a school network, and
`academico-sis/academico` (MIT, `410★`) covers the small-to-medium institution.
`chamilo/chamilo-lms` (GPL-3.0, `1,019★`) holds the regional install base but is copyleft —
integrate, do not fork. **Opportunity:** a multi-campus operations layer with attendance and
fee-risk scoring and parent-facing summaries, built MIT-throughout and therefore resellable
across ministries. 🔵 The demand signal is already on this page and unchanged: `87 %` of
LAC higher-education institutions use AI, **`26 %`** have a strategy and **`9 %`** evaluate
it — the gap is governance and evaluation capacity, not enthusiasm, and
**`Ley 2626 de 2026`** in Colombia makes curricular delivery a statutory obligation rather
than an aspiration.

**p578 — 2026-10-31.** 🔴 **No movement, and the nil is specific: the five GitHub axes swept
this pass — `topic:tutoring` (466), `topic:learning-path` (466), `topic:stem-education`
(235), `topic:math-education` (152), `topic:literacy` (115) — returned `0` LATAM-placeable
repositories between them.** `1,434` repositories, no row the region could claim. 🔵 That is
consistent with what `p576` established the hard way: **this region is reachable on
language axes (`topic:educacao`, `topic:educacion`) and on jurisdiction queries, and not on
English-language capability axes.** Recorded so the next pass does not spend its budget
here. **Opportunity unchanged** and still the strongest on this page: the `87 %`-use /
`26 %`-strategy spread (UNESCO IESALC) and Colombia's `Ley 2626 de 2026` are demand that
exists whether or not GitHub supplies a LATAM-origin agent — the components get imported,
and the **curriculum alignment and the Spanish/Portuguese content pipeline** are the
billable part.

**p577 — 2026-10-31.** **ECLAC (Oct 2025)** ranks **Chile, Brazil and Mexico** as
regional leaders across 100+ indicators of capability, research, adoption and
governance, and finds persistent gaps in data, training and regulation. The **IDB
fAIr Tech Radar (Nov 2025)** describes adoption advancing against structural gaps
and developing regulatory frameworks. **UNESCO IESALC with UNU-IAS** maps
university AI use across teaching, research, community engagement, administration
and governance.
**Platform reality:** the region runs Moodle and Chamilo (both GPL-3.0), not
Canvas — so the LTI-attachment pattern applies with Spanish- and
Portuguese-language content generation on top.
**Verified local artifacts are tiny:** `pedagogy-benchmark` (MIT, 13★, built from
Chile's Agencia de la Calidad de la Educación and CPEIP exams) and
`libro-3d-interactivo` (MIT, 14★, Univ. de Los Lagos, Chilean curriculum OA 13).
Treat them as patterns to reimplement, not dependencies. The reusable assets are
the **curriculum-alignment method** and the **Spanish-language evaluation set**.
**Gap, stated explicitly:** LATAM market size is **not credibly published**. One
firm's ≈US$18.2M (2024) figure is three orders of magnitude off regional peers and
should not be used. Do not size a LATAM deal from this page.


#### 🆕 `p576` — the region now has instruments, and its own-language open-source supply is a hobby tier

🟢 **See this pass's section at the top of this page for the six jurisdictions and
`Ley 2626 de 2026`.** What belongs here is the consequence: **the mandates are arriving in
jurisdictions whose local-language open-source supply cannot meet them.**

🔴 **Measured first-hand this pass, with denominators read off GitHub's own result counts:**

| axis | repositories | top of the star ladder |
| --- | --: | --- |
| `topic:educacion` (Spanish) | **410** | 🔴 **`71★`** — a JavaScript course, not a product |
| `topic:educacao` (Portuguese) | **378** | 🟢 **`722★`** — `portabilis/i-educar`, a real platform of record |

🔵 **The two languages are not in the same condition, and that is the finding.**
🔴 **Spanish-language supply has no platform tier at all**: below the `71★` course
repository sit teaching materials, a Moodle attendance plugin for **Chile's SENCE** platform
(`29★`), and a Spanish vocational-training grading app (`9★`). 🟢 **Portuguese-language
supply does have one** — `portabilis/i-educar` (**`722★`, `GPL-2.0`**, default branch
**`2.12`**), with `i-diario` (**`117★`, `AGPL-3.0`**, branch **`1.6`**) and `ipti/br.tag`
(**`20★`, `GPL-2.0`**) around it — 🔴 **and every one of them is copyleft.**

🟢 **Brazil also has the one artefact in this whole base that a curricular mandate can be
validated against**, and it is permissive where it counts:
**[`bncc-dev/bncc-dados`](https://github.com/bncc-dev/bncc-dados)** — the **Base Nacional
Comum Curricular as structured, verified data: `1,721` aprendizagens** (`1,580` across the
three stages of basic education plus `141` from the Computing complement, *Parecer CNE/CEB
2/2022*), in **JSON, CSV and SQLite**, each record carrying competencies, organisation
contexts and provenance, checked against the official document. **Code MIT, data CC BY 4.0**,
Zenodo **DOI `10.5281/zenodo.21625233`**, **npm and PyPI packages with the data embedded
that work with no internet**, and a **local MCP server** (`npx -y @bncc/mcp`). 🔵 Its
licence note states the legal basis — BNCC normative texts are official acts and not
copyrightable under **art. 8º, IV, Lei 9.610/98** — so the CC BY 4.0 covers the compilation
and curation, held by **Profy Tecnologias Educacionais Ltda**.

🟢 **And beside it, a measurement instrument:**
**[`bncc-dev/bncc-benchmark`](https://github.com/bncc-dev/bncc-benchmark)** — an open
**LLM-hallucination benchmark against the BNCC**, `15,300` responses from `17` models.
**Dual-licensed: MIT for `harness/` and `test/`, CC BY 4.0 for the item bank, results and
methodology**, with an explicit warning that the stored model responses were generated by
third-party models and carry their own reuse conditions. 🔵 **This is the only
jurisdiction-specific evaluation harness on these shelves**, and it is what makes
**`P-578-A`** sellable: a Brazilian deployment can be *measured* against the national
curriculum, not merely claimed to align with it.

🔵 **Carried, still the best-corroborated regional claim here:** **`92 %` of students and
`79 %` of faculty** engaging with AI (DEC + Tec de Monterrey, 2026) against an
institutional-governance gap independently measured by **UNESCO IESALC** (`200`
institutions, `19` countries, fielded Aug–Oct 2025). 🟢 **New this pass:** a **UNESCO IESALC
/ UNU-IAS working paper dated September 2026** maps AI adoption across Latin American and
Caribbean universities on teaching, research, community engagement, administration and
governance — a second mapping instrument, independent of the first.

#### 🔴 `p574` — Brazil's digital accessibility duty exists, its standard exists, and its REGULATION does not — plus a refutation of a date this pass nearly published

🔴 **The claim this block was nearly built on is false.** A 2026 Brazilian trade
article frames **`11 March`** as *"um marco na acessibilidade digital do
Brasil"*, and read quickly that looks like a compliance deadline in the current
year. 🟢 **It is a commemorative date**: `PL 2251/2025` in the Senate creates a
**Dia Nacional da Acessibilidade Digital**, and states have already enacted it
(Pernambuco, `Lei 19.201/2026`). 🔵 Recorded because the next pass will meet the
same article: **there is no `11 March 2026` Brazilian accessibility deadline.**

🟢 **What is actually true, and it is a better sales position than a deadline:**

| element | state |
| --- | --- |
| 🟢 The duty | **`LBI` (Lei 13.146/2015) art. 63** obliges digital accessibility on the sites of **companies headquartered in Brazil and government bodies alike** — in force since 2015 |
| 🟢 The technical standard | **`ABNT NBR 17225`**, published **`11 March 2025`** — a real national web-accessibility standard |
| 🔴 The regulation | **MISSING.** Art. 63 has never been regulated; making the ABNT standard a binding reference requires a **federal decree** that does not exist |
| 🔴 The enforcement | **Prosecutorial.** The **MPF** has recommended the federal government regulate art. 63 within **45 days** and is litigating to have the courts impose that duty; the public prosecutor's office is the single most active actor in Brazilian digital-accessibility cases |

🔵 **So the LATAM engagement is procurement-and-litigation-driven, not
calendar-driven** — the opposite of North America. The buyer is an institution
that wants to be defensible before a *promotoria* inquiry, and the deliverable is
the same evidence trail `P-576-A` builds, with no date to point at.

🟢 **A statutory channel North America and EMEA do not have, and it changes which
repository you may use.** **Libras** — Brazilian Sign Language — is statutory
(`Lei 10.436/2002`, `Decreto 5.296/2004`), so sign-language support is a
*procurement item* in Brazil rather than a nice-to-have. 🔴 **And this is exactly
where the layer's licensing bites:** the field's flagship,
[`sign/translate`](https://github.com/sign/translate) (`797★`), is
**CC BY-NC-SA** — noncommercial, so it cannot be in a paid deliverable. 🟢 **The
substitute is first-hand verified:**
[`sign-language-translator/sign-language-translator`](https://github.com/sign-language-translator/sign-language-translator)
— **Apache-2.0**, licence file `11,364 B`, `pyproject.toml` agrees, `380★`,
designed precisely for building a translator for a *specific* sign language.

🟡 **One regional evidence item from the mandated leg, grade `C`:** a **UNESCO
IESALC** working paper with **UNU-IAS**, dated **September 2026**, mapping AI use
across Latin American and Caribbean universities in five areas — teaching,
research, community engagement, administration and governance. 🔵 Worth a primary
read next pass; the publication itself was not fetched.

🔴 **Supply: `0` of the layer's `29` addresses are LATAM-origin.** Stated as a
gap, not left silent — and consistent with `p572`'s finding that this base's
origin instrument can only place **institutional** publishers.


#### 🟡 `p572` — the missing LATAM *classroom* rates, found where `p571` said to look, at grade `C†`

🟢 **The gap was real and the prescribed remedy worked. `p571` recorded: "query
CETIC.br and ministries, stop re-running legs" — the targeted query returned
quantified classroom adoption; the generic regional leg, run again this pass,
returned none.**

🔵 **And the reason this sat open for two passes is now clear: it was never a
missing *source*, it was a missing *population*.** This page already carried
Cetic.br at **53% of school administrators using AI, 22% reporting school
guidelines**. What was absent was the **classroom**: students and teachers.

| population | figure | instrument | grade |
| --- | --- | --- | --: |
| High-school students (internet users) using generative AI for schoolwork | **~70%** ("seven in ten") | **TIC Educação**, Cetic.br / NIC.br | 🟡 **C†** |
| Those who received school guidance on that use | **32%** | same | 🟡 **C†** |
| Teachers using generative AI in school activities | **58%** | relayed by *O Povo* **attributing** TIC Educação | 🔴 **D — do not quote** |
| School administrators (already on this page) | **53%** use / **22%** guidelines | Cetic.br | 🔵 B |

🔴 **Why `C†` and not `A`, stated plainly: `cetic.br` does not resolve from this
session.** It is outside the sandbox's permitted network domains
(`getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND`), so **the primary was not read** — these figures come
through search-result relay, with the `~70%` corroborated by the Cetic.br release
*title* itself ("Sete em cada dez alunos do Ensino Médio usam IA generativa em
pesquisas escolares, revela TIC Educação"). 🔴 **The `58%` teacher figure is
single-secondary and the search's own summary could not confirm it against
Cetic.br — it is recorded as `D` and must not be quoted to a client.**

🔴 **An unresolved conflict, registered rather than papered over (`572-D`):** this
page attributes its administrator figures to **"Cetic.br (2026)"**, while the
edition reachable this pass is **TIC Educação 2024**, with its book PDF stamped
`2025-12-17` and the sectoral study `2025-11-24`. 🔵 Either the page's `(2026)` is
a publication-year label for 2024 fieldwork, or there are two editions in play. **A
survey year and a publication year are different things and this KB is currently
asserting one as the other.** Resolving it needs the primary, which needs
`cetic.br` allowed in the session's network settings.

🟢 **What it is worth even at `C†`:** the student-to-guidance spread — **~70%
using, 32% guided** — is a **38-point** governance gap at school level that
mirrors, almost exactly, the **61-point** gap UNESCO measured in LATAM higher
education (87% use, 26% strategy). 🔵 **Two independent instruments, two different
education tiers, the same shape.** That is the single most quotable LATAM finding
in this KB, and it is now evidenced at both tiers rather than one.

**The best-evidenced adoption-governance gap in the world, from the most
authoritative source in the KB.** The **UNESCO IESALC** study (with UNU-IAS),
launched at **Digital Learning Week 2026** at UNESCO HQ in Paris, surveyed **200
higher-education institutions across 19 Latin American and Caribbean countries**:
**87% already use AI in at least one area of activity, and only 26% have a formal
AI strategy.** **55% of universities have no specific AI budget**, and public
universities have the lowest rate of dedicated AI funding at **4%**. A UNU working
paper (1 September 2026, Arianna Valentini) maps adoption across five dimensions:
teaching and learning, research, community engagement, administration, and
governance.

School-level data is national and primary. **Cetic.br (2026)**: **53% of Brazilian
school administrators use AI, while only 22% report that their school has
guidelines**. UNESCO reports **more than 50% of teachers in Chile and Brazil**
already use these tools while **fewer than 10% of institutions region-wide have
formal guidelines**. **Ceibal (Uruguay)** reports **75% of public-school teachers**
using them — the highest national figure located anywhere.

**Regulation: volume without binding force.** A 2026 Universidad de los Andes
study identified **716 AI-related regulatory instruments, rules and legislative
proposals across 23 countries and territories, more than 80% initiated since
2023**. Education-specific binding law is almost nonexistent: **Peru's Ley 31814
plus DS 115-2025-PCM** remains the only binding instrument naming education;
Chile's MINEDUC *PotencIA el aprendizaje* is guidance.

**Brazil's PL 2338/2023 is not law, and its status is now pinned.** It passed the
**Senate in December 2024** and sits in the **Câmara dos Deputados** special
committee **awaiting the rapporteur's opinion** — Deputy **Aguinaldo Ribeiro
(PP-PB)**. Roughly **37 proposals are attached** to it; the last recorded
movements (20 July – 2 September 2026) are attachments including **PL 3060/2026**,
with nothing new recorded as of early October. On **24 August 2026** the
rapporteur stated the vote would wait until **after the October 2026 elections**.
It still needs a Câmara plenary vote, and any textual change sends it back to the
Senate. *Evidentiary caveat: the official Câmara record could not be read from
this session — see Declared gaps.*

Institutional players are unusually well defined: **UNESCO IESALC** (study),
**UNESCO's Observatory on AI in Education for Latin America and the Caribbean**
(launched **14 April 2026, Santiago, Chile**), **Cetic.br** and **Ceibal**
(national data), **CENIA** (Chile) and **Tecnológico de Monterrey** (UNESCO
partnerships), and the **Digital Education Council**, which publishes an *AI in
Higher Education LATAM Survey 2026* in English and Spanish.

**Opportunities.**
1. **AI strategy and governance for universities — the single clearest
   opportunity in this KB.** 87% use it; 26% have a strategy. The gap is 61
   points, measured by UNESCO, in a region where Globant already has the deepest
   presence. It is advisory work, not platform work, and the 55%-no-budget figure
   says to price it accordingly.
2. **Brazilian public-sector school management.** `portabilis/i-educar` (GPL-2.0)
   runs municipal *Secretarias de Educação* nationwide. Copyleft, so attach via
   LTI/MCP rather than fork — and Cetic.br's 53%/22% split is the business case
   for the governance layer on top.
3. **A compliance window that is still open.** With PL 2338 stalled past the
   October elections and no education-specific instrument outside Peru, clients
   can build governance before being compelled to. That is a *preparedness* sale,
   not a *compliance* sale, and it should be positioned honestly as such.
4. **Spanish- and Portuguese-language assets.** `Chamilo` has a strong LATAM
   install base; `ltijs` has a Brazilian maintainer; **`dio-agent`** (2,750★,
   Portuguese) proves the regional demand for study-mentor agents — **but it is
   unlicensed** (see [`../agents/top.md`](../agents/top.md)), so it is evidence,
   not a component.

**Added on the fifth pass — two new sources, and the budget finding is the one
